Column notes

  • 1034D (p. 523, previous stint's page, read for our lead-in only) — Matth. VI lemma prints si autem nequam fuerit etiam corpus tuum tenebrosum erit: that is Lc 11:34's wording under a Matth. citation. Harmonized quotation, no [var:]. Ecce tu pulchra es … oculi tui columbarum (Cant. V) is Ct 1:14 (second es omitted), cited to ch. V: abridgment, no marker.
  • 1035A — Populus hic me labiis honorat (Isa. XXIX) is the Mt 15:8 form of Is 29:13, word order me labiis. Allusive quotation, no marker.
  • 1035B — Matth. XI: Migne sets et ego vos in italic and requiescere faciam in roman; Vulg. Mt 11:28 et ego reficiam vos. Plate read: the words are as our Latin. [var:] fired ("make to rest" vs "refresh"). Italic boundary kept as printed.
  • 1035B — 2 Tim 4:8 printed de caetero reposita est … quam reddet mihi in illum diem justus judex: Vulg. In reliquo … reddet mihi Dominus in illa die. Dominus omitted, in illum diem for in illa die. Sense unchanged; no [var:], logged. The plate sets justitiaequam fused (read p. 524); carried as [sic: justitiaequam] (inside the italic lemma, so no second italic), sense "of justice which" rendered beside it.
  • 1035D — plate p. 524 prints in Legem videlicet et Eva gel.um: capital Legem (our Latin legem), and Evangelium with two sorts not inked on this copy (single witness, worn/uninked type; our Evangelium taken as the reading, no marker).
  • 1036A — Joel 2:19 frumentum, vinum et oleum, et replebimini in eis: Vulg. et vinum … replebimini eis. Trivial, no marker. Ps 103:15 is paraphrase (order bread-wine-oil rearranged), no marker.
  • 1036B — Libet adhuc amplius, adreverenter attendere: plate p. 524 prints adreverenter as one word (non-word; presumably ac reverenter). [sic: adreverenter], sense "and reverently" rendered beside it.
  • 1037C — our Latin quam agit meus Deo; plate p. 525 clearly mens ("which the mind gives to God"). English follows the plate. TEI patch.
  • 1037D — Lc 7:32 Cantavimus vobis et non saltastis: Vulg. Cantavimus vobis tibiis. Abridgment, no marker.
  • 1038A — Hunc enim pulcherrimum sanctitatis ludum est mirabilis Propheta suo praeferebat praeludio: plate p. 525 confirms est (not iste). The sentence has two finite verbs and does not construe; rendered literally ("this most beautiful play of holiness is the wonderful Prophet bore before him in his own prelude"). Probably iste or a dropped relative. No marker (real word, not a negation/correlative).
  • 1038A–B — Rom 12:1 and Mt 5:16 adapted to third person (exhibeant corpora sua; opera eorum … Patrem suum): syntactic adaptation, no marker. Rom 10:10 without enim: no marker.
  • 1038B — Thren. III printed Levate … corda vestra cum manibus ad Deum; Vulg. Lam 3:41 Levemus corda nostra cum manibus ad Dominum in caelos. Person and mood change the exhortation (Wolbero glosses it as the prophet exhorting); [var:] fired. Plate read: corda in a worn italic that looks like cordu; our corda stands.
  • Scan-wide note (all pages 524–533). This archive copy prints a solid black square wherever a one-letter word stands (a, e, o): e.g. 1035A quae ■ superno, 1036B quippe ■ Domino, 1040D ■ regione, 1041D ■ charitate. It is a property of the scan, not Migne's type. Our Latin's one-letter words are therefore unverified at those sites; none is marked. Where it matters to a scripture collation it is said below.
  • 1038D — Mt 22:40 tota lex pendet et prophetae: Vulg. universa lex. Synonym, no marker.
  • 1039A — Jac. II printed offenderit autem in uno praecepto charitatis: Vulg. Jac 2:10 offendat autem in uno. The added praecepto charitatis is the point Wolbero's argument turns on (the two charity precepts as the "breasts"). [var:] fired. The citation I Cor. XII for the list of gifts (1 Cor 13) is Migne's locator, left as printed.
  • 1039B — our Latin recti diligunt. te. (stray full stop); plate p. 526 prints recti dili-|gunt te. with no stop. English follows the plate. TEI patch.
  • 1039B — second occurrence of memores uberum tuorum super vinum; recti diligunt te.: plate p. 526 prints vinnm (turned u); our Latin vinum. Rendered "wine"; no [sic:] can be fired until the patch lands, because the marker must match the Latin twin. TEI patch filed; the orchestrator may add [sic: vinnm] after patching.
  • 1039D — adversa pertulerunt et perfertur: plural perfect then singular passive present, as printed on p. 526. Rendered literally ("have endured adversities, and it is endured"); no emendation.
  • 1040C — Ejice … filio liberae (Gen. XXI): the wording is Gal 4:30 (Paul's form: cum filio liberae), not Gn 21:10 (cum filio meo Isaac). Citation as printed, no [var:] (matches a Vulgate verse).
  • 1040D — solis exteriorum persecutionem procellis: plate p. 526 prints the accusative persecutionem where the genitive persecutionum is required. Real word with no grammatical slot, and English has no exponent for the case: [sic: persecutionem] with the sense "the persecution" beside it.
  • 1040D — Gen. XVI printed Hic erit servus homo et manus omnium contra eum, a regione fratrum suorum figet tabernacula; Vulg. Gn 16:12 Hic erit ferus homo : manus ejus contra omnes, et manus omnium contra eum : et e regione universorum fratrum suorum figet tabernacula. Plate confirms servus ("a slave man" for "a wild man"). [var:] fired on servus. Note that Wolbero's own gloss at 1041A still reads quamdam feritatem ("a certain wildness"), and calls Ismael servus there too: both readings are in play in his exposition. manus ejus contra omnes and universorum omitted: abridgment. a regione vs Vulg. e regione: the plate's one-letter word is blacked out on this scan (see scan-wide note), so no marker.
  • 1041A — Isa. V printed lucem tenebras, et tenebras lucem: Vulg. Is 5:20 ponentes tenebras lucem, et lucem tenebras (order of the pairs reversed). Fragment, sense symmetrical; no marker.
  • 1041B — ego quasi unam pellem compacta operosum mei regis … ; ergo tentorium: plate p. 527 confirms every word. The sentence does not construe (compacta feminine nominative; operosum left without its noun until ergo tentorium after the Colossians note). Rendered literally, not repaired. Probably compacta [sum in] operosum … tentorium, with ergo intrusive.
  • 1041C–D — Mal 4:2 reordered (Vobis autem timentibus … orietur sol justitiae), Rom 8:35 recast from question to statement with list reordered: adaptations, no marker.
  • 1041D — Prov. XVI printed Sunt enim viae rectae quae videntur hominibus pravae: the Vulgate (Pr 16:25; also 14:12) says the opposite, Est via quae videtur homini recta, "a way that seems right". Wolbero's sicut econtrario, pravae quae videntur rectae supplies the Vulgate sense as his second member. [var:] fired.
  • 1040A — persecutio plane, quae infertur filiis ancillae: the dative says the persecution is inflicted on the sons of the bondwoman, while the whole passage (and 1040B filios … liberae persequuntur filii ancillae) has them as its authors. Plate p. 526 confirms filiis ancillae. Rendered literally ("inflicted on the sons of the bondwoman"). Probably a filiis ancillae intended; no marker (real words; the lapse is the author's or Migne's, not a negation/correlative).
  • 1042B — 2 Cor 7:10 recast (tristitia haec secundum Deum est, et poenitentiam … operatur): adaptation, no marker. Prov 14:10 in gaudio illius for ejus: trivial.
  • 1042C — plate p. 527 prints Salo-|manis (line break after Salo-), a non-word for Salomonis; our Latin agrees. [sic: Salomanis], sense "Solomon" beside it.
  • 1042D–1043A — 1 Cor 4:9 huic mundo (Vulg. mundo), 2 Cor 4:10 in corporibus nostris (Vulg. in corpore nostro), 2 Cor 6:16 in eis (Vulg. in illis): trivial, no marker. oleum in vasis (Matth. XXV) is a two-word allusion; the English keeps one italic span ("oil in vessels of our own") to hold the printed italic boundary.
  • 1043B — praemissit (for praemisit), plate p. 528 as our Latin: a medieval double-s perfect spelling, Pattern 9, no marker.
  • 1043C — Act 5:17–18 and 5:40 abridged and merged; no marker.
  • 1043D — Act. VIII printed dispersi sunt omnes per regiones Samariae praeter apostolos: Vulg. Act 8:1 per regiones Judaeae et Samariae. Judea dropped; [var:] fired (the gloss that follows turns on the Samaritan precept of Mt 10:5).
  • 1043D — Mt 10:6 ad perditas oves Israel (Vulg. ad oves quae perierunt domus Israel): same sense, no marker.
  • 1044A — Act. XIII printed vobis quidem oportebat loqui verbum Dei: Vulg. Act 13:46 Vobis oportebat primum. The priority ("first") is lost, which bears on the gloss (the preaching to "my own nation" before the gentiles). [var:] fired.
  • 1044A — plate p. 528 prints judicati sæternæ vitæ (for judicatis aeternae), as our Latin. Word-division/letter-shift defect: [sic: judicati saeternae] carried as the whole run (inside the italic lemma, no second italic), sense "judge yourselves unworthy of eternal life" beside it.
  • 1044A — Rom 11:11 salus gentibus (Vulg. salus est gentibus): trivial. Rom 11:25–26: contingit (Vulg. contigit), plate confirms contingit: present for perfect, rendered "befalls"; logged only. Plate prints fierit (Vulg. fieret), as our Latin: non-word, [sic: fierit], sense "should be saved" beside it. mysterium without hoc: trivial.
  • 1044D — our Latin au in spiritu mansuetudinis; plate p. 528 prints an. English "or" follows the plate. TEI patch. 1 Cor 4:21 omits in caritate, et; 1 Cor 5:1 quis for aliquis, patris without sui: abridgment, no marker. (Cited I Cor. IV for both; locator as printed.)
  • 1045A — 2 Cor 7:9 secundum Deum (Vulg. ad Deum); Gal 3:1 word order: no marker.
  • 1045C — Hbr 12:8 omnes filii (adds filii); 1 Jo 2:19 Quia de nobis exierunt (Vulg. Ex nobis prodierunt): same sense, no marker. Jr 10:20 agrees.
  • 1045D — ad unius Matris grem um pertinere: plate p. 529 sets grem um with a gap and no visible i, as our Latin. Split run carried whole, [sic: grem um], sense "bosom" beside it. (The gap may be an uninked i; this is the only witness on disk.)
  • 1046B — omnia quae voluit potuit (Psal. CXIII): paraphrase of Ps 113:11 omnia quaecumque voluit fecit (Wolbero's potuit makes it a statement of power); no marker, it is his gloss not a quotation (no italic).
  • 1046C — our Latin ends the lemma vineam meam non custodit. ("she has not kept"); plate p. 529 prints custodiv. in italic with the final sort broken or uninked (clearly v, not t). Read as custodivi, English "I have not kept". TEI patch proposed (our custodit → plate custodivi, final letter damaged on this copy; a second witness would settle the last letter). No marker.
  • 1046D — Jerem. IX printed Ascendit mors per fenestras vestras, ingressa est domus vestras: Vulg. Jr 9:21 per fenestras nostras ; ingressa est domos nostras. Person changed ("your" for "our"); [var:] fired. domus as acc. plural is a sound fourth-declension form, no marker.
  • 1046D — ⚑ the lemma at the end of the adverse-powers exposition prints Filii matris meae … posuerunt me custodem in vineis, vineam meam custodivi. with no non. Plate p. 529 confirms: vineam meam custodivi. The dropped negation is carried (7a): English "my own vineyard I have kept", which contradicts the gloss just before it (cogunt eam in animae suae custodia minime stare) and Ct 1:5 as quoted six times elsewhere in this range. [var:] fired with the Vulgate reading.
  • 1047B — quas de singulis tribubus accipit (present among pluperfects), per alias vero (fem. for alios, 1047D): as printed; gender has no English exponent (Pattern 9), tense rendered as printed.
  • 1047C — Unde cum David rex nomine praemonente cogitaret: plate p. 530 confirms nomine. "the name forewarning" does not make much sense; probably Nathane (Nathan) intended. Rendered literally, no marker.
  • 1047C — tanto et reverentia praemio dignam judicavit: plate confirms. reverentia has no construction between tanto and praemio; rendered "worthy of a reward so great, and of reverence". Probably tanta reverentia et praemio. Crux only.
  • 1047C — I Reg. VII printed Praedicens … praedico tibi quod domum tibi aedificabit Dominus: Vulg. 2 Rg 7:11 praedicitque tibi Dominus quod domum faciat tibi Dominus (aedificaturus is 1 Par 17:10's verb). A free paraphrase in the first person; no [var:]. Locator I Reg. (= 2 Sam) as printed.
  • 1047D — sua gratia adivit: plate confirms adivit ("approached"); probably adjuvit ("helped"). Real word, rendered "he approached it with his grace". Crux only.
  • 1048A — Dan 10:1 et verum verbum (Vulg. et verbum verum), Balthasar (Vulg. Baltassar): trivial. Et ego intelligebam is not verbatim in Dn 8 (cf. Dn 8:15, 12:8): allusion, no marker.
  • 1048B — quando alicu voluntatem suam dat intelligere: plate p. 530 prints alicu (non-word, for alicui), as our Latin. [sic: alicu], sense "someone" beside it.
  • 1048B — ut Joseph promitteretur non consulto pro hoc Deo: plate confirms promitteretur. Obscure ("that Joseph should be promised"); perhaps permitteretur or ut ad Joseph mitteretur. Rendered literally. Gen. XXXI is Migne's locator for Gn 46:1–3; the quotation is a free paraphrase (cum omni domo sua for cum omnibus quae habebat, vocantem se dropped, ne timeas after Descende); no [var:].
  • 1048C — se ei hoc per intellectum judicasse and Simul vero et re et intellectu et voce judicare videtur: judicare where the whole scheme runs on indicare. Plate confirms both. Rendered "judged" / "to judge" as printed. Crux only.
  • 1048C — Isa. VI printed Vae mihi quia tacu: plate p. 530 tacu (non-word for tacui), as our Latin. [sic: tacu] (inside the italic lemma), sense "I have held my peace" beside it. vir pollutis labiis (Vulg. pollutus labiis): same English sense, no marker.
  • 1048D — Is 6:7 printed et tetigit labia mea dicens, ecce tetigi hoc labia tua: Vulg. et tetigit os meum, et dixit : Ecce tetigit hoc labia tua. Mouth → lips, and tetigi ("I have touched") for tetigit ("this hath touched"): the coal's agency passes to the seraph. [var:] fired. Is 6:9 Audite audientes me adds me: logged only.
  • 1049B — Jo 10:27 printed et ego Dominus agnosco eas: Dominus is roman on the plate (Wolbero's insertion), agnosco for cognosco: no marker. Quinam sunt quas pascit Deus, nisi hi quos: gender slip, Pattern 9.
  • 1049B — Judicio sunt haec scire cupienti: plate confirms Judicio; probably Indicio ("these are an indication"), the same j/i confusion as 1048C. Rendered "for a judgment". Crux only.
  • 1049C — sub vestimentis ovium lupi rapaces (Vulg. Mt 7:15 in vestimentis ovium … lupi rapaces): allusion, no marker. Non novi vos, discedite a me, maledicti, in ignem aeternum (Matth. XXV) conflates Mt 25:12 (nescio vos) and 25:41: conflation, no marker. si ut ipse talibus dicit: as printed ("if, as he himself says"), probably sicut; crux only.
  • 1049C — I Cor. II lemma conflates Rom 8:5 (qui … secundum spiritum sunt, quae … spiritus sunt sentiunt) and 1 Cor 2:14 (animalis without homo): conflation, no marker. Plate p. 531 sets nonpercipit tight but with a hair space; not a fusion.
  • 1050A — magis et minus accidere potest Porro: no stop before Porro on plate p. 531 or in our Latin; carried without a stop (Pattern 8). Likewise montium sublimia sunt (1050B) ends its paragraph without a stop, on the plate and in the English.
  • 1050B — ad unum Dei regnum, 37 pertinere noscuntur: the comma stands before the Cologne page number; the English keeps a comma there.
  • 1050B — Ezech. XXXIV conflates Ez 34:11, 12, 13, 14 (erunt pascua eorum for earum): conflation and gender, no marker.
  • 1050C — ut in eis suae indignationis gratia cubet et requiescat: plate p. 531 confirms indignationis. Rendered literally, "the grace of his indignation" says the opposite of the passage (God resting with delight in the devout, set against his labouring in the reprobate). A real word whose faithful English asserts what the author argues against: [cj: indignationis; read dignationis, "condescension"] (Pattern 18; the work uses dignatio for God's condescension at 1039C and 1049A).
  • 1050C–D — Isa. LXVI printed Super quem requiescam nisi super humilem et quietum et trementem sermones meos?: Vulg. Is 66:2 ad quem autem respiciam, nisi ad pauperculum, et contritum spiritu, et trementem sermones meos? The printed form (the familiar patristic/Old Latin text) is what the gloss rests on: God's requies. [var:] fired.
  • 1050D — Mal 2:17 quod dixistis (Vulg. dicitis), et illi responderent narrative frame: no marker. Sap 1:5 agrees.
  • 1050D–1051A — Divinitas ergo quae … repellitur: Spiritus … (Sap. I), cujus repulsio illorum est abjectio, ea nimirum in sanctorum mentibus … cubat et requiescat: anacoluthon, cubat indicative beside requiescat subjunctive, as printed. Rendered literally ("lies and may rest").
  • 1051A — our Latin requiescat, cujus susceptio; plate p. 532 prints requiescat. cujus (full stop, lower-case c). English carries the stop and the lower case (Pattern 8). TEI patch.
  • 1051C — nisi quod caeterae duae tantum ultima quippe, ut diximus, sine tempore est, ad ejus constituuntur formam: plate confirms; the parenthetic ultima … est is unmarked and the sentence runs on. Rendered as closely as the words allow, with no brackets supplied.
  • 1052A — the Rom 2:14–16 paraphrase is located (Num. II) on plate p. 532 and in our Latin: Migne's locator (for Rom. II), kept verbatim. No italics, paraphrase; no marker.
  • 1052B — sive pro conjuranda lege sancta: plate confirms conjuranda. Rendered "binding themselves by oath to the holy law". Perhaps conservanda; crux only.
  • 1052C — tantus erat fervor … tantus zelus … ut … non dubitaret: singular verb with zelus as subject; rendered "it did not hesitate". Hebr 11:36–37 adapted (Sancti for Alii vero, secti sunt, tentati sunt omitted): abridgment, no marker.
  • 1053A — qui autem in tenebris ambulat, nescit quo eat: plate p. 533 sets eat in roman, as our Latin (Vulg. Jo 12:35 vadat); Wolbero's own word, no marker.

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)

  • 1035D · in legem videlicet → in Legem videlicet (capital L) · p. 524
  • 1037C · quam agit meus Deo → quam agit mens Deo · p. 525
  • 1039B · recti diligunt. te. → recti diligunt te. (stray full stop; plate breaks dili-|gunt te.) · p. 526
  • 1039B · memores uberum tuorum super vinum; recti diligunt te. (second of the two subdistinction lemmata) → super vinnm; (turned u on the plate; after patching, the English may take [sic: vinnm]) · p. 526
  • 1044D · au in spiritu mansuetudinis → an in spiritu mansuetudinis · p. 528
  • 1046C · vineam meam non [1046C] custodit. → custodivi. (plate custodiv., final sort broken or uninked; the v is certain, a t is excluded) · p. 529
  • 1051A · cubat et requiescat, cujus susceptio → cubat et requiescat. cujus susceptio · p. 532

Columns 1053B–1070D. Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 533–541, each opened once at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (533 = 1053/1054 … 541 = 1069/1070). Second witness patrologiaecur195mign.pdf opened at pp. 534 and 536 for two letter-level checks. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0015.json.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 533–541 (feet read full width on every page; p. 539's foot carries only the signature PATROL. CXCV. and 34). No [cn:] inserted; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0015.tsv is header-only. Nothing unplaced, no sequence to check.

Lemma count: 96 italic spans + 2 guillemet spans = 98, paragraph-bounded (no paragraph with an odd delimiter count), agreeing with 11512-lemmata-0015.txt (98).

Markers fired (all on plate-read columns):

  • [var:] ×3: 1054C (Is 31:9 Vivit for dixit), 1059A (Ct 6:8 Una et … genitricis suae), 1062C–D (Hab 3:8 equitatus tuus sanitas est).
  • [sic:] ×18: 1055B simultane, 1055D sed alium, 1057A dici, dicitur, 1059B approba um, 1059C pulchritudinem, 1059D santissimae, 1060C Israelitci, 1061A equitus, 1061B antiquitatus, 1061D sacra dandarum, 1062C Eccelsia, 1062D inedicibilis, 1063D Aethiopia, 1064A corde, 1064C ego, 1065C pugnativum, 1065D proportior.
  • [cj:] ×1: 1055D ut (read ne).
  • [ed:] ×1: 1067–1068, the diagram (FIGURA) the digitization omits.

Scan note

As on T03's pages, this archive copy blacks out many one-letter words and some two-letter ones (a, e, o, and at 1063B and 1064D an et): e.g. 1059D velut triticum ■ paleis, 1069A inferior ■ regione (the first e regione on the same page prints clean). None bears on a scripture collation except 1063B, which was settled on scan B (below).

Column notes

  • 1053B — Ps 38:4 printed exardescit ignis (present); Vulg. exardescet (future). Rendered "blazes out". Tense only, and Wolbero's gloss runs in the present (tota … ardet); logged, no [var:].
  • 1053C — nec quidquam praecedentes partes conferre poterunt. Si haec ultima defuerit, qui enim perseveraverit …: plate p. 533 sets the full stop after poterunt, so the conditional hangs on the quotation. English carries the stop (Pattern 8). Mt 24:13 enim for autem: trivial.
  • 1053C — In caudam quippe finis est corporis: accusative after in with est, as printed. Rendered "in the tail"; case has no English exponent here, no marker.
  • 1054C — Isa. XXXVI printed Vivit, inquit, Dominus, cujus ignis est in Sion …: Vulg. Is 31:9 dixit Dominus : cujus ignis …. The oath formula "The Lord liveth" replaces "saith the Lord". [var:] fired. Locator Isa. XXXVI (for XXXI) as printed.
  • 1054D — Jo 14:2 printed In domo … Patris mansiones multae sunt: Vulg. Patris mei. Abridgment, no marker. The printed italic is split (In domo enim Patris …); the English keeps one span round the verse.
  • 1055B — qui non simultane sed veraciter illum diligant: plate p. 534 prints simultane (our Latin agrees). No such adverb is attested in the sense the antithesis needs (probably simulate, "feignedly"). [sic: simultane], sense "feignedly" beside it. (If simultane is ever shown to be an attested medieval spelling, Pattern 9 would withdraw this marker.)
  • 1055B — Jo 14:23 printed et Pater meus diligit eum (present); Vulg. diliget. Rendered "loves". Tense only; logged.
  • 1055B — lemma Ne vagari incipiam: the g of vagari is worn on p. 534 (reads like vaaari); our vagari stands, no marker.
  • 1055C — Lc 2:46 abridged (sedentem in medio doctorum omitted, eum for illum, audientem without illos): abridgment, no marker.
  • 1055C–D — Subjecti autem inter se veluti fratres sint ficta charitate junguntur: veluti … sint construes as "as though they were brothers"; rendered so.
  • 1055D — possit agnoscere: plate p. 534 sets po sit with a gap (uninked s); our possit taken, no marker.
  • 1055D — ⚑ … a quibus recte vivendi formam assumere, 43 ut sit ei necesse vagabundam circuire et ignoranter fortasse abire in errores sed alium simulatorum: plate p. 534 confirms ut. The purpose is the one the lemma states (Ne vagari incipiam): she asks for discretion so that she need not wander. Printed ut makes the English assert the reverse. A real word whose faithful English asserts what the author argues against, and the missing force is a negation: [cj: ut; read ne, "lest"] (Pattern 18, negation class).
  • 1055D — same clause: sed alium (plate p. 534, our Latin agrees), a broken run with no construction; almost certainly sodalium, the word of the lemma just expounded (post greges sodalium tuorum). Carried whole, [sic: sed alium], sense "companions" beside it.
  • 1056A — the first lemma prints Si ignoras te, o pulcherrima, inter mulieres? with a comma after pulcherrima and a question mark; the VERS. 7 lemma has neither. Both carried as printed.
  • 1056C — divina se deferente gratia: plate confirms deferente. Rendered literally, "divine grace betaking itself away" (se deferre); deserente ("forsaking") may be meant. Crux only.
  • 1056D — quod tantum ejus naturam Deus diluerit: plate p. 534 confirms diluerit ("washed"), where the sense wants dilexerit ("loved"). A real word; rendered "so washed his nature". Crux only (no negation or correlative at stake).
  • 1057A — sicque ex hoc sui cognitione quasi et cujusdam dici luce dicitur ad desiderandam … justitiam: plate p. 535 confirms dici (for diei) and dicitur (for ducitur, the counterpart of trahitur in the next sentence). Neither has a grammatical slot. [sic: dici] "day", [sic: dicitur] "is led". hoc … cognitione: gender, Pattern 9.
  • 1057B — quae conquesta fuerat, quae non tantum ab extraneis …: second quae where quod is expected; rendered "who had complained, who suffered…", as printed.
  • 1057C — 1 Cor 14:38 / 2 Cor 6:14 conflated and recast (omnis ignorans ignorabitur; nulla participatio luci ad tenebras for Vulg. quae societas luci ad tenebras?): adaptation of two verses into a statement, no marker.
  • 1057C — nutri et fove notos tuos carnales et luxuriosos: plate confirms notos ("acquaintances"); the parallel at 1058D (ubi carnalis nutriat motus) suggests motus ("motions"). Real word, rendered "acquaintances". Crux only.
  • 1057D — 1 Jo 2:15 recast (quia omnis qui diligit mundum non est charitas Patris in illo): adaptation, no marker. Mt 6:24 and Eph 4:5 agree.
  • 1058B — omnia enim quae fecit Deus erant valde bona (Gen. II): paraphrase of Gn 1:31 (Viditque Deus cuncta quae fecerat, et erant valde bona), located Gen. II. No marker.
  • 1058C — quoddam immediatum ostendens: the dialecticians' "immediate" contraries (no middle term); rendered "showing a certain thing with no mean between".
  • 1058C — our Latin privatoria actione injustitiae enim malum; plate p. 535 prints actione. injustitiae (full stop, lower-case i). English carries the stop and the lower case. TEI patch.
  • 1059A — Cant. VI printed Una et columba mea, perfecta mea, una est matris suae electa genitricis suae: Clementine Ct 6:8 Una est columba mea, perfecta mea, una est matris suae, electa genetrici suae. Plate p. 536 confirms Una et and genitricis suae. [var:] fired on the whole (the verse is Wolbero's proof of the one Church, and the printed form differs in two words).
  • 1059A–B — Mt 17:4 printed faciamus hic tria tabernacula: Clementine si vis, faciamus tria tabernacula. si vis omitted, hic added. Logged only (Wolbero's point, that Peter "was not heard", does not turn on si vis).
  • 1059B — neque approba um ejus tale consilium: plate p. 536 sets approba um with a gap and no visible t, as our Latin. Split run carried whole, [sic: approba um], sense "approved" beside it (same class as T03's grem um; the gap may be an uninked sort).
  • 1059B — Nihil enim est aliud Evangelium quam lex et prophetae futurum rebus et verbis denuntiant, jam factum Evangelium repraesentat: does not construe (a quod seems lost after quam). Rendered as closely as the words allow.
  • 1059B — Ez 1:16 conflated (Aspectus … rotarum et opera velut si sit rota in medio rotae): no marker.
  • 1059C — Unum namque sunt et judicem se continent Vetus et Novum Testamentum: plate confirms judicem; invicem ("contain each other") is the obvious sense. Rendered literally, "hold themselves a judge". Crux only.
  • 1059C — quae tanta mysteriorum et sacramentorum pulchritudinem decoratur: plate p. 536 confirms the accusative pulchritudinem after tanta (ablative). "Adorned with so great a beauty" would silently repair the case (Pattern 12, test 2): [sic: pulchritudinem], sense "beauty" beside it.
  • 1059D — debet ea gratanter et decenter attendere, et studio bonae actionis attendere: attendere twice, as printed; not an identical doubled run, so no [d:]. Rendered "attend … and attend".
  • 1059D — o santissimae sorores: plate p. 536 san-|tissimae (no c), as our Latin. [sic: santissimae], sense "most holy". Note the address: Wolbero writes here to a community of sisters (vos, plural throughout; English "you").
  • 1060A — Ps 83:2–3 Quam dilecta tua tabernacula: word order only, no marker.
  • 1060B — VERS. 9 for Ct 1:8 (Clementine numbering): Migne's label, carried as printed. The next VERS. head (1069A, Ct 1:9) is also VERS. 9.
  • 1060B — the lemma repeated at the end of the paragraph prints its assimilavi with a worn v that reads like assimilari on both copies (A p. 536, B p. 534). The glyph lacks the tall stroke of the clean v in the VERS. line but is not an italic r. Read as v; our assimilavi stands, no marker.
  • 1060C — exercitus Israelitci populi: plate p. 536, as our Latin. [sic: Israelitci], sense "Israelite".
  • 1061A — Simul etiam incipere libet quomodo … praecessit … et virga illa praetulerit: incipere (probably inspicere). Rendered "it is pleasing also to begin how". Crux only.
  • 1061A — Quid ergo est equi-|tus iste?: plate p. 537 equitus (for equitatus), broken at the line end. [sic: equitus], sense "company of horsemen" beside it.
  • 1061B — Ille ergo antiquus Domini antiquitatus: plate p. 537 antiquitatus (for equitatus, the eye caught by antiquus). [sic: antiquitatus], sense "company" beside it.
  • 1061D — omnium sacra dandarum rerum utilitatibus: plate p. 537 sets sacra dandarum with a space, as our Latin (= sacrandarum). Split run, [sic: sacra dandarum], sense "to be consecrated".
  • 1062B — congregatio fidelium qua ab Abel justo … tendit: qua for quae, as printed; renders "which" either way. Logged only. Hbr 13:14 adapted to the third person: no marker.
  • 1062C — quae praesens Eccelsia accipitur: plate p. 537 Eccelsia. [sic: Eccelsia], sense "Church". sed ipse et certitudine futurae discussionis secernitur: ipse masculine, as printed; rendered "it itself".
  • 1062C — Ps 67:18 located Psal. XLVII (Migne's locator), millia laetantium in eis (Clementine millia laetantium ; Dominus in eis): abridgment, no marker.
  • 1062C–D — Habac. III printed qui ascendis super equos tuos; et equitatus tuus sanitas est: Clementine Hab 3:8 Qui ascendes super equos tuos, et quadrigae tuae salvatio. The whole exposition turns on equitatus (the Lord's "company of horsemen"). [var:] fired.
  • 1062D — inedicibilis boni praemio: plate p. 537 ine-|dicibilis, a non-word (for indicibilis or ineffabilis). [sic: inedicibilis], sense "unspeakable".
  • 1063A — ad finem venientes: plate confirms finem; ad fidem ("coming to the faith") is the likely sense. Real word, rendered "coming to the end". Crux only. Is 24:16 agrees.
  • 1063B — Apoc. VI printed data est ei potestas super quatuor partes terrae occidere et interficere gladio, et fame, et morte, et bestiis terrae: Clementine data est illi potestas … interficere gladio, fame, et morte, et bestiis terrae. occidere et added: no marker. Scan A blacks the word before bestiis; scan B p. 536 reads et, as our Latin.
  • 1063B — Equitatui enim ejus recte inferus comparatur: inferus (for infernus of the verse just quoted), a real word; rendered "hell". primi et notiores fuerunt praesentis ducis sequaces: in passione quidem illius ducis supra in albo equo venientis. Herodes et Pilatus …: stop before Herodes on the plate, carried; praesentis rendered "present" as printed.
  • 1063C — Nam de hoc nomine extollente se supra omne quod dicitur Deus: plate confirms nomine (for homine, "this man"). Real word; rendered "this name exalting itself". Crux only. 2 Thes 2:4 agrees.
  • 1063C — Is 18:1–2 agrees with the Clementine word for word.
  • 1063D — second trans flumina Aethiopia: plate p. 538 Aethiopia (nominative/ablative, for the genitive Aethiopiae printed twice just above). "Beyond the rivers of Ethiopia" would hide the case: [sic: Aethiopia] (inside the italic lemma, so no second italic), sense "of Ethiopia" beside it.
  • 1064A — Vasa ergo papyri sunt corde saecularium doctorum: plate p. 538 corde (abl. sing., for corda). No grammatical slot: [sic: corde], sense "hearts".
  • 1064A — Ez 32:22 printed Ibi Assur et omnis multitudo ejus, et in circuitu ejus sepulcra illius: Clementine in circuitu illius sepulchra ejus. Pronouns exchanged, et added: no marker. The note (Ezech. XXXII) is followed by Quid with no stop, on the plate and in our Latin; the English carries no stop.
  • 1064A — Job 41:25 rex super omnes filios superbiae? (Clementine universos, no question): synonym; the question mark is Migne's and is carried.
  • 1064B — Ez 32:27 Qui descendunt (Clementine descenderunt): tense, no marker. Rom 6:13 Non exhibeatis (Clementine Sed neque exhibeatis): no marker.
  • 1064C — Cum armis ego suis ad inferna descendere est: plate p. 538 ego (for ergo). No grammatical slot: [sic: ego], sense "then" beside it.
  • 1064C — Hujus ergo Pharaonici equitatus oppositione Dominicus: ille equitatus agnoscitur: plate sets the colon after Dominicus; the sense is "the Lord's company is known by opposition to Pharaoh's". Carried with the colon. quia si nunquam bonus fuit quem malorum pravitas non exercuit: the si has no apodosis of its own; rendered literally.
  • 1064D — ut sponsa … convenienter praedicentur: no stop at the end of the paragraph, on the plate and in our Latin; the English carries none. Apoc 21:2 without novam: abridgment.
  • 1064D–1065A — sic ipsa ex Aegypto hujus mundi liberanda, post … observationem. Triumphato 53 demum …: the plate's full stop leaves the sic clause without a verb. Carried.
  • 1065A — Non autem abs re … considerare: no finite verb (est understood); rendered as a verbless clause. secundum similitudinem quam poscimus: poscimus ("demand") as printed; perhaps ponimus. Crux only.
  • 1065B — Mt 19:29 abridged (vel fratres, aut sorores … aut uxorem omitted): abridgment, no marker. The guillemeted Jerome quotation is carried in « » exactly as printed.
  • 1065C — ut sit melioris partis perfectioni et alterius imperfectioni nulla prorsus sit comparatio: sit twice, as printed.
  • 1065C — Illa enim antiqui Dei circumcisio: rendered literally, "that circumcision of the ancient God" (probably antiqui populi Dei). Crux only.
  • 1065C — sive carnaliter pugnativum fuga et victoria: plate p. 539 pugnativum (adjective form where the genitive plural pugnantium is required). No grammatical slot: [sic: pugnativum], sense "of those fighting".
  • 1065D — eum qui est secundum spiritum: the second member (secundum carnem) seems lost; rendered as printed.
  • 1065D — tantumque proportior est veritas figura: plate p. 539 proportior, a non-word (a comparative coined on proportio, or a slip for praestantior). [sic: proportior], sense "greater" beside it.
  • 1065D — in ista hoc est in veritate pugnabatur: imperfect as printed ("there was fighting").
  • 1066A — quae est praemium fortissimum: rendered "the strongest reward"; perhaps fortissimorum. Crux only.
  • 1066B — Job 7:1 printed Militia hominis vita ejus (Clementine Militia est vita hominis super terram): paraphrase, no marker. secus quem: rendered "according to which".
  • 1066C — the first statement of the definition: our Latin pro justitia et veritate aeterna adipiscenda vita; plate p. 539 prints veritate et aeterna (as in the three restatements that follow). Without et, "eternal truth"; with it, "truth and eternal life". English follows the plate. TEI patch.
  • 1066D — Junguntur ergo inseparabiliter dilectio Dei et equitatus. Domini cum constet: stop after equitatus on the plate and in our Latin, splitting equitatus Domini. Carried, lower case after the stop (Pattern 8).
  • 1067A — sic ipsius equitatus et affectio et causa divina: divina where diversa is expected (the next clause lists the two different causes). Rendered "the cause divine". Crux only.
  • 1067–1068 — ⚑ the diagram. Below invitat. (1068A) the plate prints 56 FIGURA. and a full-width figure filling the rest of the page: a square frame lettered IVNGVNTVR INSEPARABILITER down both sides; an upper arch CONTRARIA over VITA ÆTERNA and a lower arch CONTRARIA under VITA HOMINIS; top row DILECTIO · CAVSA · PVGNÆ · INVIDIA; bottom row EQVITATVS DOMINI · PVG[NA] · EQVITATVS DIABOLI; left CONSENTIT PER GRATIAM, EX DEO, SPIRITVS; right CONSENTIT PER CVLPAM, EX DIABOLO, CARO; centre PROPORTIO over CENTVPLA, crossed by diagonals lettered REPVGNANT P[ER] INCONSEQVENS (one whole, the other split REPVGNANT P / INCONSEQVENS). Our Latin keeps only \[1067] 56\ FIGURA. [1067]. The English carries that line as it stands and adds an [ed:] (Pattern 13a, clause 2: a reader-visible unit the digitization lost, recoverable from the plate) giving the labels in English. The Latin labels are recorded here for the orchestrator; whether they should enter the Latin as a TEI patch is a markup question left open (not filed below).
  • 1068A — Sap 2:24 enim … intravit (Clementine autem … introivit): synonyms, no marker. Apc 22:17 agrees.
  • 1069A — sic ea quadrata formula disponat: jussive with no expressed subject; rendered "let one arrange them".
  • 1069B — the guillemeted Gregory sentence is carried in « » as printed.
  • 1069B — si adhuc constiterit eum per munditiam … non pulchre auxisse: auxisse ("increased"), perhaps vixisse. Rendered literally. Crux only.
  • 1069D — duo subjecta suscipiunt quidem se, sed non quae aliter unum praedicatum: obscure as printed; rendered word for word.
  • 1070C — animi devotatio: rendered "devotion". devotatio is taken as a late-Latin form (not marked); if it proves unattested it would be a [sic:] candidate.
  • 1070D — chunk ends at the Cologne page number 59; on the plate 59 is followed by a leader-dot run (59 ….. Axa quippe), which our chunk 0020 opens with.

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)

  • 1058C · privatoria actione injustitiae enim malum → privatoria actione. injustitiae enim malum (full stop, lower-case i) · p. 535
  • 1066C · Deum est diligere pro justitia et veritate aeterna adipiscenda vita toto animo → pro justitia et veritate et aeterna adipiscenda vita toto animo · p. 539

Columns 1070D–1089A. Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 542–550 each opened once at 300 dpi, printed corner columns read first (542 = 1071/1072 … 550 = 1087/1088), text collated line by line and the foot read full width in the same visit; p. 541 opened for the 1070D lead-in only; p. 551 (shared with the next stint) read for 1089A. Two sites re-read at 500–600 dpi (1071D se posuit, 1088D Ezechiele/seriem). Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0020.json.

Migne's foot notes

None on any page 541–551. Every foot was read full width; each page ends in running text. No [cn:] inserted in the Latin; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0020.tsv is header-only. No numbering sequence to check, nothing unplaced, no asterisk- or letter-keyed note.

Standing decisions (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • proportior (1071B, 1071C, 1074D; also 0018 in T04's range) = greater in proportion.
  • Murenulas aureas faciemus tibi, vermiculatas argento (Ct 1:10) = We will make thee golden murenulae, vermiculated with silver. murenula stays italic Latin (Song rule case 2: the gloss derives it from murena, rendered "the moray"); vermiculatas = "vermiculated", because the gloss explains it in modum vermicularum.
  • monile / monilia (Ct 1:9) = necklace(s); Ct 1:9 = Thy cheeks are beautiful as the turtle-dove's, thy neck as necklaces. T04 (0015–0019) may have met Ct 1:9 first: reconcile at merge.
  • accubitus (Ct 1:11) = reclining, not Douay's "repose", because the gloss runs accubitus / accumbere / se inclinare / reclinatorium: While the king was at his reclining, my nard gave forth its odour.
  • Ct 1:12 follows T01: A bundle of myrrh is my beloved to me, he shall abide between my breasts. Ct 1:13 = A cluster of Cyprus is my beloved to me, in the vineyards of Engaddi (the gloss interprets Cyprus as tristitia, so the island name stays). Ct 1:14 follows T03: Behold, thou art fair, my love, behold, thou art fair, thine eyes are doves'. Ct 1:6 = Lest I begin to wander after the flocks of thy companions (sodales = companions, set against amicus = friend, 1086A).
  • pulchritudo genarum / colli = beauty of the cheeks / of the neck; praedicatio · incorporatio = preaching · incorporation; compunctio = compunction; intelligibilis · intellectibilis = intelligible · intellectible (Boethian pair); nardus pisticus = pistic nard; inedicibilis = unutterable (T01's rendering).
  • The chunk head is TEI's ## . / ## . (cont.); carried as printed in our Latin. Plate p. 541 prints the Cologne page 59 followed by a run of five leader dots before Axa quippe (1070D); no heading stands there on the plate.
  • Pattern 17: the sisters (1071D–1072B) are you; the Bridegroom to the bride (1085B ff.) is thou; Horace's animum rege … tu compesce is thou.

Markers fired (all on plate-read columns)

  • [var:] ×7: 1072A (2 Cor 6:16 / Lev 26:12), 1074C (1 Cor 5:5), 1076C (Ez 1:21), 1078C (Rom 9:28), 1079C (Prov 15:30), 1084D (Apoc 20:6), 1087D (Prov 27:3).
  • [sic:] ×8: 1071D se posuit, 1074A nemoriatur, 1075D ornament, 1075D sive, 1077B sponsio, 1083B basalmo, 1085A hotrum, 1087C genere.
  • [cj:] ×2: 1079A esse non potest (double negative), 1082B voluit (dropped negation).
  • [d:] ×1: 1079C et sponsus et sponsus.

The scripture divergence list (every span checked against sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt by chapter and verse)

[var:] fired:

  • 1072A Lev. XXVI; II Cor. VI printed Inhabitabo in eis et inambulabo; et ipsi erunt mihi in populum et ego vero eis in Deum. Vulg. 2 Cor 6:16 inhabitabo in illis, et inambulabo inter eos, et ero illorum Deus, et ipsi erunt mihi populus. Besides the conflation, the printed clause has no verb: ego vero ("I truly") where every form of the text has ero ("I will be"). Rendered "and I truly to them for God". Plate p. 542 reads vero.
  • 1074C 1 Cor 5:5 printed Satanae in interitum carnis, ut 63 salvus sit in die Domini: spiritus is absent (plate p. 543, with the Cologne number between ut and salvus), so the one saved is the man, not his spirit; Wolbero reads it that way (he is later re-incorporated).
  • 1076C Ez 1 printed ibant pariter et rotae sequentes ea, et cum flantibus flabant et cum elevatis pariter elevabantur: a conflation of Ez 1:19–21 whose middle clause reads flantibus flabant ("with those that blew they blew") where 1:21 has cum stantibus stabant. Plate p. 544 is clear. Wolbero's gloss is on stant sive moventur, so the printed verb is foreign to its own exposition.
  • 1078C Rom. IX printed verbum abbreviatum et consummatum Dominus faciet super terram; Vulg. Rom 9:28 verbum enim consummans, et abbrevians in aequitate: quia verbum breviatum faciet Dominus super terram. The gloss rests on the passive pair consummatum … abbreviatum.
  • 1079C quidam sapiens: Fama, inquit, bona impugnat ossa (roman on the plate, unlocated): Prov 15:30 fama bona impinguat ossa. Plate p. 546 reads impugnat ("attacks"); the gloss that follows (nisi hujusmodi compositionis pinguedine repleatur) needs impinguat ("makes fat").
  • 1084D Apoc. XX printed Beatus est sanctus qui habet partem; Vulg. Apoc 20:6 Beatus, et sanctus, qui habet partem. Plate p. 548 reads est.
  • 1087D Prov. XXVII printed sed ira viri utroque gravior; Vulg. Prov 27:3 sed ira stulti utroque gravior (also Grave saxum et ponderosa arena for Grave est saxum, et onerosa arena). The fool's anger has become every man's.

Divergences NOT marked (abridgment, adaptation, allusion, synonym; one line each):

  • 1071C Apoc 14:3–4 (canticum novum dicere et Agnum quocunque ierit sequi): allusion.
  • 1071D Ps 41:2–3 (cited Psal. LXI, Migne's locator): Sicut cervus desiderat for Quemadmodum desiderat cervus, ad Deum vivum without fortem.
  • 1072B Lc 9:58 recast as indirect speech (quod non habeat ubi caput reclinet).
  • 1073B 1 Cor 12:8 abridged (Quia alii datur … alii sermo scientiae). 1073C–D Ps 118:46, 48 agree. 1074A Ps 131:9 agrees; Ex 28:35 alluded to.
  • 1074C Act 3:19 without igitur. 1074D 1 Cor 12:8–10 (located II Cor. XII) omits alii operatio virtutum.
  • 1075D Mt 22:40 in duobus praeceptis for in his duobus mandatis. 1076A–1077C Ct 1:4, 1:8, 1:9, 1:10, 1:11 agree.
  • 1078B Unguentum nardi spicati pretiosi located Joan. XII: it is Mk 14:3's wording (Jo 12:3 nardi pistici pretiosi); a harmonized quotation, and the gloss on spicas uses it. 1078B 1 Cor 12:31, 1078C Ps 62:6, 1078D Ps 118:96 agree. 1078C Rom 13:10 with quia for ergo, preceded by Wolbero's own clause.
  • 1078D Mt 22:37–40 (located Matth. XXI) abridged, tota lex for universa lex, praeceptis for mandatis. 1078D, 1087D 1 Jo 4:8/16 agree. 1079A 1 Cor 13:4 agrees.
  • 1079C 2 Cor 2:15 with in omni loco from 2:14: conflation. 1079D Jo 12:3 end agrees. 1080A Jo 1:1, 1:14 agree.
  • 1081A the catalogue of perils and torments (2 Cor 11:26–27; Heb 11:36–37) is roman on the plate: paraphrase.
  • 1082B Mt 27:25 agrees. 1083A 2 Cor 1:3 agrees. 1083D Rom 6:5 adapted (mortis Christi for mortis ejus, fiant for erimus).
  • 1084B Gal 5:16 and 1084C Rom 8:13 agree. 1084C Si commorimur et convivemus (roman; 2 Tim 2:11 si commortui sumus): tense only.
  • 1085D Jac 2:17 (roman) without est; Mt 25 lamps alluded to. 1085D Jo 15:15 abridged (sed amicos for Vos autem dixi amicos).
  • 1086A Ct 1:6 agrees. 1086D Eph 4:31 omits et indignatio, ab ea for a vobis. 1087A Rom 12:18 as an infinitive. 1087A malum in bonum vincat (roman): allusion to Rom 12:21.
  • 1087B 1 Cor 10:4 recast (Christus enim petra est); 2 Cor 7:10 recast; Mt 5:5 agrees; Lc 6:25 quia plorabitis for quia lugebitis et flebitis (synonym).
  • 1087D Rom 5:5 recast; Is 11:2–3 list agrees; Jac 1:20 without enim; Prov 12:16 Stultus for Fatuus; Prov 27:4 agrees.
  • 1088A Horace, Epist. I.2.59–61 and 62–63: not scripture, not collated; Migne lineates them and so does the English.
  • 1088A Spiritus quoque intellectus est located Isa. IX: Wolbero's own sentence under a locator. 1088B ut quod sibi non vult fieri alteri non faciat located Matth. VII: the negative rule (Tob 4:16 form), paraphrase. 1088B Ps 10:6 agrees. 1088D Ps 23:8 (located Psal. XXV) agrees in the phrase quoted. 1089A 1 Cor 9:22 paraphrased (roman).
  • Migne's wrong locators (Psal. LXI, II Cor. XII, Matth. XXI, Jos. VIII for Gideon) are kept verbatim.

Column notes (plate and TEI agree unless stated)

  • 1070D (p. 541) The lead-in 59 ….. Axa quippe: plate prints five leader dots, TEI four spaced dots; carried as our Latin has them.
  • 1071A aut cum bono supernae patriae contemplatur, quae quia necdum adipiscitur: Gregory's Moralia has bona (plural, agreeing with quae). Rendered literally, "contemplates by the good … which".
  • 1071B quidquam sublunari globo, beatiore animo atque puriore substantia valet: Boethius has quidquid sub lunari globo; as printed the clause has a second finite verb and does not construe. Rendered literally.
  • 1071D familiaribus, ut credimus: an adjective with no noun; rendered "as familiar things".
  • 1071D quas beata solitudo … se posuit: plate p. 542, read at 500 dpi, sets se posuit as two words with a full space; quas has no verb unless it is seposuit. [sic: se posuit], sense "has set apart" beside it.
  • 1072B in duo dividitur, hoc in praedicationem: as printed ("this into preaching"), probably hoc est in.
  • 1072C per collum ergo praedicati sancti: rendered "the proclaimed saints"; perhaps praedicatores.
  • 1073A incorporatis vero alia per conversionem: incorporatis where incorporatio is wanted; rendered "of the incorporated".
  • 1073C quem serenda capacis ingenii comprehendunt: rendered literally "which they comprehend, to be sown, of a capacious wit"; perhaps serena.
  • 1073C et divina habent gratia, ut: ablative, rendered "have it also by divine grace, that".
  • 1074A tintinnabula inserta habeat nemoriatur: fused on plate p. 543. [sic: nemoriatur], sense "lest he die" (Ex 28:35 et non moriatur).
  • 1074B quasi immeritus repellerentur: singular adjective, plural verb; rendered "as though an undeserving man, they were being repelled", so both numbers show.
  • 1074D–1075A non quod in hoc aliarum gratiarum, quorumlibet habeat, qui unam habuerit: as printed it says the holder of one grace has none of the others; the argument (quilibet sanctus … pluribus ornetur virtutibus) wants the reverse. It's rendered literally. Not a [cj:]: no negation or correlative was lost from the printed words, and the sense is obscure rather than false.
  • 1075D hujus ornament decorem: plate p. 544 prints ornament followed by a space (no final i), as the TEI. [sic: ornament], sense "ornament".
  • 1075D nempe sive charitate nulla virtus … esse … potest: plate sive for sine. sive has no slot, and read as printed the clause would say virtue exists "or by charity". [sic: sive], sense "without" (7a⁗: the preposition's force kept in plain words).
  • 1077A eamdem formositatem perfecti non agnovit: rendered "the same beauty of the perfect"; probably perfecte, which the next sentence prints (perfecte non agnoverit).
  • 1077A fratres sui contra eum pugnaverint: masculine eum for the bride; English has a gendered pronoun, so it's rendered "against him".
  • 1077B ardentius sponsio inhaereat: sponsio is nominative and inhaereat needs a dative (the subject is ipsa). [sic: sponsio], sense "to the Bridegroom".
  • 1077C–D Et est locus ab efficiente causa probetur: no construction (a quo or ut seems lost); rendered literally.
  • 1077D collatae in se gratia bonum: gratia for gratiae; rendered "the good of the grace conferred".
  • 1078B Charitas enim gratia est, quia omnibus praeponderat virtutibus: the seven qualities of the nard root (radice gravi, crassa, brevi, nigra et fragili, folio parvo densoque … aristas) are expounded in order, so this first one should be gravis ("heavy … it outweighs"). The plate prints gratia in italic, as the TEI. Crux only.
  • 1078D tribus constans syllabis et septem litteris: charitas as printed has eight letters (caritas seven). As printed.
  • 1078D–1079A nihil autem boni sine Deo esse non potest: the double negative states that every good thing can exist without God, the opposite of the conclusion drawn from it. [cj: esse non potest; read esse potest] (the 8195 @1250B class).
  • 1079A ut omnem proximi necessitatem suam putet, folio parvo densoque est: comma, no stop, on the plate; kept.
  • 1079C ille rex et sponsus et sponsus coelestis: the plate prints the doubling. The runs are identical, so [d:] goes on the second.
  • 1079D quia pedes Domini ungere, fuit divinitatis ejus celsitudinem … considerare: pedes where the next sentence (caput … divinitatem, pedes … humanitatem) wants caput. Rendered literally.
  • 1080A our Latin odoris hujus sua vitatem; plate p. 546 prints suavitatem as one word. English "sweetness". TEI patch.
  • 1080D succumbere] parata: plate p. 546 has a tall stray sort after succumbere, like a closing bracket and not a comma; TEI has nothing. Not carried.
  • 1081B quia pro ea ejus nomine: as printed ("for it").
  • 1081C mea commorabitur,, quia: the plate shows two commas (italic and roman?); the English has one.
  • 1081D et ore suam perpetuam sumunt: suam perpetuam has no noun (salutem?); rendered "take their own perpetual".
  • 1082B Et quia hujus medelam suscipere voluit, botri dulcedine caruit: the Jewish people, which "abhors and despises this cluster" (1082D), is said to have willed the healing and so gone without the sweetness. The sense needs noluit. [cj: voluit; read noluit, "was unwilling"] (dropped negation, the 7561 @0648 class).
  • 1082B de terra lacte et melle manente: plate manen'e, with a worn t and the vowel e; the formula is manante ("flowing"). Rendered "abiding". Crux only.
  • 1082C our Latin sicut mos est protantium; plate p. 547 reads portantium. English "those who carry". TEI patch.
  • 1083A Engaldi beside Engaddi: name kept as printed.
  • 1083B Ex basalmo autem misto: plate p. 548 as the TEI. [sic: basalmo], sense "balsam".
  • 1083B et sacerdotalis suae imperialis dignitas confirmatur: rendered literally, "the priestly, its own imperial, dignity"; perhaps sive.
  • 1083C quia in illos profecto sit mihi cibus: accusative for ablative; rendered "in those".
  • 1084A sive impositionem manu[s]: plate p. 548 shows manu then a blurred sort; TEI manus. Undecided, so no patch; English "the hand".
  • 1084C conresurgant ei vivendo. justitiae cum eodem Apostolo: full stop and lower-case justitiae on the plate; kept (Pattern 8).
  • 1084D–1085A ut nardo … redolente et myrrha intimae mortificationis hostiam ibi vivam jam existentes atque per hotrum spiritualiter debriantem … dignos indicaverit adventu: hostiam (sg.) beside existentes (pl.) and no expressed object for dignos indicaverit. It doesn't construe; rendered as closely as the words allow. hotrum: plate p. 548 as the TEI, so [sic: hotrum], sense "cluster". debriantem is the medieval debriare and is rendered "inebriating" with no mark.
  • 1085B Cologne 75 is 75 in the TEI (internal space); carried verbatim.
  • 1085B meumque adjutorium quod tamen tibi non dixerat: third person inside the Bridegroom's own speech (defuerat?); rendered literally.
  • 1085C si unum et duobus dupliciter: plate et (two sorts, not the blacked-out e); rendered literally.
  • 1086A sed rerum probat qua id intentione fecerint: no subject for probat (exitus?); rendered "he proves of things".
  • 1087A ut extraneos … quam domesticos … diligat: tam absent; rendered "as her own household".
  • 1087B hoc est pro saeculari loco et laetitia: probably joco ("jest"); rendered "worldly place".
  • 1087B Super aquas sedere consuerit: syncopated form beside consuevit (1086D); rendered normally.
  • 1087C vel pro dilatione coelestis regni genere: the seven dove-marks are re-expounded as infinitives (non irasci, … sperare, [gemere], … intendere) and the moan-for-song mark needs gemere. The plate prints genere, which has no slot. [sic: genere], sense "to groan".
  • 1088C quemdam sive qualitati competentem conversandi inveniat statum: rendered literally; perhaps suae qualitati.
  • 1088D our Latin sanctarum seriem. Scripturarum; plate p. 550 (600 dpi) shows no stop after seriem (a speck sits before it). The English has no stop. TEI patch.
  • 1088D our Latin in Ezechicle; plate p. 550 at 600 dpi reads Ezechiele (the seventh sort has a crossbar). This is probable, not certain. English "Ezekiel" either way. TEI patch (probable).
  • Scan-wide: this copy blacks out one-letter words (a, e, o), e.g. 1085A resurgentis ■ morte, 1085C medium ■ duobus. No one-letter word in this range bears on a scripture collation (the only italic one, 1085D Jo 15:15 audivi a Patre, is legible and agrees), so scan B wasn't consulted.

  • 1082C · sicut mos est protantium → sicut mos est portantium · p. 547
