Column notes

  • 1089B — duo extremi referuntur ad spiritum timoris Domini: "the two last" (initial and filial), not "the two extremes". As printed.
  • 1089C — our Latin Mundamus est cessare a bono; plate p. 551 clearly Mundanus (paired with servilis in the sentence before). English "Worldly fear" follows the plate. TEI patch.
  • 1089C — ubi timor et spiritus se invicem comitantur, sicut legitur, timor est sine spe: plate confirms spiritus and sine spe. Rendered literally ("fear and the spirit accompany one another, as it is read, fear is without hope"). The sense wants something like timor et spes … timor non est sine spe; not repaired, no marker (the words construe; the logic is the difficulty).
  • 1090A — utile, honestum judicans: asyndeton as printed, carried.
  • 1090A — Tu, inquit, o dilecte: the vocative o is blacked out on scan A; scan B p. 549 prints it, italic, as our Latin. The speaker is the bride, so inquit = "she says" here (the Bridegroom speaks at 1093C, o dilecta, "he says").
  • 1090C — dicendum quomodo et caput et ad capitis membra appetetur: plate confirms appetetur (future passive of appeto, "will be sought"). Rendered literally. Probably aptetur ("is applied"): the same idea is expressed by aptatur at 1091B. Real word, no marker.
  • 1090D — quia et ad caput et ad pietatis membra diximus referendum: plate confirms pietatis where the argument (and 1090C twice) has capitis. Rendered "the members of piety". Crux only.
  • 1091B — tanto majorem habet requiem in divinitatis contemplatione quasi in uno lectulo sunt. Deus per gratiam …: the full stop and the plural sunt are on the plate. Carried; the following verbless series (Deus … inspirans … homo … perficiens) is rendered verbless.
  • 1091C — participabilis et participans hoc est caput membrum: no et between caput and membrum on the plate; English "head, member" supplies none.
  • 1091D — inhabitantis proportionis gratiae tuae donationes: plate confirms proportionis. Rendered "the gifts of the proportion of thy grace dwelling in the mind". Crux only.
  • 1092A — VERS. 16 prints Tigna domorum nostrarum cedrina laquearia nostra cypressina. with no mark between cedrina and laquearia (plate p. 552 and our Latin). The English carries none (Pattern 8), so it reads "are of cedar our panelled ceilings of cypress". The re-quotation below (1092A) has the comma.
  • 1093A — our Latin ut manu artificis runc natum decorum praestet aspectum; plate p. 553 prints runc'natum: between runc and natum stands the upper stroke of a broken i sort, at the width of one letter, not a word space. Scan B p. 551 shows the identical fragment (same printing). The word is runcinatum, "planed" (runcina, a plane). English "planed" follows the plate. TEI patch (our space for Migne's damaged i). No [sic:]: the letter is on the plate.
  • 1093B — quorum omnia vita, eruditione, institutione, auctoritate … confirmatur: plate confirms omnia. Taken as an accusative of respect ("in all things") and so rendered; no marker.
  • 1093C — hunc gloriosum … statum, ad illius 84 constitere nutum … fatetur: plate confirms constitere (a perfect 3rd plural in form, where the construction after fatetur wants an infinitive, constitisse). Rendered "that this glorious state … has stood at the nod". Crux only.
  • 1094A — Alter quoque, o dilecta, ego sum flos campi, ille nimirum de quo Isaias dicit: plate confirms Alter. It construes as "another (flower) too … that one of whom Isaiah says" and is rendered so ("Another too, O beloved, I am the flower of the field, that one, namely, of whom Isaiah says"). Aliter is possible; no marker.
  • 1094A — multi alii procedunt flores, id est gratia spiritualis quos: singular gratia spiritualis between plural flores and quos, as printed. Rendered "that is, spiritual grace, which".
  • 1094B — quia non scabrosa, neque tortuosa, vicus, sed plana et aequalis: plate p. 553 confirms vicus ("a village") between commas. It does not construe; rendered literally ("because it is not rough, nor tortuous, a village, but level and even"). Not repaired; perhaps a corruption of situs or of a third adjective.
  • 1094C — nam gloriantes … se continentes profectum non habent lilium candoris virginei, et interioris munditia signum: plate confirms both. Rendered literally: "have not progress, the lily of virginal whiteness, and, by inward purity, the sign". Probably profecto non habent lilium, candoris virginei et interioris munditiae signum ("they certainly have not the lily, the sign of virginal whiteness and inward purity"). Crux only.
  • 1094D — ita tu, o Ecclesia mea, sponsa mea, sive quaevis me diligens anima esse debetis …, ut cum me hoc modo fueris imitata, vere mea proberis amica: the plural debetis after the singular tu, then singular fueris … proberis. Pattern 17 renders the number as printed: "so thou … you ought to be … when thou hast imitated me … thou mayest be proved".
  • 1094D / Ct 2:2 — amica mea = "my love", following the peer rendering of Ct 1:14 at 1087 (0023) and 1034D (0010), one voice within the work. As at 0023, Wolbero's gloss turns amica into amicitia (verae amicitiae probatio, 1095A Dei amicitia); the English keeps "friendship" for amicitia and "a friend to me" for the predicative mihi amica (1095A), which is not the lemma.
  • 1095A — plate p. 554 sets liliuminter as one word, with normal spacing either side, in the italic sicut liliuminter spinas; the same phrase is set correctly one line below. Our Latin agrees. [sic: liliuminter] (inside the italic lemma, so no second italic), sense "lily among" rendered beside it.
  • 1095B — ut dicit B. Gregorius, quisquis malos non tolerat, ipse tibi per intolerantiam testis est: plate confirms tibi. Gregory (Hom. in Ezech. I.9) has ipse sibi … testis est, "is witness to himself". Rendered "to thee" as printed; crux only (not a negation or correlative).
  • 1095B — Frater sum draconum: sum is roman on the plate (Wolbero's insertion; Vulg. Job 30:29 Frater fui). No marker.
  • 1095C–D — quia quae spinis comparantur, constat 87 quod in adversam partem recipiantur: the Cologne number sits after constat; English "it is evident 87 that".
  • 1096A — tria in se habens utilia quibus reficit, hoc esse fructum, odorem et umbram: hoc esse for hoc est, as printed. Rendered "this being fruit, odour and shade". The period sicut malus … incomparabilis habetur, ita dilectus meus … inter filios … exstitit is kept as one sentence.
  • 1096C — gratia vero talem aestum umbra misericordiae et remedio poenitentiae movit: plate confirms movit. Rendered "moved". Probably removit or amovit ("removed"); crux only.
  • 1096C–D — Haec nimirum arbor lignum est vitae, cujus umbra et protectio divina quam fundant rami illius, rami honoris et gratiae, qui videlicet sunt … oppositiones: the relative cujus umbra never receives a verb (anacoluthon), as printed and in the English.
  • 1097A — plate p. 555 prints perfecta corporis et ani mae meae refectio, anima split with a full word space. Scan B p. 553 is identical. Our Latin agrees. Split run carried whole, [sic: ani mae], sense "soul" rendered beside it.
  • 1097B — Sub cujus umbra seu Ecclesia, quia … illius rami per totam mundi latitudinem diffusis, mirabiliter protegitur: plate confirms seu ("or") and the case clash rami … diffusis (nominative noun, ablative participle). Rendered literally ("Under whose shade or the Church, because, the branches of that tree … being spread, she is wonderfully protected"). seu is probably sedet ("sits"), which Ct 2:3 sedi and 1096C–D would support; no marker (the sentence is broken, not false).
  • 1097D — et in toto virtutum gratiarum divino munere comparant species: asyndeton virtutum gratiarum as printed ("the species of the virtues, of the graces").
  • 1098A — sicut vinum … suo naturali colore omnem noxium excoquit humorem: plate confirms colore ("colour"), where excoquit ("boils out") wants calore ("heat"). Rendered "by its natural colour". Crux only.
  • 1098B — Haec est ergo cella vinaria perfectio, et angustam viam libere intrantibus, suaviter sapiens et ob hoc perfectibilis divini praecepti intelligentiae de charitatis excellentia. Plate confirms every word. It does not construe (intelligentiae genitive or dative with no governor; sapiens … perfectibilis with no noun). Rendered as closely as the words allow, not repaired.
  • 1099C — ut primo toto corde, deinde tota anima, postremo tota virtute Deus diligatur credendo, tota anima confitendo, tota virtute operando: toto corde is not repeated before credendo. The English does not supply it.
  • 1099D — a Domino servus … a servo Dominus: Dominus capitalized on the plate; here the earthly master, rendered "master".
  • 1100C — second quotation of Ct 2:5, plate p. 556 Fulcite me loribus: the first sort of the word is a damaged, hooked glyph, not an f (compare floribus at 1100A, clean). Scan B p. 554 shows the same damaged sort (same printing). Our Latin reads loribus. [sic: loribus] (inside the italic lemma), sense "flowers" rendered beside it. It may be a broken fl ligature; a wrong sort cannot be excluded.
  • 1100D — Cupio disso'vi: plate prints dissolvi with the l broken (fragment visible); our Latin dissolvi. No marker.
  • 1100D — ⚑ Quorum profectum videre pro magna interim reputat consolatione, quando Dei faciem quam tantopere desiderat videre, perfecte praevalet comprehendere. Plate p. 556 confirms: no non. The literal English says that Paul "is perfectly able to comprehend the face of God", which is what the whole passage denies: 1100B videre sicuti est non praevalet, and the dilatio of the reward (1100D, 1101A). Carried literally, with [cj: praevalet; read non praevalet, "is not able"] (Pattern 18: the English asserts what the author argues against; the dropped word is a negation, the 7561 @0648 form of the gloss).
  • 1101A — Unde quibusdam suis discipulis consolatoria mittit, verba dicens: the comma after mittit is on the plate. Rendered "he sends consolatory things …, saying words", keeping the mark.
  • 1101C — ⚑ non profectu et utilitate proximorum consolatur, verum etiam re praesentium bonorum et spe futurorum sublevatur: plate p. 557 confirms non … verum etiam with no solum. The literal English says the bride is not consoled by her neighbours' progress, which contradicts the whole exposition of VERS. 5 just ended (magna certe consolatio … quando videt proximos proficere, three lines above). Carried literally with [cj: non; read non solum, "not only"]: a correlative whose answering member (verum etiam) is printed and whose own member is not (Pattern 18, the 7561 @0648 and 11535 @1231B class).
  • 1101D — Laeva Christi, id est sinistra: a gloss of one Latin word for "left hand" by another; English has no pair. sinistra stands in italic Latin ("The left hand of Christ, that is, the sinistra"), per the Song rule's case 2 (a gloss depends on the word).
  • 1102A — Haec est enim dextera ejus quae eum amplexetur, … quae eum adepta fuerit, … requiescere possit: plate confirms eum twice (masculine, where the bride/soul wants eam). English "him" as printed; the construction of the second quae is loose and is rendered "which, when she shall have obtained him, she may be able to rest".
  • 1102B–C — nec oculus videre, nec auris audire, nec in cor hominis possit ascendere (I Cor. II): 1 Cor 2:9 adapted into an infinitive clause; only nec oculus, nec auris, nec in cor hominis are italic on the plate, and the English keeps those three spans.
  • 1102D — the end of Book I (Amen). The LIBER SECUNDUS head that closes chunk 0029 stands at the top of p. 558 (cols 1103/1104), above 95 PROOEMIUM; confirmed there.

Scripture: collation of every ⚠ span (7a″)

Every ⚠ span in 11512-lemmata-0025.txt, walked against sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt by chapter and verse, the plate read at each.

  • 1089B Perfecta charitas foras mittit timorem (I Joan. IV) — 1 Jo 4:18 without the leading sed; charitas spelling. Agrees.
  • 1089C–D Nolite timere eos qui corpus occidunt, animae vero non habent quod faciant (Matth. XX) — conflation: Mt 10:28 opening with Lc 12:4's close (non habent amplius quid faciant). Sed potius timete eum, qui potest et corpus et animam perdere in gehennam (ibid.) — Mt 10:28 with corpus and animam in reverse order. Conflation and order, no marker. Locator Matth. XX as printed (Mt 10).
  • 1090A o dilecte, pulcher es et decorus, 1090C ecce tu pulcher es et decorus — Ct 1:15 re-quoted in pieces (dilecte mi dropped, es moved). Fragments of the verse just quoted, no marker. VERS. 15 itself agrees.
  • 1090B non ad mensuram dat Deus spiritum (Joan. III) — Jo 3:34 without enim. Agrees.
  • 1090D–1091A apud quem non est ulla commutatio nec vicissitudinis obumbratio (Jacob. I) — Jac 1:17 non est transmutatio: ulla added, commutatio for transmutatio; same sense ("change"). No marker.
  • 1091A Super quem requiescam, nisi super humilem et quietum et trementem sermones meos? (Isa. LXVI) — Vulg. Is 66:2 ad quem autem respiciam, nisi ad pauperculum, et contritum spiritu, et trementem sermones meos? The printed (Old Latin/patristic) form is what the gloss rests on (requies, qui in humilitate et quiete … servat). [var:] fired, worded as the peer's at 1050C–D, where the same text is quoted.
  • 1092A Tigna domorum nostrarum cedrina, laquearia cypressina — Ct 1:16 re-quoted without the second nostra. Abridgment, no marker. VERS. 16 agrees (punctuation aside: see column note).
  • 1093D sum lilium convallium, 1094A ego sum flos campi — Ct 2:1 with sum supplied. No marker. VERS. 1 agrees.
  • 1094A–B spiritus Domini … spiritus timoris Domini (ibid.) — Is 11:2–3, ⚠ only because the span crosses the verse boundary. Agrees.
  • 1094C (roman, not a span) quos exspectavi ut facerent uvam, fecerunt autem spinas (Isa. V) — allusion to Is 5:4 (faceret uvas, et fecit labruscas), turned to "thorns" for the lemma. Not italic; no marker.
  • 1095A sicut liliuminter spinas — Ct 2:2 with the plate's fused liliuminter: [sic:] (see column note), not a divergence.
  • 1095B Filii hominis increduli et subversores sunt tecum, cum scorpionibus habitas (Ezech. II) — Vulg. Ez 2:6 Tu ergo, fili hominis … quoniam increduli et subversores sunt tecum, et cum scorpionibus habitas. Migne's Filii hominis (plate p. 554 confirms) turns the vocative "O son of man" into the subject, "the sons of man are unbelievers and subverters with thee". Who the unbelievers are changes. [var:] fired.
  • 1095B Et justum Loth … animam justi iniquis operibus cruciabant (II Petr. II) — 2 Ptr 2:7–8: animam justi for animam justam ("the soul of the just man" / "the just soul"), Loth for Lot. Same sense, no marker.
  • 1095B Scio ubi habitas … et habes nomen meum, et non negasti fidem meam (Apoc. II) — Apc 2:13 et tenes nomen meum. "Hast" for "holdest"; no marker.
  • 1095C In medio nationis pravae et perversae inter quos lucetis quasi luminaria in mundo. Verbum vitae continentes (Philip. II) — Phlp 2:15–16 sicut luminaria. Synonym, no marker.
  • 1095C Quanto plus Pharao populum Dei premebat, tanto plus multiplicabatur (Exod. I) — paraphrase of Ex 1:12 (Quantoque opprimebant eos, tanto magis multiplicabantur), Pharaoh named. Paraphrase, no marker.
  • 1095D sicut malus arbor, 1096A ita dilectus meus — Ct 2:3 re-quoted inside the gloss (arbor added, ita for sic). Gloss wording, no marker. VERS. 3 agrees.
  • 1096B Sanguis ejus super nos et super filios nostros (Matth. XVII) — Mt 27:25, agrees; locator Matth. XVII is Migne's, left as printed. Crucifige, crucifige (Joan. XIX.) agrees.
  • 1096C Si quis haec vel illa fecerit, morte moriatur (Exod. XIX) — a generic formula (morte moriatur, Ex 21:12 and passim), not a verse of Ex 19. Paraphrase; locator as printed; no marker.
  • 1097A velut malus inter ligna silvarum — Ct 2:3 with velut for sicut. Gloss wording, no marker.
  • 1097B Speciosus forma prae filiis hominum, diffusa est gratia in labiis tuis; propterea unxit te Deus, Deus tuus oleo exsultationis prae participibus tuis (Psal. XLIV) — conflation of Ps 44:3 (propterea benedixit te Deus in aeternum) with Ps 44:8 (propterea unxit te Deus, Deus tuus, oleo laetitiae prae consortibus tuis); exsultationis and participibus are the Hebrews 1:9 / Old Latin wording. Conflation, no marker.
  • 1097B Sub cujus umbra, cujus fructus dulcis est gutturi — gloss re-quotation of Ct 2:3. No marker.
  • 1097C VERS. 4 Introduxit me rex in cellam vinariam, ordinavit in me charitatem. — Vulg. Ct 2:4 Introduxit me in cellam vinariam: Migne adds rex (plate p. 555 confirms), from Ct 1:3's Introduxit me rex in cellaria sua. The exposition builds on it (1099D a rege sponsoque suo in cellam introducitur vinariam). [var:] fired once, at the verse lemma. The re-quotation at 1098C Introduxit me rex in cellam vinariam, … et ordinavit in me charitatem repeats rex (and adds et); same divergence, not marked twice.
  • 1097D laetificat cor hominis (Psal. CIII) — Ps 103:15 laetificet. Mood of a three-word fragment, no marker.
  • 1098D Qui non diligit fratrem suum quem vidit, Deum quem non videt, quomodo potest diligere? (I Joan. IV.) — 1 Jo 4:20 Deum, quem non vidit: present for perfect. Rendered "whom he seeth not" as printed; no marker.
  • 1099B Deus charitas est (I Joan. IV) — 1 Jo 4:8/16; ⚠ only for the spelling charitas/caritas. Agrees.
  • 1100A VERS. 5 Fulcite me floribus, stipate me malis, quia amore langueo. — the comparand prints Ct 2:5 as Fulcite in me floribus. Migne's Fulcite me floribus is the reading of the printed Clementine as usually cited, so the extra in may be a defect of clementine-flat.txt rather than a divergence of Migne's; either way Migne's words are the standard ones and nothing is fired. (Flagged for the comparand's defect list; not verified against another Clementine copy.)
  • 1100C Fulcite me loribus, stipate me malis — [sic: loribus] (see column note), not a divergence.
  • 1100D Cupio dissolvi et esse cum Christo … manere autem in carne necessarium propter vos? (Philipp. I.) — Phlp 1:23–24 desiderium habens dissolvi … permanere autem in carne. Paraphrase of the opening (Cupio), manere for permanere; same sense, no marker.
  • 1100D–1101A Scimus quoniam si terrestris domus … aeternam in coelis (II Cor. V) — 2 Cor 5:1 without enim. Agrees.
  • 1101A Gratias ago Deo meo … exspectantibus revelationem Domini nostri Jesu Christi (I Cor. I) — 1 Cor 1:4–7: quia for quod, nihil desit vobis for nihil vobis desit. No marker.
  • 1101C sine fide impossibile est quemquam Deo placere (Hebr. XI) — Hbr 11:6 Sine fide autem impossibile est placere Deo: quemquam added. Same sense, no marker. The following fides autem si non habeat opera, mortua est in semetipsa (Jac. II) is roman on the plate, Jac 2:17 verbatim after Sic et.
  • 1101C VERS. 6 Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera ejus amplexabitur me. — Ct 2:6 dextera illius. Pronoun, no marker. (The re-quotations at 1102A–B print illius.)
  • 1102B sinistra sub capite — gloss substitution of sinistra for laeva (Wolbero's own id est sinistra, 1101D). No marker.
  • 1102B videmus nunc quidem per speculum in aenigmate, tunc autem facie ad faciem (I Cor. XIII) — 1 Cor 13:12 with quidem added. No marker.
  • 1102D Longitudo quippe dierum in dextera ejus, in sinistra ejus divitiae et gloria (Prov. III) — Pr 3:16: quippe added, ejus for illius, no et before in sinistra. No marker. Delectationes in dextera ejus sunt usque in finem (Psal. XV) — Ps 15:11 in dextera tua, adapted to the third person (ejus sunt roman on the plate). No marker.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Following the peers: sponsus/sponsa = Bridegroom/bride · dilectus/dilecta = beloved · charitas = charity · dilectio = love · amica mea = my love · thou/you by number (Pattern 17).
  • Introduced here: lectulus = bed · floridus = flowery · tigna = beams · laquearia = panelled ceilings · malus = apple tree, mala = apples · cella vinaria = wine-cellar · fulcite … stipate = stay me up … compass me about · languere = languish · laeva = left hand (with sinistra kept in italic where it glosses laeva) · timor initialis / filialis / servilis / mundanus = initial / filial / servile / worldly fear.
  • Verse heads: I have used VERSE n.--, the majority form in the finished English (0011, 0013, 0015, 0016, 0021, 0022); 0006 and 0008 use VERS. n.--. One form should be chosen at merge.
  • The Cologne page numbers 80 … 94 are carried verbatim at their places.

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)

  • 1089C · Mundamus est cessare a bono → Mundanus est cessare a bono · p. 551
  • 1093A · ut manu artificis runc natum decorum → ut manu artificis runcinatum decorum (plate runc'natum: the i sort broken, its upper stroke visible at one letter's width; identical on scan B p. 551) · p. 553

Columns 1103A–1120B (Book II: the PROOEMIUM and CAPUT II from Ct 2:7 to 2:16). Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 558–566, each opened once at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (558 = 1103/1104 … 566 = 1119/1120; on p. 566 only 1119A–1120B, to fecit dilectione., belongs to this range). Second witness patrologiaecur195mign.pdf (PDF page = floor((col+9)/2)) opened at pp. 557, 558, 560, 562, 564 for one-letter and letter-level checks. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0030.json.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 558–566 (feet read full width on every page). No [cn:] inserted; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0030.tsv is header-only. Nothing unplaced, no sequence to check.

Lemma count: 195 italic spans, paragraph-bounded (no paragraph with an odd delimiter count), 0 guillemet spans; agrees with 11512-lemmata-0030.txt (195). Per chunk: 20 · 39 · 44 · 45 · 47.

Markers fired (all on plate-read columns):

  • [var:] ×1: 1113B, Ct 2:13 printed Surge, propera, amica mea (Vulg. without propera).
  • [sic:] ×11: 1105C praepudiente, 1106D sponsus, 1108C sol, 1110A–B per motus, 1111A formo sa, 1111C–D assumunt, 1112D se, 1113A solidaria, 1113B iu, 1118B purvulas, 1118D conturbernium.
  • [cj:] ×2: 1109C obedientiae (read inobedientiae), 1109C quoque (read quo).

Vocabulary (one voice with 0000–0019): sponsus "the Bridegroom"; dilectus / dilecta "the beloved" (both, as in the peers; the one place the argument turns on the gender, 1120A, keeps the Latin forms in italics); amica mea "my love"; speciosa mea "my fair one", formosa mea "my beautiful one"; filiae Jerusalem "daughters of Jerusalem"; VERS. n. → VERSE n. (orchestrator ruling, 2026-09-25); the sisters addressed in the plural (vos) = "you"; the Bridegroom speaking to the bride in the singular = "thou". canticum is "canticle" throughout the proem so that canticum Domini and Canticum canticorum stay one word. PROOEMIUM → PROEM (corpus precedent). SEQUITUR CAPUT II. → CHAPTER II FOLLOWS.

Scan note

As on T03/T04's pages, scan A blacks out one-letter and some two-letter words (a, e, et, ad, te). Where they bore on a quotation they were read on scan B: 1107B Mt 6:33 et haec omnia (B p. 558), 1111D veluti e salute (B p. 560), 1116D 2 Cor 3:18 a claritate in claritatem (B p. 562), 1119C Ct 2:17 et inclinentur and 1119D Jo 14:21 diligetur a Patre (B p. 564). All agree with our Latin.

Column notes

  • 1103A — Ps 136:3–4 and (1103C) Ps 118:54 agree with the Clementine. in aliena terra (1103A) is Wolbero's inversion of the verse's in terra aliena, in his own sentence: no marker.
  • 1103B — Hbr 13:14 printed Quia non habemus hic manentem civitatem (Vulg. Non enim habemus): adapted to the sentence, no marker.
  • 1103D — the seventh "voice" of the Spirit is printed per spiritum Domini, where Is 11:2–3 has spiritus timoris Domini (the preceding six follow Isaiah). Rendered as printed, "through the spirit of the Lord". Crux only.
  • 1103D — constituitur tota modulatio, cantici 96 Domini: the comma after modulatio is on the plate; carried.
  • 1104A — Aliena est terra. Homo terrena semper diligendo, et ob hoc alienus a Deo, atque ideo impotens cantare …: the second sentence has no finite verb on the plate or in our Latin; carried as a verbless sentence. 1 Cor 2:14 abridged (autem, ea omitted): no marker.
  • 1104B — Canticum Domini canticorum: Wolbero's own play (the "Canticle of the Lord of canticles"), not a quotation; rendered literally. The orator's officium … finis formula is Augustine's (De doctr. christ. IV), unmarked in the text.
  • 1104C — Sed ut quidam fuit, liceat sperare timenti: plate p. 558 confirms fuit; ait ("as a certain one said") is the obvious sense, introducing a tag. A real word; rendered "as a certain one was". Crux only.
  • 1104D — the lemma re-quoted laeva ejus sub capite meo et dextera illius amplexabitur me? with a question mark (it closes a question, Nonne illa est …): carried.
  • 1105A — exoccupata, constrata: plate as our Latin; rendered "disengaged", "laid down". Ps 45:11 agrees.
  • 1105C — sed adhuc praepudiente mentis infirmitate: plate p. 559 praepudiente, a non-word (for praepediente, "hindering"). [sic: praepudiente], sense "hindering" beside it. Is 18:4 agrees.
  • 1105D — Quid autem dilecti est adjuvare, nisi praeceptorum suorum decretali conscripto obligare?: plate confirms adjuvare ("to help"), where the lemma's verb is adjurare ("to adjure"), which obligare glosses. A real word; rendered "to help". Crux only.
  • 1105D — Quatuor enim naturae notabilia dicitur habere capreas: accusative capreas as printed; the four marks then run in the singular (cum vulnerata fuerit). Rendered "it is said that roes have … that when she has been wounded".
  • 1106C — Alter alterius onera portate et sic …: plate p. 559 (and B p. 557) shows a raised spacing quad after portate, not a point; no punctuation carried. Gal 6:2 agrees.
  • 1106C — donec ipsa vetit: plate p. 559 prints velit, clean (B agrees). TEI patch below; English follows the plate ("till she herself please").
  • 1106C — necessariis utilitatibus: both copies set necessa iis with a gap and no r (a dropped sort); our necessariis taken, no marker (same class as T04's po sit).
  • 1106D, 1106D–1107A — hujus admirationis talis est vis and hujus admirationis verbum: plate confirms admirationis both times, where the passage is about the adjuratio of the lemma. A real word; rendered "admiration". Crux only. The same slip recurs at 1107B (praedictae admirationis).
  • 1106D — Inde est quod non audent eos suscitare quasi vicarios Christi officio, sponsus vero sanctae conversationis studio: plate confirms sponsus, a nominative singular in a slot that needs an accusative plural parallel to vicarios (sponsos). No grammatical slot: [sic: sponsus], sense "as spouses" beside it. Hbr 13:17 printed with reddituri moved before pro animabus vestris and the rest of the verse omitted: no marker.
  • 1107A — our Latin dilecta semper flagrans, confirmari et defendi tali adjuramento; plate p. 560 prints dilecta semper flagrans, gaudeat se confirmari et defendi (two words our Latin lacks, without which confirmari has no governing verb). English follows the plate ("may rejoice that she is confirmed"). The sentence then ends securior facta proclamat, so it has two finite verbs; rendered as printed. TEI patch.
  • 1107A–B — juridicialis (1107B) is the attested rhetorical term (Cicero); rendered "juridical", no marker. Mt 6:33 printed Primum quaerite (Vulg. Quaerite ergo primum): no marker.
  • 1107B–C — our Latin hujus mei, comparem studii; plate p. 560 hujus mei comparem studii (no comma). The comma is ours; English "the partner of this my pursuit". TEI patch. Lc 10:42 agrees.
  • 1107D — incirconscriptus: medieval spelling, Pattern 9. motabilitatem rendered "movableness" (a late form of mutabilitas/mobilitas; not marked). per inspirationem suam venire afferetur: afferetur rendered "alleged" (affero, "to allege"), perhaps for asseretur. The question quomodo venire diceretur … closes with a full stop on the plate; carried.
  • 1108A — Phil 2:8 printed factus est enim obediens Deo Patri usque ad mortem, mortem autem crucis (Vulg. Humiliavit semetipsum factus obediens usque ad mortem): Deo Patri added; adaptation, no marker.
  • 1108B–C — Ps 18:6–7 printed with quia and et joining the half-verses: no marker.
  • 1108C — VERS. 9 prints en iste stat post parietem nostrum (Clementine Ct 2:9 En ipse stat); the lemma is re-quoted En ipse stat at 1109A. Rendered "lo, this one standeth" and "lo, he himself standeth". Not material; no marker.
  • 1108C — sol unigenito non ad mensuram dat Deus spiritum: plate p. 560 sol (for soli, "to the only-begotten alone"). No grammatical slot: [sic: sol], sense "alone" beside it. The scripture here conflates 1 Cor 7:7 (Alii quidem sic, alii vero sic, Vulg. alius … alius), Jo 3:34 and Col 2:9 (habitat for inhabitat), located (I Cor. VII; Joan. III; Coloss. II): conflation, no marker. 1108D Lc 3:22 / Jo 1:32 conflated likewise.
  • 1109A — aufert eis omnem occasionem diffidendi dilectae praetendens dilecti hujus pietatem: the subject is not expressed; the lemma that follows (En ipse stat …) is the bride's, so "[she]" is supplied. dilectae (dative or genitive) rendered "to the beloved". Rendered as closely as the words allow.
  • 1109B — Eph 2:14–16 paraphrased (solvens inimicitias inter Deum et hominem reconciliavit nos Deo): no marker.
  • 1109C — in isto luxuriae, in illo impudicitiae, in aliquo obedientiae, in nonnullo impatientiae: plate p. 561 confirms obedientiae in a list of vices. The faithful English makes obedience a vice, which Wolbero enjoins at 1106D (filii obedientiae); the missing force is the negative prefix. [cj: obedientiae; read inobedientiae, "of disobedience"] (Pattern 18, lost-negation class).
  • 1109C — facit qualemcunque parietem inter hominem et Deum quoque vitium majus vel crebrius sive numerosius et in homine, eo gravius et immobilius pondus est in pariete: plate confirms quoque. The answering member eo gravius is printed and its own member is not (quo … eo, "by as much as … by so much"): a stranded correlative, 18a's admitted class. [cj: quoque; read quo, "by as much as"]. et in homine rendered literally.
  • 1109D — Primo namque diu stetit ne veniret … propter multa prohibendo adventum ejus, multis afflictionibus … quibus insequebatur eos: the subject of prohibendo and insequebatur is not expressed (the human race's malice is meant, not the beloved). Rendered "by hindering … by which those were pursued" so as not to bind the agency to the beloved. Crux only.
  • 1110A — tantae malitiae quasi cujusdam parietis interpositione ends the paragraph with no stop, on the plate and in our Latin; the English carries none (Pattern 8).
  • 1110A–B — donec tandem per motus afflictione suorum: plate p. 561 sets per and motus as two words with a full space mid-line (= permotus, "moved"). Split run carried whole: [sic: per motus], sense "moved" beside it. Veni, Domine, et noli tardare, relaxa facinora plebis tuae is the Advent antiphon (after Hab 2:3), liturgical, not scripture quoted as scripture: no marker (L3).
  • 1110C — 1 Cor 13:9 agrees (enim omitted).
  • 1110D — VERS. 10: our Latin Ea dilectus meus loquitur mihi; plate p. 561 prints En dilectus meus, as the Clementine. English follows the plate ("Behold"). TEI patch. The same lemma prints formo sa split, on both copies (A p. 561, B p. 560), as our Latin: [sic: formo sa] inside the italic lemma (no second italic), sense "beautiful" beside it.
  • 1111A–B — Ct 2:11–13 quoted in the "cause" lists with vineae florentes odorem dederunt (Vulg. dederunt odorem suum) and imber recessit: abridgment and order, no marker.
  • 1111C — per quam legendo dilecta saltus induti pariete mortalitatis nostrae, et loquentis ad se per fenestras …: no finite verb (something like cognoscit seems lost); plate as our Latin. Rendered word for word.
  • 1111C–D — Verum ipse dilectus veluti e salute dilectae sollicitus in hujus modi locutione assumunt sibi officium boni oratoris: plate p. 562 confirms assumunt (plural) with a singular subject. Rendering "takes on" would silently repair the number (Pattern 12 test 2): [sic: assumunt], sense "takes on" beside it. e salute read on B p. 560 (e, not de); rendered "out of the salvation". aliquid ratum et insolitum: ratum ("settled"), perhaps rarum; crux only.
  • 1111D — Porro causam hujus advocationis eum docet: masculine eum where the bride is meant; rendered "him", as printed. Crux only. The attentive/docile/benevolent triad is the rhetoricians' exordium rule (Ad Herennium / De inventione).
  • 1112A — Lam 3:44 adapted (quasi nubem ne transeat oratio oppositam): no marker.
  • 1112B — si abjecto errore infidelitatis mihi per fidem reconciliari: infinitive with no finite verb; rendered literally ("to be reconciled").
  • 1112C — dilectione Dei sola servans omnes mundi curas … postposueris: servans ("keeping") as printed, perhaps spernens; crux only. revertas (active of revertor): Pattern 9.
  • 1112D — Cur autem ad haec tantopere se invito?: plate p. 562 se with first-person invito; no grammatical slot (the Bridegroom is speaking to the bride: te). [sic: se], sense "thee" beside it.
  • 1113A — Mt 3:2 agrees, locator (Matth. X) as printed. quae solidaria et in divina contemplatione posita: plate p. 563 solidaria (for solitaria, "solitary", the turtle's mark of solitude). [sic: solidaria], sense "solitary" beside it.
  • 1113A–B — Lc 1:50 in reported speech, misericordiam ejus in progenie et progenies (Vulg. a progenie in progenies), located (Luc. II): adaptation, no marker.
  • 1113B — fuit enim iu deserto praedicans baptismum poenitentiae: plate p. 563 iu (a turned n). [sic: iu] inside the italic quotation, sense "in" beside it. The verse is Mc 1:4 recast (located Matth. III); Facite fructus dignos poenitentiae is Lc 3:8's form (Mt 3:8 fructum dignum): no marker.
  • 1113B — ⚑ VERS. 13 printed Ficus protulit grossos suos, vineae florentes dederunt odorem suum. Surge, propera, amica mea, speciosa mea, et veni. Clementine Ct 2:13: Surge, amica mea, speciosa mea, et veni (no propera). Plate p. 563 confirms propera. The word is load-bearing: the lemma is repeated at 1114C with propera and expounded et propera docendo (1114D). [var:] fired. (The lemma list marked it ⚠.)
  • 1113C — Rom 10:10 woven with gerunds (corde credendo ad justitiam, ore autem confitendo ad salutem): no marker. Judaea hactenus sterilis carnalis: asyndeton as printed.
  • 1113D–1114A — the "sevenfold cause" list; Causas enim hujusmodi, et quod ex his efficiebatur, sive efficitur causas, hoc est credere …: causas twice; rendered as printed.
  • 1114A — ut videlicet insinuat: indicative after ut, as printed.
  • 1114A–B — Gn 1:1–2 conflated (In principio … terra erat inanis et vacua; Vulg. Terra autem erat): no marker.
  • 1114D — non sine tui periculo de infosso reddes rationem talento: allusion to Mt 25:18–30; no marker.
  • 1115A — Quia te columbae comparavi 108. Columba autem: the full stop after the Cologne page number is on the plate; carried.
  • 1115B — Eph 5:27 recast in the first person (ut te mihi exhiberem sponsam gloriosam non habentem maculam, neque rugam): no marker. Nam cum foeda ejus per te ipsam, pro te ipsa non suffecisti: foeda ejus construes poorly (perhaps foeda esses); rendered literally, "foul of it". The te before ipsa is blacked on scan A; our Latin followed.
  • 1115B — honestum genus causae … humile genus causae: the rhetorical classes of case (Ad Herennium I.3); rendered "an honourable kind of case … a lowly kind of case".
  • 1115D — our Latin quae ex fortitudine, divinitatis erant in Christo; plate p. 564 ex fortitudine divinitatis (no comma). English follows the plate. TEI patch. multa virtutum, operationum et virtutum insignia: virtutum twice, as printed (rendered "powers … virtues"). ostendit judicium (perhaps indicium): rendered "judgment"; crux only. in qua videlicet charitate Ecclesiae quidem generaliter: genitive/dative Ecclesiae where a nominative is wanted; rendered literally.
  • 1116A — 1 Jo 4:16 and 1 Ptr 2:22 agree. 1 Jo 2:1–2 woven, pro peccatis ejus (Vulg. nostris) adapted to the soul: no marker.
  • 1116C — sicut ipsa horum suavitate contemplationis assiduitatem affecta est: accusative assiduitatem has no construction; rendered as an accusative of respect ("affected as to the assiduity").
  • 1116D — the Cologne page number prints 100 where the sequence (109, 111) wants 110; carried verbatim as printed. Ex 34:29 allusion. 2 Cor 3:18 agrees (Nos vero omnes omitted); the a before claritate is blacked on A and read on B p. 562.
  • 1117A — Ez 1:5 agrees.
  • 1117B — Mt 5:16 adapted (opera tua, glorificent Patrem tuum): no marker. Rom 10:10 printed confessio sit ad salutem (Vulg. fit), plate p. 565 confirms sit; subjunctive in a reported clause, rendered "is". Logged only.
  • 1117C — exprimi [potest]: Migne's own bracketed supplement, on the plate. Carried in brackets ("[can] be expressed") as his, not ours.
  • 1117D — Saepe tacens vocem verbaque vultus habet is Ovid, Ars am. I.574, introduced ut quidam dicit; not scripture. 1 Rg 1:18 printed Vultus ejus non sunt amplius in diversa mutati (Vulg. vultusque illius): no marker.
  • 1118A — Hac ergo specie et pulchritudine tua intende, prospere procede et regna: Ps 44:5, unitalicized, woven into the sentence; rendered with the Douay echo ("set out, proceed prosperously, and reign").
  • 1118B — capite nobis vulpes purvulas: plate p. 565 purvulas, inside the italic lemma. [sic: purvulas] (no second italic), sense "little" beside it. The VERS. 15 lemma that follows prints parvulas clean.
  • 1118C–D — in nostrae partis compellite transire conturbernium: plate p. 565 conturbernium (for contubernium). [sic: conturbernium], sense "fellowship" beside it.
  • 1118C — our Latin verbis et exemplis confirmant Capite, inquit; plate p. 565 prints a full stop after confirmant. English follows the plate. TEI patch.
  • 1118D — 1 Cor 3:19 + 1:19 conflated (in astutia sua, Vulg. eorum; located I Cor. I, III): no marker. Vinea enim nostra floruit (Vulg. nam): no marker. ne teneram fidem pietatis opera, et pravi dogmate et pravis exemplis suffocent: pietatis opera has no clear construction (a second object?); rendered literally as an apposition.
  • 1119A — Ps 103:15 printed laetificat (Vulg. laetificet): mood only; logged. cultro spiritualis exercitii sacramenta vitiorum praecidere: plate p. 566 confirms sacramenta, where the vine-dresser's figure wants sarmenta ("shoots"). A real word; rendered "sacraments". Crux only. radici cordis in memoria habendo circumponere: no object expressed; rendered "to lay it about the root".
  • 1119B — vinea Sorec, quod electam et pulchram sonat: Jer 2:21 / Is 5:2 (Heb. soreq), etymology as printed. Jo 15:2 agrees (Pater inserted in roman by Wolbero).
  • 1119C — quandiu praecepta Dei servamus semper excolimus: no object; rendered literally. Rom 9:16 recast (Non enim est volentis … sed miserentis Dei; Vulg. Igitur non volentis … sed miserentis est Dei): no marker. The Cologne number is set 113 in our Latin (space inside the italics); carried as is.
  • 1119C — VERS. 16, 17 agrees with Ct 2:16–17a (the ⚠ in the lemma list is the verse boundary). et inclinentur read on B p. 564.
  • 1119D — Jo 14:21 agrees (Qui autem shortened to qui); a Patre read on B p. 564.
  • 1120A — Proprium est enim relativorum ad convertentiam dici: Aristotle's rule for relatives (Cat. 7, Boethius's ad convertentiam), "to be said reciprocally". The argument turns on dilectus / dilecta and their cases, which English "beloved" cannot show, so the forms dilectus … dilecta … dilectus dilectae, dilectus et dilecta, dilecti dilecta are kept in italic Latin (Song rule, case 2: the gloss depends on the word). ut videant dilectione divina quae et in unoquoque sua diligit dona: videant has no object; rendered literally.
  • 1120B — nisi enim me diligeret ex misericordia, ego eum diligere non possem ex gratia, ipse me non diligeret ex misericordia.: plate as our Latin. The third clause repeats the first's words, now negative, with no conditional of its own (something like et nisi ego eum diligerem seems lost before it). Not a contiguous doubled run, so no [d:]; rendered literally. quidquid boni mihi adjacet velle, posse agere, perficere, located (Rom. IV): echoes Rom 7:18 and Phil 2:13; locator as printed.

  • 1107B–C · quando hujus mei, comparem studii → quando hujus mei comparem studii · p. 560

Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0035.json.

Migne's foot notes

None on any page 566–574. Every foot was read full width. The only matter below the text is the signature PATROL. CXCV under the left column of p. 571 and the gathering number 36 under its right column. No [cn:] inserted in the Latin; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0035.tsv is header-only. No numbering sequence to check, nothing unplaced, no asterisk- or letter-keyed note.

The query at 1127C, concessus (an. concussus?), is an inline parenthetical in the running text on the plate (p. 570), not a foot note. Our TEI carries it as [n: ( an. concussus?)], and the English carries that note verbatim at the same spot.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Ct 2:17 Donec aspiret dies et inclinentur umbrae = Till the day breathe, and the shadows decline (1120B). T07 (0030–0034) may have met the verse first: reconcile at merge.
  • Revertere: similis esto, dilecte mi, capreae, hinnuloque cervorum super montes Bethel = Return: be thou like, my beloved, to a roe, and to a young hart upon the mountains of Bethel. caprea = roe, hinnulus (cervorum) = young hart, cervi = harts, following T06's Ct 2:7/2:9 in 0031. Wolbero's own capreae aut hinnulo = "to a roe or to a young hart".
  • Ct 2:16 dilectus meus mihi et ego illi = my beloved to me and I to him; qui pascitur inter lilia = who feedeth among the lilies.
  • lectulus (Ct 3:1, 3:7) = little bed. The diminutive is kept because the gloss works on the object (requies, lectulus malae securitatis, lectulum Salomonis).
  • genitrix (Ct 3:4) = her that bore (me), set against mater = mother, because 1125B distinguishes the two (dicamus genitricem quoque ejus esse, secundum hoc quod eum generat).
  • Ct 3:5 follows T06's Ct 2:7 exactly: I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, by the roes and the harts of the fields, that you stir not up nor make the beloved to awake, till she herself please.
  • Ct 3:6 virgula fumi = a little rod of smoke, not Douay's "pillar", because the gloss rests on the rod (Quasi virgula fuit, quia recta et rigida, 1132B; virgam aequitatis, 1133C). ascendit = ascendeth, because the gloss is on ascensus. universi pulveris pigmentarii = of the whole powder of the perfumer, singular, because the gloss reads it as one universal powder (universus pigmentarii pulvis … charitas).
  • dilectio = love, charitas = charity, dilectus / dilecta = the beloved (1122D inter dilectum et dilectam = "between him who is beloved and her who is beloved").
  • solitudo = solitude throughout, including in the proof-texts (Is 35:1, Jer 50, Ps 54:8), because 1129C–1131D is an exposition of solitudo corporis / animi. desertum = desert; Mt 23:38 deserta = "deserted".
  • continens / continentia (1130A–B) = the continent one / continence. English "continent" keeps the Latin's double sense (holding in / self-restraint) and does not choose between them.
  • concorporatio · concorporalis · consacramentalis (1134B–1135A) = concorporation · concorporal · consacramental.
  • VERS. n.-- = VERSE n.-- (orchestrator's convention).
  • Pattern 17: the sisters (1127B–1129C) and the watchmen (1124B) are you; the bride to the Bridegroom (1122A–C) is thou; Mt 5:23–24 and Mt 6:6 are thou, Jo 14:27 you.

Markers fired (all on plate-read columns)

  • [var:] ×2: 1121B (Ct 2:17 Bethel), 1127A (Jo 12:35 Currite).
  • [sic:] ×5: 1120C dicautur, 1123C praevale, 1123D gradiuntu, 1124B suscitatur, 1129A testitudinis.
  • [cj:] ×3: 1124D pro determinatione (dropped non), 1125D operitur (for aperitur), 1133B quanto animus (stranded correlative).
  • [d:], [ed:]: none.

The scripture divergence list (every span checked against sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt by chapter and verse)

[var:] fired:

  • 1121B Ct 2:17 printed super montes Bethel (and again at 1122A and 1122C); the Clementine has super montes Bether. The gloss depends on the printed name: super montes Bethel, id est domus Dei quae est Ecclesia (1122B). Marked once, at the verse lemma. Plate p. 567 reads Bethel at all three places.
  • 1127A Jo 12:35 printed Currite dum lucem habetis ne vos tenebrae comprehendant; Vulg. Ambulate dum lucem habetis, ut non vos tenebrae comprehendant. Wolbero adduces it in a chain of proof-texts for running and leaping (capreae et cervi … quosdam saltus … mentis agilitatem, directly after 1 Cor 9:24 Sic currite), so the printed verb carries the argument. Plate p. 570.

Divergences NOT marked (abridgment, adaptation, word order, allusion, spelling; one line each):

  • 1120C 1 Cor 15:28 agrees. 1120D, 1123A Ct 2:16 agrees.
  • 1120D Ego ero illis in patrem et ipsi erunt mihi in filios et filias (roman, located II Reg. VII): 2 Rg 7:14 put into the plural and conflated with 2 Cor 6:18. Paraphrase.
  • 1121A Et ego mundis intersum cogitationibus (located Prov. VIII): not in Prov 8 or anywhere in the Clementine. Allusion or a non-Vulgate saying. Not collatable.
  • 1121A Mt 5:8 agrees. donec dies est is Wolbero's recasting of the lemma.
  • 1121B Eph 5:9 (roman, unlocated) agrees.
  • 1121C cum fieret vox supra firmamentum, stabant et submittebant alas suas (roman, located Ezech. I): Ez 1:25 has super firmamentum. Roman type, paraphrase within Gregory's exposition.
  • 1122A Ct 2:17 recast: Revertere, dilecte mi, similis esto capreae aut hinnulo cervorum (word order, and aut hinnulo for hinnuloque). This is the bride's speech in Wolbero's own reuse; Bethel is marked at the lemma.
  • 1122A 1 Cor 13:12 with enim woven in. 1122D, 1 Jo 4:8/16 Deus charitas est: spelling only.
  • 1123B–1126C Ct 3:1–5 agree (genitricis for genetricis is spelling). The second lemma at 1124C swaps the pronouns (tenui illum … introducam eum against tenui eum … introducam illum); English "him" either way.
  • 1123D Mt 7:13–14 (roman): paraphrase. 1124A Lc 12:37 agrees (located Luc. XXII, Migne's locator kept).
  • 1125A domus Matris et cubiculum genitricis: Wolbero's nominal recasting of Ct 3:4.
  • 1126D Col 3:1–2 conflated in reverse order (Quae sursum sunt sapite … quae sursum sunt quaerite). Phil 3:20 agrees.
  • 1126D Heb 4:11 Festinate ingredi for Festinemus ergo ingredi (second person for first, ergo dropped): person only; the exhortation stands.
  • 1126D–1127A Festinantes et properantes in occursum Domini (located Amos. IV): Am 4:12 praeparare in occursum Dei tui; the wording is closer to 2 Pet 3:12 exspectantes et properantes in adventum diei Domini. Allusion.
  • 1127A 1 Cor 9:24, Lc 21:28 agree. Ps 83:6–7 agrees (lacrymarum spelling).
  • 1127B si Spiritu ambulatis, et facta carnis mortificastis (located Rom. VIII): Gal 5:16 Spiritu ambulate and Rom 8:13 si autem spiritu facta carnis mortificaveritis conflated and put into the indicative perfect as an address to the sisters. Gal 5:17 agrees.
  • 1128A–B Ez 3:12 post vos for post me: adapted to the sisters (Migne sets post in italic and vos in roman). Agrees otherwise.
  • 1127C 4 Rg 3:13 Quare congregavit hos tres reges ut traderet eos in manus Moab? omits Dominus, so the verb has no expressed subject; rendered "Why hath he gathered". 4 Rg 3:15–16 et dixit for et ait, Haec dicit Dominus dropped: abridgment.
  • 1128D, 1129B Horace, Ars poetica 343 and 355–356: not scripture, not collated. Migne sets Omne tulit punctum as its own indented line; our Latin carries it inline after Flacci:, and the English follows our Latin's paragraphing (layout only, no rebuild needed for the sense). The two lines at 1129B are lineated in both.
  • 1130C Ps 64:13 agrees. 1130D Is 41:18 in stagnum aquae for in stagna aquarum (singular); Wolbero's gloss repeats the singular. Not material.
  • 1130D Is 35:1 agrees in words; the plate punctuates Laetabitur deserta, et invia et exsultabit solitudo, and the English follows that comma.
  • 1131A Mt 23:38 without Ecce. 1131A Lam 2:2 (located Thren. II, introduced in Jeremia): the Clementine's nec pepercit omnia speciosa Jacob: destruxit in furore suo muntiones is repunctuated and cut so that omnia speciosa Jacob becomes the object of destruxit. The printed words are all in the verse; the sense (the Lord destroyed Jacob's beauty) is not reversed. Not marked.
  • 1131B Terra in solitudine redigetur (located Jer. L): Jer 50:13 redigetur tota in solitudinem. Paraphrase.
  • 1131C Ps 54:7 without initial Et; Ps 54:8–9 agree. 1131C Mt 4:1 alluded to.
  • 1131D Mt 6:6 Cum orabis, intra in cubiculum, et clauso ostio ora Patrem tuum for Tu autem cum oraveris, intra in cubiculum tuum … in abscondito: abridged.
  • 1132C–D Rom 8:38–39 printed Certus sum quod neque vita, neque mors for Certus sum enim quia neque mors, neque vita, and creatura aliqua for creatura alia; seperare is a medieval spelling (plate p. 572). The inversion of life and death is noted here; the gloss does not turn on it.
  • 1132D Rom 5:5 agrees. 1133A 1 Cor 13:4–7 agrees except that the second Caritas is not repeated.
  • 1133C Rom 8:18 quia non sunt for Existimo enim quod non sunt. Ps 50:19 with enim. 1133D Ps 21:7 without autem. Ps 105:23 in conspectu Dei for in conspectu ejus. Ex 32:31–32 agrees; Migne prints a question mark, carried.
  • 1133D canem se mortuum et pulicem (located I Reg. XXIV, XXVI): allusion. 1134A minimum se apostolorum (located II Cor. XV, Migne's wrong locator for 1 Cor 15:9, kept); Rom 9:3 alluded to.
  • 1134A Ct 3:7 agrees.
  • 1134C Caput enim Ecclesiae Christus, ipsa vero corpus Christi (located I Cor. XI): allusion to 1 Cor 11:3 / 12:27 / Eph 5:23.
  • 1134D Jo 6:57 carnem meam … sanguinem meum … in eo for meam carnem … meum sanguinem … in illo: word order and pronoun only.
  • 1135B Mt 5:23–24 agrees except that ergo is dropped. 1135C Jo 14:27 printed Pacem meam do vobis, pacem relinquo vobis, the two clauses in reverse order. Not material.

Column notes (plate and TEI agree unless stated)

  • 1120C ut hoc ipsum quod sunt aliorum dicautur: plate p. 566 prints dicautur (the n set as u). It has no form. [sic: dicautur], sense "they are said" beside it.
  • 1120C ubi ipse in me … et ego in eo … pascantur: plural verb for the two subjects; rendered "shall be fed".
  • 1121A fugato caligine: masculine participle with feminine caligo. Gender, no English exponent (Pattern 9).
  • 1121C Huic enim de sanctis animalibus scriptum est: the passage is Gregory's (Moralia), where Hinc is expected. The plate prints Huic; rendered literally, "For to this".
  • 1121C qualitate: plate p. 567 has a broken sort (qualita followed by a damaged letter); TEI's full word is not in doubt.
  • 1122A veluti servorum hinnulus: plate p. 567 prints servorum ("of servants") in a sentence built on hinnulus cervorum ("of harts") twice over. Rendered literally, "like a young hart of servants". cervorum is the obvious reading. Crux only: a real word, and the English is odd rather than false.
  • 1122C Quo enim velocior potuit saltus tuus … quam una hora omnium linguarum scientiam dare.: a rhetorical question closed with a full stop on the plate; kept (Pattern 8).
  • 1122C–D the plate's cucurri ti and ed minime are worn sorts where the TEI has cucurristi and sed; the letters are visible in outline. No patch.
  • 1123C ut quod per se intelligere non praevale: plate p. 568 prints praevale followed by a space and comma. [sic: praevale], sense "does not prevail" (the non stands outside the marker).
  • 1123D pauci vero qui per angustam viam gradiuntu: plate p. 568 as the TEI, gradiuntu before the locator. [sic: gradiuntu], sense "walk".
  • 1123D our Latin allegoriam quoque quae est quasi augusta via; plate p. 568 reads angusta. English "narrow". TEI patch.
  • 1124B Tales ergo vigiles dilecta cunctabunda suscitatur, dicens: suscitatur ("is roused") is passive, and it has an accusative object and introduces a question. sciscitatur ("questions") is the verb the sentence needs. A real word with no grammatical slot (the Pecte class), so [sic: suscitatur] with the sense "questions" beside it.
  • 1124D hujus gratiae participium, et virtute diffusae, in se per Spiritum sanctum charitatis: diffusae agrees with gratiae but the clause does not construe cleanly. Rendered as closely as the words allow: "the participation of this grace, and, by virtue, of it diffused in herself".
  • 1124D Nam dictio ista donec pro determinatione, sed pro sempiterno posita esse intelligi debet: plate p. 568 has no non (the line reads pro determinatione, sed pro sempiterno posita esse). As printed, the page says the donec of Ct 3:4 is meant as a limit, and the clause that follows (ut ultra a mente nostra decidere non possit) and the sed both say the opposite. This is the dropped-non of a non … sed pair, the 7561 @0648 class. [cj: pro determinatione; read non pro determinatione, "not for a limit"].
  • 1124D meo illum et aliorum sensui miserendo: rendered literally, "pitying him to my sense and to that of others"; the construction wants inserendo ("implanting him in"). Crux only.
  • 1125A domus namque quaeque … in qua non tantum Dominus, sed et tota familia intrat: capital Dominus on the plate, though the sense is the master of a house; rendered "the Lord" as printed.
  • 1125C et qui adhuc altera … investigare … possunt: altera ("other things"), perhaps for altiora ("higher things"). Rendered literally.
  • 1125D quia eis subtilitas … et quaedam arcana discussio sacramentorum divinorum ex secreto erudientis Ecclesiae operitur: operitur = "is covered, hidden", in a sentence that explains why the beloved is shown to the great alone (Unde eis tantum dilectus in cubiculo genitricis ostenditur, quia eis … operitur). As printed, the reason given is that the secrets are hidden from them, the reverse of the claim. [cj: operitur; read aperitur, "is opened"]. Plate p. 569 prints operitur clearly.
  • 1125D Post autem ista dilectae inquisitio, inventio et introductio, hoc etiam modo accipi, ut …: no finite verb (potest?). Rendered as printed, a sentence without a main verb.
  • 1126A quam est aliter dicere mundum istum: rendered "which is otherwise to say this world".
  • 1127D Nam quia humilitati vestrae videbatur … utile ac necessarium indicatis: present indicatis in a past frame; rendered literally, "you indicate".
  • 1128C excessum meum facilem fore aestimavi: "that my excess would be easy". Literal.
  • 1129A saxa movere sono testitudinis: Horace's line (Ars 395) has testudinis. Plate p. 571 prints testitudinis, a non-word. [sic: testitudinis], sense "of the lyre".
  • 1129D–1130A Ascensus quippe est corporis ascensus et cordis ends col. 1129 with no stop, and col. 1130 opens a new paragraph Desertum Ecclesia gentium; desertum Synagoga …: the sentence on ascent is never finished and the list of the five deserts begins without a verb. Plate p. 571 shows exactly this (the column ends at the page signature; the next opens with the Cologne 125). Migne prints no note here. Rendered as printed: the English sentence stops where the plate stops, and the list is rendered with its implied "is". Something has probably fallen out in Gravius's or Migne's copy.
  • 1130A vero gratia mundus deseret se continentem: vero gratia ("but by grace") where verbi gratia ("for example") would suit the sentence better. Rendered literally. deseret is future; the plate's dese-|?et is clipped at the left edge, and the TEI's deseret is taken.
  • 1130C noncupentur: a medieval spelling of nuncupentur; rendered "is named" (Pattern 9).
  • 1130D doctrinae postmodum rivos emanere fecit: emanere for emanare. The spelling is rendered "to flow forth" (Pattern 9, a vowel variant).
  • 1132B Novem facti, cum pro orthodoxa fidei integritate servanda … perpessi sunt, myrrham fumantis fidei … emiserunt: plate p. 572 prints Novem facti, ("nine made"). The subject should be the martyrs of the persecuted Church (Nam et facti? Nonnulli?). No reading is certain. Rendered literally, "Nine made".
  • 1132C miraculorum acerbitate excaecavit: "by the bitterness of miracles". Literal.
  • 1132C in cultu Dei et facta religione ferventes: rendered "in religion put into deed".
  • 1133A quaecunque ultima, et de toto corpore boni munitissima fuerit virtus, haec sit charitatis atomus: munitissima ("most fortified") where an atom of powder wants minutissima ("smallest"), the word Wolbero uses at 1133C–D (in minutissimum pulverem, Minutissimo … pulvere). Rendered literally. Crux only: odd, not a claim against his argument.
  • 1133B quia quanto animus circa exteriora occupatur, et quanto magis cogitationum inutilium frequentia deseritur, tanto magis divinitatis amore … subvehitur: plate p. 573 has no comparative in the first quanto clause, so as printed the soul is raised to God the more it is occupied with outward things, against 1131D (quanto minus ea quae mundi sunt cogitat, tanto magis) and the whole exposition of the desert. The answering tanto magis is printed and the first member's comparative is not: the stranded-correlative class (11535 @1231B). [cj: quanto animus; read quanto minus animus, "the less the soul"].
  • 1133D quando per valde humiliati cordis confractionem in summis virtutibus positus: masculine positus with no expressed subject; rendered "one set in the highest virtues".
  • 1134A ut magis effugiat animos demirantium quam teneatur consideratione imitari: rendered as closely as the words allow.
  • 1134B lectulum Salomonis [1134B] hujus: the column anchor falls between Salomonis and hujus; the English puts hujus ("this") before the lemma and keeps the anchor after it.
  • 1135A–B aliquibus ad exitum provenit / quod nulli ad exitum … proficit: exitus ("end, departure") where exitium ("destruction") is the usual pairing with vita and salus. Rendered "to their end" as printed.
  • 1135B quicunque de eo participantur: deponent use of participo; rendered actively.
  • 1135D illo veniente, et re incorporante qui reconciliavit nos: plate p. 574 spaces re incorporante as two words, as does the TEI. It is either re (ablative of res, the res sacramenti of 1135A–C) or a split reincorporante. Rendered as two words, "incorporates in reality". Crux only.
  • Scan-wide: this copy blacks out most one-letter words and some two-letter ones (1120D ■ converso, 1123B requies ■ tranquillitas, 1130A dicitur ■ deserendo, 1131D magis ■ carnalibus). None bears on a scripture collation or a reading in this range (the italic a pusillanimitate at 1131C prints clearly), so scan B was not consulted.

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)

  • 1123D · quae est quasi augusta via → quae est quasi angusta via · p. 568

Lemma count

103 marked spans in the Latin of 0035–0039 (19 + 27 + 7 + 26 + 24), counted paragraph-bounded: no line has an odd asterisk count, and there are no guillemets. That agrees with the 103 in 11512-lemmata-0035.txt. The count includes the Cologne page numbers (114–131), the head 123 and the two Continuatio. heads.

Columns 1136A–1151C (Book III, CAP. III. Continuatio to the opening of CAPUT IV, Ct 3:7 gloss end through Ct 4:3 lemma). Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 574–582, each opened once at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (574 = 1135/1136 … 582 = 1151/1152), text collated and the foot read full width in the same visit. Page 574 is shared with the previous stint and was collated from Sancti, quoque (foot of col. 1135) on; page 582 was collated to the VERS. 3 lemma at 1151C. Second witness raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecur195mign.pdf opened at pp. 575 (cols 1141/1142), 578 (1147/1148), 579 (1149/1150), corners read; it is the same printing. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0040.json.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 574–582; every foot read full width, and the text runs to the bottom margin on all nine pages. No [cn:] inserted in the Latin; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0040.tsv is header-only. Nothing unplaced, no sequence to check.

Lemma count: 132 italic spans, 0 guillemet spans, counted paragraph-bounded (0040 = 21, 0041 = 32, 0042 = 40, 0043 = 3, 0044 = 36; no paragraph with an odd delimiter count; the three Continuatio. heads are included, as in the inventory). Agrees with the 132 in 11512-lemmata-0040.txt. The English carries the same number per chunk.

Markers fired (all on plate-read columns):

Column notes

  • 1136B — Nam quibus omnia cum illo donavit, quando omnipotentem non habent, nihil est quod in ipso et per ipsum non possint. Plate p. 574 confirms non habent (zoomed). Rendered literally: "when they do not have the Almighty, there is nothing which … they cannot do". The argument wants "since they have the Almighty"; quando may be interrogative ("when do they not have the Almighty?"), which construes, but the plate prints a comma, not a question mark. Carried, crux only (the negation is printed and is in the English).
  • 1137B — numerositas sexagenario numero determinatur : non enim nihil rationis habet: plate p. 575 prints a colon after determinatur; our Latin has no mark. English follows the plate ("fixed at the number sixty: for"). TEI patch (punctuation).
  • 1137D — Solent autem mihi qui Arithmeticae artis scientiam habent … dicere: plate confirms mihi. Rendered "are wont to say to me". superfluum / superflui / superfluitatem kept on one root ("superfluous … superfluity") because the gloss turns on it.
  • 1138A — plate prints sexagenari i at a line end with a wide space before the final i; read as justification spacing at the end of the line, not a split sort. Our Latin sexagenarii. No marker.
  • 1139B — aliud avaritiae contra liberalitatem: the list otherwise runs virtue contra vice; here the order is reversed. Plate confirms. Rendered literally ("another of avarice against liberality"). Crux only.
  • 1139D — suggestio, delectasio, consensus: plate p. 576 confirms delectasio (zoomed; the s is clear). Non-word for delectatio. [sic: delectasio] with the sense "delight" beside it.
  • 1139D — ut praecito capite malae suggestionis: plate confirms praecito (zoomed). Non-word for praeciso. [sic: praecito], sense "cut off" beside it.
  • 1140A — parant sagittas suas ut sagittent pauperem in abscondito (Psal. X): roman on the plate, not a span; an allusion (Ps 10:3 paraverunt sagittas suas in pharetra, ut sagittent in obscuro rectos corde, with Ps 9B:9's pauperem). No marker.
  • 1140B — sequentia insinuaut: plate confirms (zoomed). Non-word for insinuant. [sic: insinuaut], sense "suggests" beside it.
  • 1140C — Unde enim accipiunt ut fortes sint, nisi … auctoritatibus. A question in form, closed by a full stop on the plate; the English keeps the stop.
  • 1140C — ferculum a ferendo dicatur: the gloss is an etymology. The English keeps ferendo in italic (as printed) and names the Latin noun in straight quotes ("\"ferculum\", a litter, is so called from ferendo, bearing"). Elsewhere ferculum = "litter". Note that Wolbero also treats the ferculum as a course of a banquet (coelestium ferculum epularum, 1141A; ad cujus epulas, 1145C); "a litter of heavenly banquets" renders the words as printed.
  • 1140D — columnae argenteae, reclinatorium aureum, ascensus purpureus, et istorum medium charitate constratum: plate cons ratum with a damaged t (letter space present); our Latin constratum. No marker.
  • 1141A — per spiritus fortitudinem et mundanae vitae candorem: plate confirms mundanae. Rendered literally, "the whiteness of worldly life" (peer rendering of mundanus = worldly, 1089C). The sense wants something like mundatae ("cleansed"); crux only.
  • 1141A — Cologne page number 131 printed after 136 (1140B), then 132–136 again (1142A–1145D) before 137 (1146D). Plate pp. 577–579 confirm the sequence as printed; carried verbatim (it is Gravius's pagination, repeated as Migne prints it).
  • 1141B — jam intelligens quasi requiescendo redimat: plate confirms. Rendered "may … redeem himself". Crux only (recreet? possible).
  • 1142D — dum ad eam veluti ad quemdam optimum et certum fieri omnes … respiciant sententiae: plate p. 577 confirms fieri. Rendered "as to a certain best and sure becoming". Probably finem ("end"), which 1 Tim 1:5 finis praecepti immediately confirms; crux only (not a negation or correlative).
  • 1143A — sed omnia et charitate tanquam multos ramos ex una arboris radice debere procedere: plate p. 578 confirms et. Rendered "all things, and by charity, ought to proceed". Probably ex charitate; crux only.
  • 1143D — tortitudinis suae infirmitate: tortitudo ("crookedness"), a real word; plate confirms. substineant (medieval spelling), no mark.
  • 1144C — sequentia non repugnant, quin unicuique capiti membra non inconvenienter adhaereant: both negations carried.
  • 1145C — quia fide corporalitatis ad corporalitatem exire debetis: plate p. 579 confirms every word. Rendered literally ("by the faith of corporality you ought to go out to corporality"). The argument (1145B ex corporalium … consideratione exite … in totam … incorporalitatem) wants a corporalitate ad incorporalitatem; not repaired, crux only.
  • 1145D — Unde vos, o filiae Sion: the vocative o prints on scan A here.
  • 1146B — our Latin conpositione; plate p. 579 prints com-|positione (line break after com-). TEI patch (orthography; English unaffected).
  • 1146D — a qua et ipse sponsalia exigit, 137 exigit fidei scilicet et virtutum merita: exigit is printed twice, either side of the Cologne page number (a catchword repetition carried from the 1630 page break). Plate confirms both. Identical runs: [d: exacts] on the second copy.
  • 1147B — ad istarum nuptiarum pulchritudinem … filiam Sion egredi hortabatur, quatenus et ipsae: singular filiam followed by plural ipsae … viderent. Plate p. 580 confirms. Rendered as printed ("the daughter of Sion … they too").
  • 1147C — the 138 CAPUT IV. head stands in the left column of p. 580 between amica mea. and VERS. 1; the D band letter sits beside VERS. 1. Our anchor order agrees.
  • 1148A — alius sublevitico: ablative/dative after alius … leviticus; plate confirms. English "sublevitical" has no case exponent (Pattern 9 class); no marker.
  • 1148C — oculi tui, inquit, colnmbarum: plate p. 580 at 300 and 600 dpi, and scan B p. 578, both show a turned sort, coln- for colu- (same printing, same damage). Our Latin reads columbarum. English "doves'" (the sense either way). No [sic:] fired, because the marker must match the Latin twin verbatim and our twin does not carry the defect; TEI patch filed below; once it lands, [sic: colnmbarum] may be added at this site.
  • 1148C — absque eo quod intrisecus latet (the second re-quotation; the VERS. 1 lemma and 1148D print intrinsecus). Plate and scan B confirm; our Latin agrees. [sic: intrisecus] inside the italic lemma, sense "within" rendered.
  • 1148D — ( caecus enim in tenebris ambulat et nescit quo ea (Joan. XII); 140 ita praelati: the opening parenthesis is never closed (only the locator's). Carried unclosed. ea confirmed on the plate; see the scripture list.
  • 1150C — nisi hujusmodi curis … depositis et toto corpore interioris hominis toto fonte illo divinitatis, de quo dicit Psalmista: the second ablative member has no participle (abluto/perfuso wanted). Plate confirms; rendered verbless as printed ("and with the whole body of the inner man in the whole of that fountain of the Godhead").
  • 1150B — our Latin instititutis; plate p. 581 and scan B p. 579 print insti-|tutis = institutis. TEI patch (English "institutions" either way). The et before suorum, blacked on scan A, prints on scan B.
  • 1151B — hac gemina intentione, materna dilectione … intentione ad allegoriam …, materia ad Vetus et Novum Testamentum, dilectione ad Deum: plate p. 582 confirms materna. The resolving triad shows the intended materia ("with twin intention, matter and love"). Rendered literally, "with maternal love"; crux only.
  • 1151C — 143 VERS. 3: our Latin carries the anchor inside the italic run [1151C] 143; kept identically in the English.

Scripture: collation of every ⚠ span (7a″)

Every ⚠ span in 11512-lemmata-0040.txt, walked against sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt by chapter and verse, the plate read at each.

  • 1135D anima justi dicatur sedes sapientiae (Sap. VII) — not a Vulgate verse (a patristic dictum; nothing in Sap 7 matches). Allusion, no marker.
  • 1136A omnia possibilia credenti (Marc. IX) — Mc 9:22 without sunt. Agrees in sense.
  • 1136A omnia nobis cum illo donavit (Rom. VIII) — Rom 8:32 quomodo non etiam cum illo omnia nobis donavit?, turned from a question into a statement. Adaptation, no marker.
  • 1138D Verus est enim sermo Dei … et discretor cogitationum (Hebr. IV) — Vulg. Hbr 4:12 Vivus est enim sermo Dei. Plate p. 575 confirms Verus (zoomed). "True" for "living". [var:] fired. Also omits ac spiritus after animae, et for ac before medullarum, and et intentionum cordis at the end: abridgment, not marked.
  • 1138D separare hominem … domestici ejus (Matth. X) — Mt 10:35–36 without veni enim; ⚠ only because it spans two verses. Agrees.
  • 1139C qui ensis super femur — gloss re-quotation of Ct 3:8. No marker.
  • 1140B ferculum sibi fecit rex Salomon — Ct 3:9 with sibi before fecit. Agrees in sense.
  • 1140B VERS. 9 Ferculum fecit sibi Salomon de lignis Libani. — Vulg. Ct 3:9 fecit sibi rex Salomon. Plate p. 576 confirms that the verse lemma omits rex (the line just above prints rex Salomon). The exposition keeps the title throughout (veri regis Salomonis, rex Salomon ferculum sibi instituit). Omission of the title, no sense changed: logged, no marker.
  • 1141A VERS. 10 Columnas ejus fecit argenteas, reclinatorium aureum et ascensum purpureum … — Ct 3:10 with et added. No marker. (Our Latin Columnus: see TEI patches.)
  • 1141C hostiam viventem in odorem suavitatis (Ephes. V) — conflation of Rom 12:1 hostiam viventem with Eph 5:2 hostiam Deo in odorem suavitatis. No marker.
  • 1141D, 1142D, 1143B, 1144A media charitate constravit etc. — gloss re-quotations of Ct 3:10. No marker.
  • 1142A columnae ejus argenteae, reclinatorium … aureum — gloss re-quotation, nominative. No marker.
  • 1142C ludibria et verbera experti, insuper vincula et carceres, lapidati sunt, secti sunt, tentati sunt, … in occisione gladii mortui sunt (Hebr. XI) — Hbr 11:36–37 without Alii vero and without et before vincula. Rendered as the peer's 1052C wording. No marker.
  • 1142D finis praecepti charitas de corde puro et conscientia bona et fide non ficta (I Tim. I) — 1 Tim 1:5 without autem and est. Agrees.
  • 1142D in his, inquit, duobus praeceptis tota lex pendet et prophetae (Matth. XXII) — Mt 22:40 In his duobus mandatis universa lex pendet. Synonyms, same sense; no marker. (1136C lex pendet et prophetae is the same verse, ✓.)
  • 1143A si linguis hominum loquar … nihil mihi prodest (I Cor. XIII) — 1 Cor 13:1–3 abridged to Wolbero's triad: 13:2's et si habuero omnem fidem … nihil sum and 13:3's et si distribuero … facultates meas are omitted, and the close reads non habeam for non habuero. Abridgment matched to the gloss; no marker.
  • 1143D ex abundantia cordis os loquitur (Matth. XII) — Mt 12:34 without enim. Agrees.
  • 1143D Sapientia requiescit in corde justi et prudentia in sermone oris ejus (Prov. XIX) — no such verse; nearest Pr 14:33 In corde prudentis requiescit sapientia. A proverb-shaped allusion; locator as printed; no marker.
  • 1144A Filius hominis non habet ubi caput reclinet (Matth. VIII) — Mt 8:20 without autem. Agrees.
  • 1144B Qui enim non diligit proximum quem videt, Deum quem non videt quomodo potest diligere? (I Joan. IV.) — Vulg. 1 Jo 4:20 qui enim non diligit fratrem suum quem vidit, Deum, quem non vidit. Plate p. 578 confirms proximum. The substitution is the argument's own keyword (nisi diligat proximum, the line before). [var:] fired for fratrem suum. The present videt ×2 for vidit is rendered as printed ("seeth"), not marked (the peer did the same at 1098D, where Migne prints fratrem).
  • 1144D propter nimiam enim charitatem suam … ut nos a maledicto redimeret (Ephes. II) — conflation of Eph 2:4 (propter nimiam caritatem suam, qua dilexit nos), Rom 8:3 (Filium suum mittens in similitudinem carnis peccati) and Gal 3:13 (factus pro nobis maledictum; redemit de maledicto), with Deus supplied. Conflation, no marker.
  • 1144D Deus homo factus — a formula, not a verse. No marker.
  • 1145A VERS. 11 … quo coronavit eum mater sua … — Ct 3:11 illum. Pronoun; no marker. Re-quotations 1145B–1146C (Egredimini, et videte, regem Salomonem in diademate…, quo eum coronavit mater sua, In die … desponsationis ejus, in die laetitiae cordis ejus) are gloss fragments of the same verse (ejus for illius, et dropped). No marker.
  • 1145A speculatio, filiae / Sion / speculationis — the interpretation of Sion, not scripture.
  • 1145D Vidimus Jesum propter passionem mortis gloria et honore coronatum, ut gratia Dei pro omnibus gustaret mortem (Hebr. II) — Hbr 2:9 videmus, and without the opening Eum autem, qui modico quam angeli minoratus est. Plate confirms Vidimus. Rendered "We saw" as printed (not conformed to the Vulgate present; 7a″'s 7914 @1071D case). Tense only; no marker.
  • 1146B et miserationes Dei super omnia opera ejus (Psal. CXLIV) — Ps 144:9 miserationes ejus. Pronoun made explicit; no marker.
  • 1146C Qui reconcilians nos Deo, pacificavit omnia quae in coelis sunt et in terris (Colos. I) — paraphrase of Col 1:20 (reconciliare omnia in ipsum, pacificans … sive quae in terris, sive quae in caelis sunt). Paraphrase, no marker.
  • 1146C tanquam sponsus procedens de thalamo (Psal. XVIII) — Ps 18:6 without suo. Agrees. (The italic n of procedens is indistinct at 300 dpi; nothing claimed.)
  • 1146C–D sibi Ecclesiam sponsam gloriosam, non habentem maculam, neque rugam (Ephes. V) — Eph 5:27 ut exhiberet ipse sibi gloriosam Ecclesiam, non habentem maculam, aut rugam: sponsam added (the gloss is on espousals), neque for aut. Adaptation, no marker.
  • 1147B Canticum canticorum — the title, not a span to collate.
  • 1148C columbarum, absque eo quod intrisecus latet — Ct 4:1 with the plate's intrisecus: [sic:] (see column note), not a divergence.
  • 1148D caecus enim in tenebris ambulat et nescit quo ea (Joan. XII) — Vulg. Jo 12:35 qui ambulant in tenebris, nescit quo vadat; the wording is in fact 1 Jo 2:11 in tenebris ambulat, et nescit quo eat, with caecus supplied (1 Jo 2:11 closes tenebrae obcaecaverunt oculos ejus). Plate confirms quo ea (no t). ea has no grammatical slot, so [sic: ea] with the sense "he goeth" beside it (the 1 Jo 2:11 eat), and [var:] fired naming both verses, since the locator points at John.
  • 1149B Capilli tui sicut greges caprarum, quae ascendunt de monte Galaad. — Ct 4:1 ascenderunt. Plate p. 581 confirms the present. Rendered "which come up". Tense only; VERS. 1 (1147D) and 1149D/1150A print ascenderunt. No marker.
  • 1149D acervus testimonii — the interpretation of Galaad, not scripture.
  • 1150B VERS. 2 … omnes gemellis fetibus, et sterilis non est inter eas. — Ct 4:2 foetibus (spelling). Agrees. Re-quotation … et sterilis non est in eis (1150B): gloss wording, no marker. 1150C–D fragments agree (quasi for sicut at 1150D: gloss wording).
  • 1150C Torrente voluptatis tuae potabis eos, quoniam apud te est fons vitae (Psal. XXXV) — Ps 35:9b–10a; ⚠ because it spans two verses. Agrees.
  • 1150C et in lumine tuo videbimus eum (ibid.) — Vulg. Ps 35:10 videbimus lumen. Plate p. 581 and scan B p. 579 both confirm eum. "Him" for "light" changes the object of the vision (the gloss that follows speaks of lumen scientiae). [var:] fired.
  • 1151A Scribe ea tripliciter in corde tuo (Prov. XXII) — conflation of Pr 22:20 descripsi eam tibi tripliciter with Pr 7:3 scribe illam in tabulis cordis tui. Conflation, no marker.
  • ✓ spans spot-checked at the plate and against the file: Jo 15:5, Jo 13:34, Gal 5:17, Pr 4:23, Ps 83:8, Ps 11:7, Gal 6:14, 1 Cor 9:22, Ps 44:10, Is 60:8, Ps 18:8, Ct 4:1, Ct 4:3 all agree.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Following the peers: sponsus/sponsa = Bridegroom/bride · amica mea = my love · lectulus = bed · pacificus = peaceable one · charitas = charity, dilectio = love · Salomon = Solomon · thou/you by number (Pattern 17; the filiae Sion are "you") · VERSE n.-- · Cologne numbers verbatim.
  • Introduced here: fortes / fortissimi = valiant ones / most valiant · ferculum = litter (etymology at 1140C handled as in the note above) · reclinatorium = reclining-place (keeps the root the gloss uses: reclinat, se reclinare, caput reclinet) · ascensus purpureus = purple ascent (in the lemma "the ascent of purple") · media charitate constravit = "the midst he strewed with charity" (Wolbero plays constravit against constratae … mentes, "strewn low", 1141D) · columnae argenteae = pillars of silver · filiae Sion = daughters of Sion · speculatio = watching · acervus testimonii = heap of testimony · Libanus … candidatio = whitening · praelati / praepositi = prelates / those set over · timores nocturni = fears in the night.
  • Heads: CAP. III.-- Continuatio.* rendered CHAP. III.-- Continuation.; CAPUT IV. = CHAPTER IV. (the peers render CAPUT SECUNDUM as CHAPTER TWO). The stints on 0030–0039 meet the same Continuatio* head; one form to be chosen at merge.

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)
