  • 1141A · Columnus ejus fecit argenteas → Columnas ejus fecit argenteas (VERS. 10; confirmed at 600-dpi zoom on scan A and on scan B p. 575) · p. 577
  • 1146B · in hujus diadematis conpositione → in hujus diadematis compositione (printed com-|positione) · p. 579
  • 1148C · oculi tui, inquit, columbarum, absque eo quod intrisecus latet → oculi tui, inquit, colnmbarum, absque eo quod intrisecus latet (turned u; same on scan B p. 578; a [sic: colnmbarum] may then be fired in 0044) · p. 580
  • 1150B · suorum praepositorum instititutis → suorum praepositorum institutis (printed insti-|tutis; same on scan B p. 579) · p. 581

Columns 1151C–1169D (Book III, CAPUT IV continued: gloss of Ct 4:3 through the opening of Ct 4:12). Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf (scan A), pages 582–591, each opened once at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (582 = 1151/1152 … 591 = 1169/1170), text collated and the foot read full width in the same visit. Page 582 is shared with T09 and was collated from the 143 VERS. 3 lemma (1151C) on; page 591 is shared with the next stint and was collated to perdunt. (1169D), with the foot read full width. Second witness raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecur195mign.pdf (scan B, PDF page = floor((col+9)/2)) opened at pp. 580, 581, 582, 583, 584, 585, 589, corners read; every single-letter question below was settled on B at 600 dpi. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0045.json.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 582–591. Every foot read full width; the text runs to the bottom margin on all ten pages (p. 587 carries only the sheet signature PATROL. CXCV. and the gathering number 27, which are not notes). No [cn:] inserted in the Latin; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0045.tsv is header-only. Nothing unplaced, no sequence to check.

Lemma count: 181 italic spans, 0 guillemet spans, counted paragraph-bounded (0045 = 48, 0046 = 23, 0047 = 45, 0048 = 21, 0049 = 44; no paragraph with an odd delimiter count; the five 138 heads are included, as in the inventory). Agrees with the 181 in 11512-lemmata-0045.txt. The English carries the same number per chunk and per paragraph.

Markers fired (all on plate-read columns):

  • [var:] ×4: 1159B (Job 40:16 thalami), 1159D (Jr 1:12 Bene dixisti), 1163A (Act 7:51 indomabili corde), 1164B (Jr 9:21 Intravit … vestras).
  • [sic:] ×8: 1152D est (foris est ostendere), 1153D petus, 1155B pavi, 1157D montitibus, 1157D canditatio, 1160D es, 1162D soculorum, 1169B husjusmodi.
  • [cj:] ×1: 1169C scriptum (read Christum).
  • [d:] none.
  • Withdrawn at the plate (fired in draft, then refuted on scan B at 600 dpi): 1152D crubescenti (B prints erubescenti: the first letter is an e with a closed eye; A's is ink-filled and reads as c) and 1162B eor (B prints cor, open c; A's is ink-filled). Both are therefore our digitization's errors, filed as TEI patches below, and the English renders the plate's word unmarked. Measured lesson for this volume: c/e cannot be decided on scan A at 300 dpi, and A at 600 dpi still misread both.

Column notes

  • 1151C–D — Nam vitta subjectorum a vitta sanctorum praedicatorum quasi dissonat: plate p. 582 prints vitta twice. The sense wants vita ("the life of the subjects is out of tune with the life of the holy preachers"), but vitta is a real word and the fillet is the image being expounded. Rendered literally ("the fillet of the subjects … the fillet of the holy preachers"). Crux only.
  • 1151D — per gratiam compunctionis: scan A's final m is clipped at the column edge and reads gratian; scan B p. 580 prints gratiam cleanly. No issue.
  • 1152A — ad aeternae vitae amorem succendunt ends with no stop on the plate (and in our Latin); the next paragraph opens Unde subditur. English carries no stop (Pattern 8).
  • 1152B — Porro quanta species septiformis pulchritudinis … praedicat: plate confirms quanta. The run of ordinals (Sexto vero loco, 1153B; Septimum, 1155A) wants quinta, "the fifth kind". Rendered literally, "how great a kind of the sevenfold beauty proclaims"; crux only (a real word, no negation, and the English asserts nothing false).
  • 1152C — malum punicum … malum granatum: the gloss is an etymology of both names, so the English names the Latin in straight quotes ("malum punicum", "malum granatum").
  • 1152D — non crubescenti: see Withdrawn above. TEI patch filed; English "does not blush", unmarked.
  • 1152D — et gloriosa oculorum compositio: plate confirms. One expects a word of modesty (demissa?) beside vultus demissio; rendered literally, "a glorious composure of the eyes". Crux only.
  • 1152D — dum vix aut nunquam sufficit sancta anima foris est ostendere: plate confirms the est. It has no grammatical slot (sufficit … ostendere), and English cannot render it in place, so [sic: est] stands where it falls ("suffices outwardly [sic: est] to show"); the clause keeps its verb and both negatives (vix aut nunquam, "hardly or never").
  • 1153A — Quid est enim quod intrinsecus latet, nisi … operatur. A question in form, closed by a full stop on the plate. English keeps the stop.
  • 1153B — the bold 145 is printed before VERS. 4 and our Latin sets it as 145 with a space; carried verbatim as the peers carry 2 and 75.
  • 1153C — quidquid venustatis vel sublimitatis in se continent doctorum eloquia: on both copies (A p. 583, B p. 581) the letter before the final t is a damaged sort that looks like r (continert). Nothing claimed; the TEI's continent is rendered.
  • 1153C — Parcere subjectis, et debellare superbos: Virgil, Aen. VI 853; Migne's note reads (VIRG., Aeneid., IV, 654.), which is a wrong locator, carried verbatim as his. On the plate the line is set centred on a line of its own with the note centred beneath it; our Latin runs the verse into the end of the paragraph and gives the note its own line. The English follows the chunk's line structure (parity); the plate's lineation is recorded here (Pattern 8a) for any rebuild. The Horace lines at 1153D–1154A are lineated on the plate and in our Latin alike, and the English keeps them one for one.
  • 1153D — ulla possit timere contrariae et adversae patris molimina: plate confirms patris. The sense wants partis, "of the contrary and adverse party"; rendered literally, "the contrary and adverse father" (which, of the devil, is not nonsense). Crux only.
  • 1153D — Horace, Ep. I.1.33–40 (Migne's note Ep. I, I, 39): Fervet avaritia, miseroque cupidine petus. Plate p. 583 and B p. 581 (600 dpi) both print five letters, no c. Non-word for pectus: [sic: petus] inside the italic line, sense "breast" beside it. Also as printed and not scripture, so no marker: ? after petus and after tumes; Nemo adeo ferus est qui non mitescere possit (Horace ut non). Both negatives carried ("No one is so wild that he may not grow gentle").
  • 1154B — nolle acquiescere (I Reg. XV) : si impatientia: plate A p. 583 and B p. 581 print a colon; our Latin has a full stop before lowercase si. English follows the plate (colon). TEI patch.
  • 1154B — Irascimini, et nolite peccare; et quia ira viri justitiam Dei non operatur (Ps. IV): the locator stands after the Jac 1:20 words though it belongs to the Ps 4:5 half. Migne's; carried.
  • 1154D — a speck sits in the paragraph indent before Sed cum idem on scan A; scan B is clean. Not a mark.
  • 1155B — ipsi eruditores minorum in Ecclesia pavi sunt in oculis suis: plate A p. 584 and B p. 582 (600 dpi) both print pavi. pavi ("I fed") is a real form with no slot in the clause; the sense is parvi, "little in their own eyes". [sic: pavi] beside "little".
  • 1155B — veluti filii matres sequendo: rendered literally, "as sons follow mothers".
  • 1155C — Sic et dilectio Dei sine dilectione proximi, nec proximi sine dilectione Dei esse ullatenus potest: one negative is printed, as the second member of a pair whose first member has none. Rendered literally with the one "nor" ("So too the love of God without the love of neighbour, nor that of neighbour without the love of God, can in any wise exist"). The argument plainly means "neither … nor … can exist"; no negative supplied (7a). Crux only.
  • 1155D — Duo ergo uno tempore … facientes: the sentence has no finite main verb before consequitur; carried as printed. Haec ergo duo … existentes: neuter duo with masculine participle, a gender mismatch with no English exponent (Pattern 9).
  • 1156A — nonnunquam sola allegoria quando pleraque juxta litteram stare possunt, quae juxta spiritum minime possunt: the clause gives, as the occasion for allegory alone, the case where the letter stands and the spirit does not. Rendered literally ("sometimes the allegory alone, when many things can stand according to the letter which can by no means stand according to the spirit"). Crux only; minime and nullo modo carried.
  • 1156D — Sed istum decorem duplici modo, hoc est … has no verb; carried verbless.
  • 1157A — aut profuturi regni dilatione: plate confirms. Rendered "from the delay of the kingdom that shall profit him"; futuri would be expected.
  • 1157A — propter quodammodo fumigantem exiguae compunctionis vel poenitentiae moerorem: plate confirms. Rendered "slight compunction"; eximiae ("excellent") may be meant. Crux only.
  • 1157C — VERS. 7: Tota pulchra es amica, et macula non est in te. Plate p. 585 confirms no mea (the re-quotation at 1157C has amica mea). English "love" in the lemma, "my love" in the re-quotation. Omission of a possessive, no [var:].
  • 1157C — Cum, inquit, te superius septennis pulchritudinum extulerim praeconiis … sed … sed tanta …: an anacoluthon (the Cum clause never gets its main clause); carried as printed, with no parentheses supplied.
  • 1157D — etsi aliqua parumper et humana fragilitate accesserit: plate confirms et (one expects ex). Rendered "for a little while and by human frailty".
  • 1157D — VERS. 8: de monti-|tibus pardorum? The plate breaks the word at a line end as monti- / tibus (A p. 585, B p. 583, 600 dpi): the first two glyphs of the second line are worn to bare minims on both copies, but they occupy two letter-widths before bus, so the syllable ti is set twice across the break. Non-word; [sic: montitibus] inside the italic lemma, sense "mountains" beside it. The re-quotations at 1158C and 1160C print montibus. The lemma's closing ? is printed and carried.
  • 1157D — Libanus enim canditatio interpretatur: plate breaks candi- / tatio (A p. 585, B p. 583, 600 dpi; both clear). Non-word for candidatio (candidatio is printed four times in the same paragraph); [sic: canditatio] inside the italic span, sense "whitening" beside it.
  • 1158A — veluti mea Sponsa: Migne capitalizes here, so the English reads "my Bride" (orchestrator's ruling, 2026-09-25: follow his capital at each instance). Every other sponsus/sponsa in this range is lower-case and is rendered "bridegroom"/"bride".
  • 1158B — si ob culpam tui: genitive of the pronoun, not tuam; rendered "for fault of thine".
  • 1158C — quaerentes mea sacramenta quaerant … earumque conversio: feminine earum after masculine quaerentes, a gender mismatch with no English exponent (Pattern 9).
  • 1158C–D — qui cum bonum insitae sibi rationis et visibilium rerum pulchritudinem ipsarum omnium Creatorem cognovissent: three accusatives with one verb, construed as printed ("when they had known the good of the reason implanted in them and the beauty of visible things, the Creator of them all").
  • 1159B — Dormit in umbra, in secreto thalami (Job XL): plate confirms thalami. See the scripture list: [var:].
  • 1159D — ut faciam illud (Jer. I): plate A p. 586 and B p. 584 (600 dpi) both print illua (the last letter is an a, with no ascender; compare faciam). Our Latin has illud. TEI patch filed; the English renders the sense "to perform it" for now, and once the patch lands a [sic: illua] may be fired there.
  • 1159D — Bene, inquit, dixisti: plate confirms dixisti. See the scripture list: [var:].
  • 1160A — ideoque anathematizati et excommunicati ab Ecclesia profecti sunt: rendered "went out from the Church" (from proficiscor).
  • 1160D — ita in nutrimentis virtutum vita animae es, and the paragraph ends with no stop. Plate A p. 586 and B p. 584 (600 dpi) both print two letters and no stop. es ("thou art") has no slot; the clause's only verb is inside it, so the English supplies the sense in plain words beside the marker ("the life of the soul is [sic: es]", 7a⁗ question 1) and carries no stop.
  • 1160D — Montes vero pardorum super superbiam eadem sunt corda reproborum: plate confirms. Rendered literally, "are, over pride, the same hearts of the reprobate"; propter superbiam may be meant. Crux only.
  • 1161C — dextrae partis oculus extra haec visibilia supra seducitur: plate confirms. Rendered "is led away above"; subducitur or sublevatur would be expected. Crux only.
  • 1161D — credere jubemur, discutere non permittimur: plate A p. 587 and B p. 585 print permittimur, matching jubemur; our Latin has permittitur. English follows the plate ("we are bidden to believe, are not permitted to discuss"). TEI patch.
  • 1161D — quomodo corporatur verbum: plate confirms (not incorporatur); rendered "is embodied".
  • 1162A — quod humana ratione non colligitur, valde … per fidei meritum colligatur: Wolbero's play on colligere / colligare; rendered "is not gathered … is strongly bound together".
  • 1162A — non habet meritum fide sua, sed qui illud … credit: the second member is elliptical (no verb of its own); carried elliptical.
  • 1162B — Vulnerasti eor meum: see Withdrawn above. TEI patch filed; English "heart", unmarked.
  • 1162B — fides nempe totius boni fundamentum. dilectio superaedificans: plate prints a full stop followed by lower-case dilectio; carried (". love, building upon it").
  • 1162D — unus soculorum idem est animi vultus: plate A p. 587 and B p. 585 (600 dpi) both print soculorum. Non-word for oculorum; [sic: soculorum] beside "the one of the eyes".
  • 1162D — ut neque elevetur prosperis, neque deliciatur adversis e contra de reproba mente dicitur: the letter after de is a damaged short sort on both copies (a raised stroke on A p. 587 and B p. 585, 600 dpi), so the plate does not decide deliciatur against dejiciatur ("cast down", which the parallel with elevetur wants). Our Latin's deliciatur is rendered ("nor takes delight in adversities"); no marker, because the plate reading is not established. The plate prints no punctuation between adversis and e contra; none supplied.
  • 1163D — non potest nisi amare virtutem ends the paragraph with no stop (plate p. 588); carried.
  • 1164A — non suscipit magis et minus, quae imperfectam cognitionem veritatis … habet: plate p. 588 confirms imperfectam. The argument (the Church in itself admits no more-and-less; its increase is in others) wants perfectam. [cj:] declined: the relative clause can be read concessively, the word is not a negative particle, and there is no printed control. Crux only.
  • 1164A — Quantum vero ad alios quos credit in Christo: plate confirms. Rendered "whom she believes in Christ"; gignit ("begets") may be meant. Crux only.
  • 1164C — quibus quotidie ad vitam nutritur aeternam ends with no stop (plate p. 588); carried.
  • 1164D — si pulchrae non sunt, neque sunt … nec pulchrae sunt … jam non est: every negative carried.
  • 1165B — per figuram tapinosin: the rhetorical term (ταπείνωσις), glossed by Wolbero himself; rendered "the figure tapinosis".
  • 1165B — meliora et pulchriora … sint omnia animae rationali: rendered "better and fairer in all things to the rational soul".
  • 1165C–D — Si ergo incorruptione spiritualis boni uniunt Deo, incorruptione vero corporalis boni, non tantum non uniunt Deo …, corruptione vero illius …: plate p. 589 confirms both incorruptione. uniunt has no object and no stated subject. Rendered literally ("by the incorruption … they unite to God"). The period that closes the question (quae comparatio potest esse in tanta dissimilitudine.) is carried.
  • 1166A — quam omnia hujus mundi delecta: plate confirms. delecta is a real word ("chosen things"); delectamenta ("delights") may be meant. Rendered "all the chosen things of this world". Crux only.
  • 1166D — Super omn es virtutes: plate A p. 589 shows a wide gap or a damaged n; read as spacing. No claim.
  • 1167C — Quis vero nesciat quod mel dulce, cera mellis est? Plate p. 590 confirms mellis. The contrast wants mollis ("wax is soft", which the next sentence develops: mollis atque tractabilis, ceram mollem). Rendered literally, "that wax is of honey". Crux only.
  • 1167C — per corporis passionem ex humilitate mollis: plate confirms. Beside ex divinitate one expects humanitate. Rendered literally, "from his humility". Crux only.
  • 1167D — et haec in praedicatione sive Ecclesia pronuntiat: rendered as printed ("and these things in preaching, or the Church, pronounces"); sua may be meant. Crux only.
  • 1168C–D — Per figuram metaphoram quae quatuor modis fit, id est ab animato ad animatum, ab inanimato ad inanimatum: only two of the four modes are listed, and the eum solum modum, qui est ab inanimato ad animatum is not one of the two named as intermingled. All as printed on p. 590; carried.
  • 1169A — the indirect questions non tamen omnibus patet quomodo … operetur Spiritus sanctus? quare … sint? quomodo … sint? are rendered in direct form behind a colon, keeping all three printed question marks (Pattern 8b).
  • 1169A — sanctorum Scripturarum: masculine for feminine (Pattern 9); "the Holy Scriptures".
  • 1169B — in hortum husjusmodi: plate A p. 591 and B p. 589 (600 dpi) both print husjusmodi. Non-word; [sic: husjusmodi] beside "of this kind".
  • 1169C — nisi per ostium, hoc est scriptum: plate A p. 591 and B p. 589 both print scriptum. A real word, and the faithful English ("through the door, that is, what is written") makes the door Scripture, where the column has just quoted the Saviour saying se esse ostium and goes straight on qui vero per eum (masculine) non accesserit. The printed control is on the same page on both sides of the word, so [cj: scriptum; read Christum, "Christ"] is fired (Pattern 18, word substitution making the English assert what the passage denies). This is the stint's one judgment call at the bar; the orchestrator may demote it to a crux.

Scripture: collation of every ⚠ span (7a″)

Each ⚠ span in 11512-lemmata-0045.txt was walked word by word against sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt, on columns read at the plate.

  • 1152D Frons mulieris meretricis facta est tibi, et noluisti erubescere (Jer. III): Jr 3:3, et added. No sense change, no marker.
  • 1153B VERS. 4 and re-quotation, Ct 4:4: agree word for word. ⚠ Comparand defect: the Clementine file prints mille clypei pendant (subjunctive); Migne's pendent is the ordinary Vulgate reading. Not Migne's divergence; no marker.
  • 1153B manu fortis is the interpretation of David, not scripture. 1153C Parcere subjectis … is Virgil (see column note).
  • 1153D–1154A the eight Horace lines are not scripture (see column note).
  • 1154A si quis dilexerit mundum (1 Jo 2:15 diligit): tense only, no marker.
  • 1154A Non efficiamur inanis gloriae cupidi (Gal. VI): Gal 5:26; agrees (locator Migne's).
  • 1154A Quia hilarem datorem diligit Deus (II Cor. IX): 2 Cor 9:7 hilarem enim datorem; connective only.
  • 1154A–B Nescitis quia corpora vestra templum sunt Spiritus sancti? Si quis violaverit templum hoc, disperdet illum; templum enim Dei sanctum est, quod estis vos (I Cor. VI): a conflation of 1 Cor 6:19 (membra vestra) and 3:17 (Si quis autem templum Dei violaverit, disperdet illum Deus). Conflation, no marker; the English keeps the printed corpora ("bodies") and the subjectless disperdet ("he shall destroy").
  • 1154B ira viri justitiam Dei non operatur (Ps. IV): Jac 1:20; agrees.
  • 1154B 1 Rg 15:22–23: agrees. ⚠ Comparand defect: the Clementine file prints nole acquiescere, not a word; Migne's nolle is sound. No marker.
  • 1154B patiens melior est expugnatore urbium (Prov. XVI): a compression of Pr 16:32 Melior est patiens viro forti, et qui dominatur animo suo expugnatore urbium. Compression, no marker.
  • 1154C–1155A Ct 4:4 fragments (clypei pendent ex ea, clypei et omnis armatura fortium pendent ex ea, mille clypei pendent et omnis armatura fortium): re-orderings in the gloss, no marker.
  • 1155A VERS. 5 and re-quotation: Ct 4:5 plus 4:6a (donec aspiret dies, et inclinentur umbrae), which Wolbero's text attaches to v. 5. Words agree; versification only.
  • 1155B duo ubera sicut duo hinnuli capreae gemelli (no tua); 1156B Qui pascuntur … umbrae: agree.
  • 1156D qui enim sunt Christi … (Gal V): Gal 5:24 autem; connective, no marker. The italic run carries Migne's roman locator (Gal V) inside it in our Latin; the English follows our Latin.
  • 1157C VERS. 7, without mea: see column note.
  • 1157D VERS. 8: agrees apart from montitibus ([sic:]) and the printed ?.
  • 1158D non sicut Deum glorificaverunt … servierunt creaturae magis quam Creatori (Rom. I): a conflation of Rom 1:21 and 1:25 (qui commutaverunt, coluerunt, et servierunt … potius quam). Conflation, no marker.
  • 1159A Ipse enim est rex super omnes filios superbiae (Job XLI): Job 41:25 universos. No sense change, no marker.
  • 1159B Dormit in umbra, in secreto thalami (Job XL): Job 40:16 Sub umbra dormit in secreto calami. "Bedchamber" for "reed" changes the image the gloss then uses (quasi in umbra peccati sibi requiem parat). Plate p. 586 confirms thalami. [var: Vulg. Job 40:16 in secreto calami, …] fired.
  • 1159B tanquam leo rugiens circuit quaerens quem devoret (I Petr. V): 1 Pt 5:8; agrees.
  • 1159A–D nocturna avis, dens vigiliarum, anathema: the name-interpretations, not scripture.
  • 1159D Quid tu vides, Jeremia? / Virgam vigilantem ego video: Jr 1:11; agree. et Dominus: Bene, inquit, dixisti, quia vigilabo ego super verbo meo, ut faciam illud (Jer. I): Jr 1:12 Bene vidisti. The Lord commends Jeremiah's seeing, and the printed text has him commend his saying. Plate p. 586 and B p. 584 confirm dixisti. [var: Vulg. Jr 1:12 Bene vidisti, …] fired. (illud: see column note and TEI patch.)
  • 1160A Si quis vobis aliud praedicaverit quam quod a nobis accepistis, anathema sit (Gal. I): Gal 1:9 evangelizaverit praeter id quod accepistis. Wording, not sense; Wolbero's gloss runs on his own verb (haeretici aliud praedicaverunt). No marker.
  • 1160C anima justi sedes est Dei (Sap. VII): not in Wisdom 7 (the patristic dictum is anima justi sedes est sapientiae). Allusion under Migne's locator; no marker.
  • 1160C–D A cubilibus leonum, a montibus pardorum (Ct 4:8 de … de); A cubilibus … leonum et montibus pardorum: prepositions only, no marker.
  • 1161A VERS. 9: agrees. 1161B Unum oculorum et unum crinis colli?, 1161C–1162A In uno … crine colli, in uno oculorum: paraphrase and fragments.
  • 1161D Deus homo factus. and 1161D fides autem non habet meritum, cui humana ratio praebet experimentum (Gregory, Hom. in Ev. 26): not scripture.
  • 1162A Rom 10:8: agrees. daemones enim credunt et contremiscunt (Jac. II): Jac 2:19; agrees.
  • 1162B Vulnerasti eor meum …: our file only; the plate prints cor (TEI patch).
  • 1162C Homo sanctus in sapientia manet sicut sol, stultus autem ut luna mutatur (Eccli. XXVII): Sir 27:12 nam stultus sicut luna. No marker.
  • 1162C ibant in directum et uno itinere gradiebantur, et non declinabant ad dexteram, neque ad sinistram (I Reg. VI.): 1 Rg 6:12 abridged (subject and place names dropped). No marker.
  • 1162D unumquodque ante faciem suam gradiebatur: Ez 1:9; agrees.
  • 1162D ea quae retro sunt obliviscens, et ad ea quae sunt ante se extendens (Philip. III): Phil 3:13 quae quidem retro sunt obliviscens, ad ea vero quae sunt priora, extendens meipsum. Paraphrase, same sense; no marker.
  • 1163A ambulabat extenso collo et erecta cervice (Isa. III): Is 3:16 ambulaverunt extento collo, adapted to Wolbero's singular reproba mens with erecta cervice added. Adaptation, no marker.
  • 1163A dura cervice et indomabili corde (Act. VII.): Act 7:51 Dura cervice, et incircumcisis cordibus et auribus. "Untameable heart" for "uncircumcised hearts and ears" is a different image, under a locator presenting it as the text. Plate p. 588 confirms. [var: Vulg. Act 7:51 …] fired, set inside the italic run after the locator, where the Latin's one italic span runs on into In uno.
  • 1163A VERS. 10: agrees.
  • 1164B Intravit mors per fenestras vestras (Jer. IX): Jr 9:21 ascendit mors per fenestras nostras. Both the verb and the person differ, and the gloss turns on the person: the prophet peccatores arguit, and vestras is what makes it a reproof. Plate p. 588 confirms. [var: Vulg. Jr 9:21 …] fired.
  • 1164D Qui ignorat, ignorabitur (I Cor. XIV): 1 Cor 14:38 Si quis autem ignorat; no marker. cupiditas, quae est radix omnium malorum (I Tim. VI): 1 Tim 6:10 re-ordered; no marker.
  • 1165B ubera vino … pulchriora, 1167A odor super omnia aromata: fragments.
  • 1166B sine fide impossibile est placere Deo (Hebr. XI): Hbr 11:6; agrees.
  • 1167D Beatus qui custodit vestimenta sua … (Apoc. XVI): Apc 16:15 omits vigilat, et. Omission, no marker.
  • 1168A Omni tempore vestimenta tua sint candida (Eccle. IX): Ecl 9:8 re-ordered; Wolbero introduces it as in proverbiis Salomonis, carried ("in the Proverbs of Solomon").
  • 1169B Nobis autem ea revelavit Deus per Spiritum suum (I Cor. II): 1 Cor 2:10, ea added. No marker.
  • 1169B Per me si quis introierit salvabitur (Joan. X): Jo 10:9; agrees. 1169C Qui non intrat per ostium, sed ascendit aliunde, ille fur est et latro (ibid.): Jo 10:1 without in ovile ovium; no marker.
  • 1169C Cur non perpenditur … non arbitror. is Gregory (the simony letters), not scripture.
  • ✓ spans checked at the plate and against the file: Ct 4:3 (all fragments), Ex 21:24, Jac 4:6, 1 Pt 5:5, Ps 4:5, Rom 8:26, Ct 4:6, Ct 4:8 re-quotations, Ct 4:9, Ct 4:10, Ct 4:11 (lemma and fragments), Ct 4:12 all agree.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Kept from the peers: VERSE n.-- · Cologne numbers verbatim (including 145) · vitta coccinea = scarlet fillet · fragmen mali punici = a piece of a pomegranate · absque eo quod intrinsecus latet = besides that which lieth hid within · eloquium = speech · Libanus … candidatio = Libanus … whitening · praedicatores = preachers · fortes = valiant men · thou/you by number (the bride and the reader addressed in the singular are "thou") · cypri = of Cyprus (the majority peer form).
  • sponsus/sponsa = bridegroom/bride, capitalized only where Migne capitalizes (1158A mea Sponsa = my Bride), per the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-09-25. soror mea sponsa = my sister, bride. dilectus / dilecta = the beloved.
  • sacra / sancta Scriptura, and sanctae Scripturae plural = Holy Scripture / the Holy Scriptures (Pattern 3). No divina Scriptura occurs in this range.
  • Introduced here: propugnacula = bulwarks · clypei = bucklers · armatura fortium = armour of valiant men · pendent ex ea = hang from it · hinnuli capreae gemelli = fawns of a roe, twins · donec aspiret dies et inclinentur umbrae = till the day break and the shadows retire (the peers' form; the gloss nouns aspiratio / inclinatio = the breaking / the retiring) · mammae / ubera = paps / breasts (Wolbero distinguishes them, 1164A: girls' and women's) · unguenta = ointments · aromata = spices · favus distillans = a dropping honeycomb · thus = frankincense · nocturna avis = night bird · dens vigiliarum = tooth of watches · Amana, Sanir, Hermon as printed · hortus conclusus = a garden enclosed · fons signatus = a fountain sealed · praedicamentum / praeconium = proclamation · documenta = instructions · munimentum = fortification.

Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 591–600, each opened at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (591 = 1169/1170 … 600 = 1187/1188), text collated and the foot read full width in the same visit. Page 591 is shared with the previous stint and was collated from Porro propter sanctarum Scripturarum (foot of col. 1169) on; page 600 was collated to consideranti sublevatur at 1188B. Second witness raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecur195mign.pdf opened at pp. 590 (cols 1171/1172), 593 (1177/1178), 594 (1179/1180), 596 (1183/1184), 597 (1185/1186), corners read; it is the same printing (same sorts, same damage). Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0050.json.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 591–600; every foot read full width, and the text runs to the bottom margin on all ten pages. No [cn:] inserted in the Latin; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0050.tsv is header-only. Nothing unplaced, no sequence to check.

Lemma count: 197 italic spans, 0 guillemet spans, counted paragraph-bounded (0050 = 59, 0051 = 38, 0052 = 30, 0053 = 36, 0054 = 34; no paragraph with an odd delimiter count; the 138/172 heads are included, as in the inventory). Agrees with the 197 in 11512-lemmata-0050.txt. The English carries the same number per paragraph, markers aside.

Convention notes. sponsus/sponsa capitalized as Migne prints them (orchestrator, mid-run): his two sentence-initial Sponsus (1176C, 1183B) → "Bridegroom", every lowercase instance → "bridegroom"/"bride". sancta/sacra Scriptura(e) → "Holy Scripture(s)" (Pattern 3); divina Scriptura → "divine Scripture" as in the peer ranges. emissiones → "sendings forth" throughout, to keep the play on emittunt (1171C) and the recurring noun; fistula "sweet cane"; Cyprus/cypri "cypress" (the gloss treats it as a tree and interprets the name moeror); amica "love"; immaculata "undefiled"; cincinni "locks"; foramen "opening"; probatissima "most approved"; aromata "spices"; impetus "rush"; dilectus/dilecta "beloved"; charissimi "dearest"; soror mea sponsa "my sister, bride" (peer form).

Column notes

  • 1170A — Et dabitur liber scienti litteras et dicetur ei: Lege, etrespondebit: plate p. 591 sets etrespondebit run together (no space); our Latin agrees. [sic: etrespondebit] inside the italic lemma (no nested italics), sense "and he shall answer" rendered beside it.
  • 1171A — Porro praedicatio, 165 nihil confert: the comma after praedicatio is printed; carried ("Further, preaching, 165 confers nothing").
  • 1171C — Quomodo praedicabunt nisi mittantur (Rom. x)? officii gratiam a Deo consequitur. Plate p. 592 confirms: a question mark after the locator, then a lower-case clause with no subject (the sense wants something like "he who is sent obtains …"). Something may have dropped; rendered as printed, the implicit subject given as "he", lower case kept.
  • 1171C — tanta divinatione providentia custoditi: plate confirms; two ablatives side by side, no copula. Probably divina. Rendered literally ("kept by so great a divination providence"); crux only.
  • 1171D — quae cum permultimodas orationum species universitatem virtutum praetendentes infra enumerentur: plate confirms orationum ("prayers"); the list that follows (1172B) is of aromatic plants/virtues, so rationum or similar may be meant. Rendered "kinds of prayers"; crux only.
  • 1172C — ut mundi hujus transitoria contemnet gaudia: future indicative after ut; plate confirms. Rendered "that it will despise"; crux only.
  • 1172D — dum humilitatis virtu e durae et superbae carnis extollentia frangitur: plate p. 592 (and scan B p. 590) shows a gap where the t should stand. Broken sort, carried: [sic: virtu e] with "virtue" beside it.
  • 1172D — Per fistulam, autem quae, ut diximus: comma after fistulam, none after autem; carried as "By the sweet cane, which however, as we have said, …".
  • 1174D — concipiunt et proferunt omnia quae eorum profectus est ecclesiasticae disciplinae regula et status: plural relative with singular est; does not construe cleanly. Plate p. 593 confirms. Rendered literally ("all the things which their progress is, the rule and state of ecclesiastical discipline"); crux only.
  • 1174D — nullum bonorum operum fructum ferre valebit ends its paragraph with no stop on the plate; the English carries no stop.
  • 1175A — our Latin aqua rum; plate p. 594 prints aqua-|rum (line break): one word, aquarum. TEI patch; English "a well of living waters", no marker.
  • 1175A — quae exipsius Ecclesiae emanat fonte: set fused on the plate (p. 594, normal spacing either side). [sic: exipsius], sense "from the fountain of the Church itself" rendered.
  • 1175C — verae rationis et eloquentia impetu: nominative/ablative where the construction wants eloquentiae; plate confirms. English "by the rush of true reason and eloquence" has no case exponent; crux only.
  • 1176C — captivitas enim nostrae quae torporis frigore … remanserat: plate p. 594 confirms nostrae against feminine nominative captivitas. A real form with no slot, and "our captivity" would hide the disagreement (Pattern 12 test 2): [sic: nostrae], sense "our" rendered.
  • 1176D — the re-quotation et fluent aromata illius (future) against the VERS. 16 lemma's fluant; plate confirms both. Rendered "shall flow" here, "let … flow" in the lemma. No marker (Wolbero's gloss form).
  • 1177A — 172 CAPUT V.: the Cologne page number 172 is printed again with the head (after 171 at 1176C) and 173 follows at 1177C; plate p. 595 confirms. Carried verbatim.
  • 1177D — ipsi quidem quasi bona seges ad vitam assumuntur, ut reprobi quasi remanens stipula … relinquuntur: ut where et/sed is wanted; plate confirms. Rendered "as"; crux only.
  • 1178C — Sicut enim uni corum, quem ad praedicationem erudiebat: plate p. 595 and scan B p. 593 both corum (same sort). Non-word for eorum. [sic: corum], sense "of them".
  • 1179B — our Latin eflicit; plate p. 596 prints effi-|cit (line break, ffi ligature). TEI patch; English "makes", no marker.
  • 1179C — Comestio enim ista et bibitio divinorum praeceptorum 175 plena perceptio: no verb printed. English carries none ("For this eating and drinking, the 175 full perception …").
  • 1179D — inebriata fuerat . qualem effectum …: plate p. 596 (and scan B p. 594) prints a full stop, then lower-case qualem; our Latin has a comma. English follows the plate (stop, lower-case "what"). TEI patch (punctuation).
  • 1180B — ut uno eodemque tempore in uno eodemque 176 ab omnino non esse, aut simul esse debeant: plate p. 596 confirms ab. The same formula eight lines earlier (1180A) prints aut non esse, aut simul esse, so ab stands for aut. ab has no grammatical slot: [sic: ab], with "either" rendered beside it so the disjunction survives (7a⁗).
  • 1180C — quia contemplationi inhaerere et dormire mundo, et vigilare Deo, et nisi …: no finite verb in the quia clause; rendered verbless as printed.
  • 1181A — sponsus dixerat de suis praedicationibus, quod tanta eos … replesset: praedicationibus ("preachings") with masculine eos, which the sense (and 1181A praedicatorum tuorum) refers to preachers. Plate confirms. Rendered "his preachings … filled them"; crux only.
  • 1181B — Sir 15:3 turned into the bride's speech (cibavit me … potavit for Cibabit illum … potabit illum). Adaptation; no marker.
  • 1181C — perversis sententiis haereticorum … cadentibus, sed veritatis et rectae fidei calore calentibus: plate p. 597 confirms calentibus. As printed the heretics' opinions are "warm with the heat of truth and of right faith", the opposite of the argument (their dew chills; the faithful a veritatis amore frigidi efficiuntur). A real word that makes the sentence assert what the author argues against: [cj: calentibus; read carentibus, "lacking"] (Pattern 18).
  • 1181D — Soror, inquit, mea, amica mea …: quid sibi vult istarum quatuor appellationum probatio? … minime.: probatio rendered "proving" (perhaps appellatio/approbatio); lower-case minime after the question mark carried ("by no means.").
  • 1182A — in sancti propositi persistant ordine et loco ends its paragraph with no stop on the plate; carried.
  • 1182B — porro qui non credit, nec sperat, nec diligit quod est 178 pro quo operetur: awkward but construable; rendered literally, all three negations carried.
  • 1183C — verae quippe signum amiticiae est: plate p. 598 and scan B p. 596 confirm. Probably amicitiae, but amiticia may be a medieval spelling; no [sic:] fired (a public claim), rendered "friendship"; crux only.
  • 1183C — qui non ex sanguinibus: plural rendered "bloods" as printed.
  • 1184A — Recta ergo diffinitio est vera innocentiae: vera agrees with diffinitio; rendered "the true definition of innocence". diffinitio is the medieval spelling (Pattern 9).
  • 1184A — qua Graece κατα αντιλεχιν, Latine ad verbum dicitur: plate p. 598 prints the Greek unaccented, as our Latin (read at zoom). Carried verbatim in the English; the Latin name ad verbum given in straight quotes with a gloss.
  • 1184B — ut ei insinuat: indicative in a purpose clause; plate confirms. Rendered "that he intimates to her".
  • 1184C — our Latin montis; plate p. 598 and scan B p. 596 both print m?ntis with an inked-in eye in the second letter, whose shape (and the crossbar visible on A) reads as e: mentis. munditiam mentis et corporis is the sense. English follows the plate ("cleanness of mind and of body"). TEI patch, flagged as a close read: if the patch pass disagrees, the English reverts to the twin with this crux.
  • 1185A — before VERS. 3 the plate (p. 599; scan B p. 597 identical) prints the re-quotation Exspoliari me tunica mea, etc., an infinitive; the VERS. 3 lemma just below prints Exspoliavi. Our Latin reads Exspoliavi at both. English follows the plate at the re-quotation ("To be stripped of my tunic, etc."). TEI patch.
  • 1185B — et a proposito meo decedere non totum arbitrarer: rendered "did not wholly purpose to depart from my resolve"; the negation is carried.
  • 1185C — Quid enim est manus ejus, quid foramen, quid venter ejus: the bride's venter meus in the lemma becomes venter ejus; rendered "his belly" as printed.
  • 1185C — quasi muto quodam et maceria perversitatis circumclusa: plate confirms muto ("mute"), probably muro ("wall", paired with maceria). Real word, rendered literally ("a certain mute, and a wall of perversity"); not a Pattern 18 site (no false assertion); crux only.
  • 1186A — our Latin refiert; plate p. 599 prints reficit. TEI patch; English "refreshes" either way.
  • 1186C — proximorum corda eadem gratia per praedicationem aperirem ends its paragraph with no stop on the plate; carried.
  • 1186D — Ct 8:6 dura sicut inferrus aemulatio: plate p. 599 confirms inferrus. Non-word for infernus. [sic: inferrus] inside the italic quotation, "hell" rendered.
  • 1187D — Ubi autem Spiritus Patri loquitur, loquitur et Filius: plate p. 600 confirms dative Patri; the quotation just before reads Spiritus Patris vestri. Rendered "where the Spirit speaks to the Father"; probably Patris. Not a negation or correlative; crux only.

Scripture: collation of every ⚠ span (7a″)

Every ⚠ span in 11512-lemmata-0050.txt, walked against sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt by chapter and verse, the plate read at each.

  • 1170A Et dabitur liber scienti litteras et dicetur ei: Lege, et respondebit: Non possum, signatus est enim (Isa. XXIX): a conflation of Is 29:12 (Et dabitur liber … diceturque ei: Lege) with Is 29:11 (scienti litteras … Non possum, signatus est enim). Against the verse that gives the frame, 29:12, the book is given to one who knows letters instead of one who knows them not. Plate p. 591 confirms scienti. [var:] fired.
  • 1170A signatus sigillis septem (Apoc. V): Apc 5:1 signatum, adjusted to agree with liber. No marker.
  • 1170A nisi Agnus qui occisus est, qui solus dignus inventus est solvere hunc librum, et aperire septem signacula ejus (Apoc. V): paraphrase of Apc 5:5, 5:9, 5:12, not a quotation. No marker.
  • 1170B–C Ps 26:1 + Ps 117:6–7: agree (the psalm title of 26:1 omitted).
  • 1170D usque in diem Domini … conservetur (I Thess. V): roman on the plate; an allusion to 1 Th 5:23. No marker.
  • 1171C Quomodo praedicabunt nisi mittantur (Rom. X): Rom 10:15 without vero. Agrees.
  • 1171C/1171D hortus conclusus et fons signatus, emissiones, paradisus, fructus pomorum: gloss re-quotations of Ct 4:12–13. No marker.
  • 1172A VERS. 14 Cypri cum nardo, nardus et crocus …: Ct 4:14 with the close of 4:13 (cypri cum nardo) prefixed. No marker. horti conclusi, fontis signati: genitive gloss forms of Ct 4:12.
  • 1172C quae est secundum Deum, quae poenitentiam in salutem stabilem operatur (II Cor. VII): 2 Cor 7:10 recast into a relative clause after tristitiam. No marker.
  • 1172C Miseri estote et lugete, risus vester … in moerorem (Jac. IV): Jac 4:9 without et plorate. Abridgment; no marker.
  • 1173A Cinerem tanquam panem manducabam (Psal. CI): Ps 101:10 without quia. Agrees.
  • 1173C Alii quidem per Spiritum datur sermo sapientiae … interpretatio sermonum (I Cor. XII): 1 Cor 12:8–10 without autem in 12:8 and without 12:9b alii gratia sanitatum in uno Spiritu, so the list of gifts drops the gift of healings. Abridgment of a list, not a changed wording; no marker, logged here.
  • 1173D qui carnem suam crucifixerunt cum vitiis et concupiscentiis (Gal. V): Gal 5:24 without Qui autem sunt Christi. Agrees.
  • 1175A puteus aqua rum viventium: our split (see TEI patches); Ct 4:15. No marker.
  • 1176A Surgit … aquilo, venit auster, fluunt aromata illius: indicative gloss forms of Ct 4:16. No marker.
  • 1176A arundo a vento agitata non esse memoratur (Matth. XI): roman allusion to Mt 11:7. No marker.
  • 1176B–C Ubi abundavit iniquitas, refrigescet charitas multorum (Matth. XXIV): Vulg. Mt 24:12 quoniam abundavit iniquitas. Plate p. 594 confirms Ubi (Rom 5:20 ubi … abundavit is the likely contamination). Cause turned into place. [var:] fired.
  • 1176C Sicut frigidam facit cisterna aquam suam … (Jer. VI): Jr 6:7 fecit; present for perfect, rendered "maketh". Tense only; no marker.
  • 1176D aquilo, et veni, auster, perfla hortum meum, et fluent aromata illius: Ct 4:16 fluant; see column note. No marker.
  • 1177A VERS. 1 (Ct 5:1): comedite et bibite, amici for comedite, amici, et bibite, and bibi vinum cum lacte meo without meum (the 1178B re-quotation has vinum meum). Order and pronoun only; no marker.
  • 1177B Si quis diligit me, mandata mea servabit … (Joan. XIV): Vulg. Jo 14:23 sermonem meum servabit (Jo 14:15/21 mandata mea is the likely source). Plate p. 595 confirms. [var:] fired.
  • 1177B dilectus in hortum suum, fructum pomorum suorum comedit: gloss forms of Ct 5:1. No marker.
  • 1177D Venite ad me omnes qui laboratis et onerati estis, et ego vos requiescere faciam (Matth. XI): Vulg. Mt 11:28 et ego reficiam vos. Plate confirms. The gloss (myrrh reaped as those who have laboured) turns on the rest. [var:] fired.
  • 1177D–1178A Venite, benedicti Patris mei, percipite regnum quod vobis paratum est ab origine mundi (Matth. XXV): Vulg. Mt 25:34 possidete paratum vobis regnum a constitutione mundi (Wolbero's is the Old Latin/liturgical form). Plate confirms. [var:] fired.
  • 1178B Veni in hortum meum, messui myrrham meam cum aromatibus meis: Ct 5:1 without soror mea, sponsa. Abridgment; no marker.
  • 1179A Responsum mollius frangit iram (Prov. XV): Vulg. Pr 15:1 Responsio mollis. Plate p. 596 confirms. A comparative in a gloss on gentle rebuke. [var:] fired.
  • 1179A–B favus cum melle, vinum cum lacte, favum cum melle suo comedit, vinum cum lacte suo bibit: gloss forms of Ct 5:1. No marker.
  • 1179B Comedite et bibite, amici, et inebriamini, charissimi: Ct 5:1, order as in the VERS. 1 lemma. No marker.
  • 1179C Inebriamur ab ubertate domus tuae (Psal. XXXV): Vulg. Ps 35:9 Inebriabuntur. Plate confirms. Person and tense changed ("we are" for "they shall be"), which the gloss uses. [var:] fired.
  • 1179D Etsi imperitus sermone, non tamen scientia (II Cor. II): 2 Cor 11:6 sed non scientia. Same sense; no marker. The locator II Cor. II is Migne's (the verse is 11:6); [n:] kept verbatim.
  • 1180A VERS. 2 (Ct 5:2): agrees (Vox dilecti mei pulsantis with no et issues). ⚠ only for the anchor split.
  • 1181B cibavit me pane vitae et intellectus, et aqua sapientiae salutaris potavit (Eccli. XV): Sir 15:3 Cibabit illum … potabit illum, turned into the bride's first person and the perfect. Adaptation to the speaker; no marker.
  • 1181C mei pleni sunt guttis noctium: gloss form of Ct 5:2. No marker.
  • 1182A Fides … per dilectionem operatur (Gal. V): Gal 5:6 per caritatem. Synonym; no marker.
  • 1183B–C quotquot receperunt eum … ex Deo nati sunt (Joan. I): Jo 1:12–13 without autem. Agrees.
  • 1183C Quicunque fecerit voluntatem Patris mei qui in coelis est … (Matth. XII): Mt 12:50 without enim. Agrees.
  • 1183C–D Jam non dicam vos servos, sed amicos, quia omnia quae audivi a Patre meo nota feci vobis (Joan. XV): Jo 15:15 abridged (the middle clause quia servus nescit … Vos autem dixi is dropped and quaecumque becomes quae). Abridgment; no marker.
  • 1184A Plenitudo legis, dilectio (Rom. XIII): Rom 13:10 cut to the definition the gloss wants. No marker.

  • 1185A VERS. 3 Exspoliavi me tunica mea …: Ct 5:3 (Clementine Expoliavi, spelling only). The re-quotation above it: see column note.
  • 1186C VERS. 5 Surrexi igitur ut aperirem …: Ct 5:5 with igitur added. No marker. meae distillaverunt myrrham (1186C gloss): distillaverunt for stillaverunt, compound of the same verb; no marker.
  • 1186D Ct 8:6 fortis est ut mors dilectio et dura sicut inferrus aemulatio?: et added, inferrus is a defect ([sic:], see column note); otherwise agrees. Jo 15:13 Majorem enim nemo charitatem habet quam ut animam suam ponat quis pro amicis suis: Vulg. Majorem hac dilectionem nemo habet, ut …. charitatem for dilectionem and quam for hac: same sense; no marker.
  • 1187A pleni sunt myrrha: gloss form of Ct 5:5. No marker.
  • 1187B VERS. 6 … Anima liquefacta est ut dilectus locutus est …: Ct 5:6 Anima mea liquefacta est, ut locutus est. mea omitted, dilectus supplied as subject. Same sense; no marker.
  • 1187B–C Domine, ostende mihi faciem tuam (Exod. XXXIII): Ex 33:13 ostende mihi faciem tuam with Domine prefixed (and Ex 33:18's request). No marker.
  • 1187C ille jam declinaverat atque transierat: gloss form of Ct 5:6. No marker.
  • 1187C Christus enim resurgens jam non moritur … (Rom. VI): Rom 6:9 without ex mortuis. Abridgment; no marker.
  • 1188A–B Accedet homo ad cor altum, et exaltabitur Deus (Psal. LXIII): Ps 63:7–8, spanning the verse break. Agrees.
  • 1188B cum elevarentur animalia de terra, elevabantur simul et rotae juxta ea (Ezech. I): roman paraphrase of Ez 1:19. No marker.

Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0055.json.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 600–609; every foot read full width, and the text runs to the bottom margin on all ten pages. No [cn:] inserted in the Latin; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0055.tsv is header-only. Nothing unplaced, no sequence to check.

Column notes

  • 1188B — quia, neque ipsa eum legendo perfecte cognoscere. neque praedicando aliis ostendere potui: plate p. 600 and scan B p. 598 print a full stop after cognoscere, with lower-case neque following; our Latin has a comma. English follows the plate ("by reading. nor show him"). TEI patch (punctuation). ipsa is feminine (the soul speaking); English "I myself" has no gender exponent.
  • 1188C — [n: (Ezech. I.)] is followed by Jam enim with no stop; the English likewise has none.
  • 1190B — filiae Jerusalem … causas tantae adjurationis percunctanter atque hoc modo dilectum alloquuntur: plate p. 601 and scan B p. 599 confirm masculine dilectum, though the daughters are addressing the bride (dilectam), whom the next verse calls o pulcherrima mulierum. English "the beloved" has no gender exponent, so the slip is invisible in the English; logged here (Pattern 9 class for rendering, but a real fact of the plate). No marker.
  • 1191B — ut pereunctantium animas … accendat: plate p. 602 is ambiguous between c and e; scan B p. 600 reads e; our Latin agrees. Non-word for percunctantium ("of those inquiring", cf. percunctanter 1190B). [sic: pereunctantium] with the sense "those inquiring" beside it. ⚠ A letter-level call on two copies of one printing; if a better witness shows percunctantium, withdraw the marker.
  • 1191B — decachordo … psalterio (Psal. XCI): Ps 91:4 in decachordo, psalterio; allusion in roman, no marker.
  • 1191B–C — tropologice, "tropologically". The Delta / four arithmetic (1+2+3+4 = 10) is rendered as printed.
  • 1192A — non dedignatus fieri carnis peccatum, crucisque maledictum [n: (Rom. VIII; Gal. III)] is followed by De qua with no stop; English likewise.
  • 1192B — unxit eum Deus oleo exsultationis prae participibus suis (Psal. XLIV): Ps 44:8 Vulg. oleo laetitiae, prae consortibus tuis; Migne's wording is that of Hbr 1:9 (oleo exultationis prae participibus tuis), turned to the third person. The Vulgate itself carries this form, so no [var:]; rendered "oil of exultation … partakers", not the Psalter's "gladness … fellows".
  • 1192B–C — non ad mensuram dat Deus Spiritum sanctum (Joan. III) and (Coloss II): these locators stand in roman parentheses inside the italic run, not as [n:] notes, in our Latin; kept identically. Coloss II has no stop in the Latin; kept.
  • 1193A — ut ita dictum, fit bonum bonorum: dictum (not dicam) rendered "so to speak"; construes loosely. Crux only.
  • 1194A — Quae sicut palmae elatae dicuntur, qui a fidelium mentes … sublevantur: plate p. 603 and scan B p. 601 set qui a with a word space. Split for quia. [sic: qui a] with "because" supplied beside it (7a⁗: it is the clause's conjunction).
  • 1194A — et ub spiritualem armaturam contra vitia atque peccata profitentur: plate and scan B confirm ub. Non-word; ob or ut both possible and neither construes cleanly with deponent profitentur. Carried as [sic: ub] with no sense supplied (more than one reading possible).
  • 1194B — dilectus in hac parte vinicolor: "wine-coloured", a real (rare) compound; glossed by Wolbero himself id est rubicundus. No marker.
  • 1194C — annuntiat · qui est quasi aquila: a stray raised point on scan A after annuntiat, not a stop; our Latin has nothing. No patch.
  • 1195C — naturalis necessitudo: A shows necessitado with a worn u; not claimed (letter-level, single copy). Our necessitudo rendered "natural necessity".
  • 1195B — quae quia bonarum malarumve rerum sunt perceptibiles, quaedam autem res …: the quia clause has no main clause; rendered as printed.
  • 1196A — quia et ad integritatem munditiamque vitae secundam dant praeceptum: plate p. 604 confirms secundam. Feminine accusative with no noun to agree with (praeceptum is neuter); probably secundum ("according to [purity of life]"). No grammatical slot: [sic: secundam] inside "give a … precept".
  • 1196C — Omnes enim qui in Christo pie volunt vivere, persecutionem patientur, (II Tim. III): the comma before the locator is printed; kept.
  • 1197B — primo loco est in incorporea creatura … secundo loco incorporea creatura rerum superiorum, quae sunt quaedam nutrimenta inferiorum: plate p. 605 confirms incorporea both times. The second member (the heavens, which nourish the lower things) wants corporea; rendered literally "incorporeal" both times. Crux only (not a negation or correlative).
  • 1197D — in vas honoris et divinis spectabile oculis: rendered "a vessel of honour and worthy to be looked upon".
  • 1198D — nec opus bonum sine castitate (GREGORIUS). » Quia ergo …: plate p. 605 prints a closing guillemet with no opening one anywhere on the page or in our range. Carried unopened in the English (Pattern 5). (GREGORIUS) is running text, not an [n:]; kept as printed.
  • 1199B — symphoniam illam διαπασον: Greek, unaccented, as printed; carried in Greek script.
  • 1199B — VERS. 15 prints Species ejus ut libani with a lower-case l (plate p. 606); the English writes "Libanus" (proper name; Pattern 9, no exponent). Elsewhere Migne prints Libani / Libanus.
  • 1199C — cum localis sit qui de loco ad locum movetur: localis / illocalis rendered "in a place / not in a place".
  • 1200B — regnum videlicet et secerdotium: plate p. 606 and scan B p. 604 confirm. Non-word for sacerdotium. [sic: secerdotium] with "the priesthood" beside it.
  • 1200C — in tota sua republica: the u is worn on both copies (sca / s:a); our sua stands. Nothing claimed.
  • 1201A — sat esse respondit [n: (Luc II)]: allusion to Lc 22:38 (Satis est); locator as printed (chapter II for XXII).
  • 1201C — in pace et tranquil itate hoc agat: plate p. 607 and scan B p. 605 set a word space inside the word. [sic: tranquil itate] with "tranquillity" beside it.
  • 1201D — quam suscitare oportet non sine aliquo labore Unde Paulus ad Timotheum: no stop after labore on A or B (the line ends in white space); our Latin has labore. Unde. English follows the plate ("labour Whence Paul"). TEI patch (punctuation).
  • 1203B — Adhuc nunc conventum quasi ad quemdam montem Dei, currit quotidie fidelis populus: accusative with no preposition or governing verb (ad conventum wanted). Plate p. 608 confirms. Rendered as printed ("Still now the assembly, as it were to a certain mountain of God, the faithful people runs daily"). Crux only.
  • 1203C–D — Hoc autem loco dilectus qui supra … inducitur, … sola corporis pulchritudine in corporis sui … splendet facie: the relative clause and the main verb are not joined; rendered as printed.
  • 1203D — Libani, qui interpretatur candidutio: plate p. 608 and scan B p. 606 both print candidutio; our Latin reads candidatio (the peers' candidatio, "whitening", 1151C). English "whitening" (the sense either way). No [sic:] fired, because the marker must match the Latin twin verbatim and our twin does not carry the defect; TEI patch filed below; once it lands, [sic: candidutio] may be added at this site.
  • 1204B — ut attento adjutori nihil dulcius in aure cordis videatur sonare: plate p. 608 confirms adjutori ("helper"). The context (in aure cordis) wants auditori ("hearer"). Rendered literally, "to the attentive helper". Crux only.
  • 1205A — secundum quod suae placet gratiae in singulo suo, membro disponere: comma printed between suo and membro; English keeps it ("in each, his member").
  • 1205A — habitus / dispositio / affectio / intentio et remissio: scholastic terms, rendered "bearing / disposition / affection / intensification and remission"; habitus = "bearing" throughout (the gloss plays on habitum per singula membra expressi, the bride's description of his bearing).
  • 1205B — custodit me in hujusmodi scientiam: accusative after in; rendered "guards me into knowledge of this kind".
  • 1205B — Dicitur autem amicus quasi animi custos: an etymology; both Latin words kept in italic with the English beside them ("he is called amicus, friend, as it were animi custos, guardian of the mind").
  • 1205C — Si Deus est animus, nobis ut carmina dicunt, / Hic tibi praecipue sit pura mente colendus: Disticha Catonis I.1 (quidam sapiens … ad filium), lineated on the plate as two verse lines; kept as two lines (Pattern 8a). Not scripture, no collation.
  • 1205C — Auribus quippe per insitam sibi vim rationis et intellectus … Deum in se retinens, Deus est: plate p. 609 and scan B p. 607 confirm Auribus. The sentence needs animus as subject (Animus quippe), which the preceding lines supply; "by the ears" has no construction. Rendered literally ("For by the ears … it is God"). Crux only.
  • 1205D — et hoc in dilecta, dilecti hujus magnam pulchritudinem spiritus constituisse: accusative-and-infinitive with no governing verb or subject accusative (a participle such as videntes seems lost). Rendered as closely as the words allow ("and this, in the beloved, to have established the great beauty of spirit of this beloved"). Crux only.
  • 1205D — valeant ad ipisci: plate and scan B set a word space. [sic: ad ipisci] with "attain" beside it.
  • 1206A — apud quem non est transmutatio nec vicissitudinis obumbratio? (Jac. II.): Jac 1:17, roman, locator as printed (chapter II for I).
  • 1206B — Dic, inquiunt, nobis: the first Dic is roman on the plate (not a span), the second (Dic, hoc est) italic; kept.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Following the peers: dilectus = beloved (also dilecta, "the beloved", the English having no gender); filiae Jerusalem = daughters of Jerusalem (no "O" unless Migne prints o); o pulcherrima mulierum = O most beautiful among women; custodes = watchmen; praeconia = proclamations; sorores dilectissimae = most beloved sisters, addressed as "you"; the bride and the daughters address each other thou/you by number (Pattern 17); Scriptura divina / sancta / sacra = Holy Scripture (Pattern 3, per the orchestrator's note); VERSE n.-- ; Cologne numbers (185–205, and the running 172 in every head) verbatim.
  • ⚠ Scriptura check for the peers: sancta / divina Scriptura are "Holy Scripture(s)" throughout 0055–0059.
  • Introduced here: candidus et rubicundus = white and ruddy; candor / rubor = whiteness / ruddiness; electus ex millibus = chosen out of thousands; decachordum psalterium = psaltery of ten strings, decachordum alone = ten-stringed instrument; comae … elatae palmarum = locks … high branches of palm trees; areolae aromatum … a pigmentariis = beds of spices … perfumers (pigmenta = perfumes); myrrha prima = choice myrrh; manus tornatiles = hands turned (tornatura = turning, torno = the lathe); venter eburneus = belly of ivory; crura … columnae marmoreae … bases aureae = legs … pillars of marble … bases of gold; species = form; guttur = throat; totus desiderabilis = all lovely; habitus = bearing; regnum et sacerdotium = the kingship and the priesthood.
  • Heads: \172\ CAPUT V. (cont.) = 172 CHAPTER V. (cont.) (after the peers' 138 CHAPTER IV.).

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)

Columns 1207A–1226A (Book III, CAPUT VI, Ct 6:1 through Ct 6:12 and the moral reading of the two chariots). Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 610–619, each opened at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (610 = 1207/1208 … 619 = 1225/1226), the text collated and the foot read full width in the same visit. Page 619 is shared with the next stint and was read to damnans et occidens (1226A). Second witness raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecur195mign.pdf opened at 600 dpi at pp. 609, 610, 611, 612, 614, 615, 617 (corners read); it is the same printing. Following the orchestrator's mid-run ruling, no [sic:] and no TEI patch here turns on c/e or another ink-filled letter read on scan A alone. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0060.json.

Migne's foot notes: one, on p. 613: (3) Deest aliquid. set under the left column. Its key is printed in the text at 1213A, after qui numerus perfecti, qui imperfecti demonstrant. That is not the line above the note (dubia est aestimatio, dum non omnes ad electorum, 1213D), which is only where the note happens to sit. Placed in the Latin as [cn: 3 | Deest aliquid.] (chunk 0061); nothing in the English. data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0060.tsv has one row. Numbering: no (1) or (2) appears anywhere on p. 613, and no note appears on any other page of 610–619. So the key is not restarting per page here. Either the numbering runs on through the work, with (1) and (2) somewhere in the 0045–0059 range (the 0050–0054 range reports none), or Migne began at (3). Not settled from this range; flagged for the orchestrator.

Lemma count: 185 marked spans (all italic, 0 guillemets), counted paragraph-bounded with no odd delimiter count in any paragraph: 0060 = 41, 0061 = 37, 0062 = 45, 0063 = 44, 0064 = 18. Agrees with the 185 in 11512-lemmata-0060.txt. The English carries the same number per paragraph once markers are set aside, except 0062 para. 1, where the TEI's italic i inside misericord i am is a markup artifact (see TEI patches) and is not reproduced.

Convention notes. VERS. → VERSE. The one lowercase sponsi (1215A) → "bridegroom"; sponsam (1222D) → "bride". sancta/sacra Scriptura → "Holy Scripture" (1207C, 1211B); divinae et humanae Scripturae (1215A) is not the canon as a name ("divine and human Scripture"). dilectus/dilecta "beloved"; amica "love"; praepositi "those set over", praelati "prelates"; acervus testimonii "heap of testimony"; areola "little bed" (the gloss turns on the diminutive); aurora "dawn" in lemma and gloss alike, since the gloss expounds the word; Sunamitis kept in Latin form in the English, because the gloss interprets the name (id est captiva, 1222A); quadriga "chariot"; the Greek music terms carried verbatim as printed (διαπασον, ἐπιτριτα, διατέσσερον, δια τεσσερον). Bold 1630 page numbers kept verbatim, including the spaced 206 and 213.

Column notes

  • 1207A — sua descendit ends its paragraph with no stop on the plate (as TEI); the English carries none.
  • 1207A–B — the plate prints quia dilectus meus descendit in hortum suum with no comma after dilectus; our Latin has dilectus, meus. English follows the plate. TEI patch.
  • 1207B — Quia scire desideratis … ecce dico tibi: plural desideratis, then singular tibi. Rendered "you desire … I tell thee" (Pattern 17).
  • 1208B — qui nullum unquam recipit augmentum vel detrimentum. Cum semper …: the plate sets a full stop before Cum, so a subordinate clause stands alone. Carried.
  • 1209A — una terrestris, altera spiritualis, tertia cœlestus: plate p. 611 and scan B p. 609 (600 dpi) both print cœlestus; our Latin reads coelestis. English "heavenly", no marker, because a [sic:] must match the Latin twin, which will not carry the form until the patch lands. TEI patch. Once the patch is applied, this site owes a [sic: coelestus].
  • 1209A — quasi hostis amarissimi: -is for -es plural; attested form (Pattern 9). "most bitter enemies".
  • 1209A/1209C — extremorum then extremarum for the same "extremes"; no English exponent (Pattern 9).
  • 1212A — qui dum … nitens, miraculis quoque stupendis fulgent: singular participle with a plural relative; rendered "while shining".
  • 1212A — quae pura gloria ejus erit in his quae nondum apparent?: plate confirms pura. The comparison (si tanta est … gloria in his quae apparent) wants quanta. Real word; rendered literally ("what pure glory"). Not a negation or correlative; crux only.
  • 1212C — nam cum sancta Ecclesia … diversa videntur: the plate's vide?tur is damaged at the n on both scans; our videntur (plural after singular Ecclesia) followed. "seem".
  • 1213A — Denarius vero qui Decalogum legis insinuat, quia ex septenario et trinario constat, septiformem Spiritus sancti gratiam et fidem sanctae Trinitatis confessio, qui numerus perfecti, qui imperfecti demonstrant (3). The sentence has no main verb for Denarius, the accusatives gratiam … fidem hang, confessio is a stranded nominative, and singular numerus takes plural demonstrant. Migne's own note (3), Deest aliquid, is keyed here: he judged that something has fallen out. Rendered literally, the hanging nominative kept ("and the faith of the holy Trinity confession, which numbers show who are perfect, who imperfect"). No marker: the note is his, on the Latin side (Pattern 19).
  • 1213B — begninitas autem ejus: plate p. 613 prints begninitas clearly (b-e-g-n, no ink-filled letter in question). Non-word for benignitas. [sic: begninitas] with "kindness" beside it.
  • 1213C — qui perfecte praecepta divina non observarent, nisi …: negation and nisi both carried ("would not perfectly observe … unless").
  • 1213D — nondum tamen jus illud divinum adepti sunt: masculine adepti after feminine animae; no English exponent (Pattern 9).
  • 1214A — Migne's key (3) is at 1213A, not at ad electorum (1213D/1214A) where the brief expected it; the note is merely set at the foot beneath that line.
  • 1214B — our Latin misericord i am; plate p. 613 prints misericordiam whole, in roman, in the ordinary line. The italic i is a TEI markup artifact. English "mercy". TEI patch (markup).
  • 1214D — licet abusive et non proprie dicatur fides de praesentibus: negation carried.
  • 1215C — omnium Christianonorum animus: plate p. 614 sets Christiano-|norum across a line break; scan B p. 612 the same. A real non-word on the plate, so it is not our hyphenation artifact: the extra syllable is printed. [sic: Christianonorum], "Christians" beside it.
  • 1215C — ut quasi quidam suavis concentus … consonantia … quibus … commovetur et componitur cultus: the ut clause has no verb of its own; rendered as printed.
  • 1215D — our Latin neeessariam; plate p. 614 and scan B p. 612 both show ink-filled letters where c/e stand. Not decidable at the plate, so per the orchestrator's ruling no [sic:] and no patch. English "necessary"; crux only.
  • 1216A — sanctae … approbant … agnoscunt are set with damaged sorts on scan A (read sapctae, apprcbant, agn.scunt); the damage is not a reading. TEI followed.
  • 1216C — Cur enim hic non attendatur … aciei ordinatae fortitudo. A question closed with a full stop; carried.
  • 1217A — ita et sancti Sancto sanctorum comparati non sunt: "are not", as printed (the comparison is left without its predicate, i.e. they "are not [anything]"); the negation is carried.
  • 1217A — cumque … recedens … defecerunt: singular participle with plural verb; rendered with the plural.
  • 1218A — et per charitatem quae perfecta foras mittit timorem, in summorum spirituum, qui seraphim, id est ardentes nuncupantur, transeuntes vero soli vicinius conjunguntur: in with a genitive and transeuntes vero with nothing for vero to answer. Does not construe; plate confirms. Rendered as closely as the words allow ("in of the highest spirits … but passing over are joined more nearly to the sun"); probably something like in ordinem has dropped.
  • 1219A — Et mirabatur, haud dubium quin Jesus: the gloss inserted into the quotation; carried.
  • 1219C — ut cognoscerem utrum aliquae fructum fidei et injustitiae fecissent: plate p. 616 prints injustitiae. As printed, the Lord came to see whether any had borne fruit "of faith and of injustice"; Wolbero's own gloss four lines later reads humiles fructum justitiae habentes (1220B). A real word that makes the sentence say the opposite of the argument: [cj: injustitiae; read justitiae, "of justice"] (Pattern 18).
  • 1219C — aliquae (feminine) for the Jews: kept, no English exponent.
  • 1220A — fecit labruscas (Isa. v). nam postquam gentes crediderunt: full stop then lower-case nam; carried.
  • 1220C — our Latin qnoque; plate p. 616 prints quo ue with the second q damaged, scan B p. 614 the same. The letter is not n on either copy. TEI patch to quoque; English "also".
  • 1220C — si floruissent vineae: scan A floruissent, scan B flaruissent (worn o). The TEI's floruissent is the reading; no marker.
  • 1221A — concedendum esse quid proposuerint: quid for quod; rendered "what they have proposed".
  • 1221B — quia gratiam Evangeiii minime adhuc intellexi: plate p. 617 prints Evangeiii; scan B p. 615 prints the same with the e/c ink-filled but the triple i clear. The defect does not rest on the e: it is the iii. [sic: Evangeiii] with "of the Gospel" beside it.
  • 1221C–D — the lemma prints Aminadab; the gloss prints Aminabad three times (ejusdem Aminabad, iste Aminabad, Quadriga … Aminabad), plate confirmed. A proper name, carried as printed each time; no marker.
  • 1223C — labens flegma: plate p. 618 confirms labens ("slipping") where the three parallel clauses have habens. Real word, rendered literally ("slipping phlegm"); crux only.
  • 1223C — colera habens rubea quae abundant; melancolica, id est colera nigra, quae sunt: the neuter plurals kept; "red choler which abound".
  • 1224B — the poet's line is introduced by printed leader dots (. . .), carried; the verse Hi metuunt, cupiunt, gaudentque, dolentque (Virgil, Aen. VI 733, reworded) and the four short lines Gaudia pelle … (Boethius, Cons. I m. 7) are lineated as the plate lineates them. Not scripture.
  • 1225A — ut omnem illum naturalem carnis impetum … frangunt: indicative after ut, rendered "that they break".
  • 1225C — delectatione carnis consensu spiritus: no comma between the two members on the plate; carried.
  • 1225C — Nam sicut quidam dici: plate p. 619 prints dici followed by the worn stump of a t (scan B p. 617 the same). TEI patch to dicit; English "says".
  • 1225D — ab utroque parte: gender mismatch (Pattern 9).
