Gloss abridgment, no marker. Note the name: Sunamiti here (and Sunamitis in 0063–0064) against Sulamite / Sulamitis in the VERS. 1 lemma and gloss (1226D, 1227A); both kept as printed ("the Sunamite" / "the Sulamite").
  • 1226C — quo alibi dicit. Faciamus hominem: plate p. 619 and scan B p. 617 print a full stop after dicit; our Latin has none. English follows the plate ("by which he says elsewhere. Let us make man …"). TEI patch. (Gen. III) is Gn 1:26; locator as printed.
  • 1226C — in sancta Trinitate, — the chapter ends on a comma on both copies (our Latin a full stop). English ends "in the holy Trinity," as printed. TEI patch.
  • 1226D — VERS. 1 agrees with Ct 7:1 (juncturae femorum tuorum); rendered "the joints of thy thighs".
  • 1227A — Revertimini ad me, et ego revertar ad vos. (Malach. V) — Mal 3:7 et revertar ad vos (no ego); locator as printed (Malachi has four chapters). Same sense; no marker.
  • 1227C — Junctura feminum tuarum sicut monilia quae fabricata sunt manu artificis (plate p. 620 confirms): against Ct 7:1 Juncturae femorum tuorum, singular Junctura and the form feminum (from femen), with feminine tuarum. The gloss turns on the word: at 1228C Wolbero says bene juncturam non femorum, sed feminum dixit (plate prints femorum roman, feminum italic). [var:] fired at the lemma. At 1228C the two Latin words are kept in the English (roman femorum, italic feminum, as printed) because the gloss IS the word (the murenulae rule); elsewhere feminum is "thighs". tuarum (feminine for neuter) has no English exponent (Pattern 9).
  • 1228B — omnia … quaecunque voluit fecit (Psal. XIII) — Ps 113B:3 / 134:6 omnia quaecumque voluit fecit; locator as printed. Agrees.
  • 1228D — debriantur, attested medieval debriare ("make drunk"); no marker. respiciens, ut: the mark after respiciens is a comma or a stop, undecidable on either copy; our comma kept, nothing claimed.
  • 1228C — VERS. 2 Umbilicus tuus sicut crater tornatilis; nunquam indigens poculis — Ct 7:2 has no sicut. "like a turned bowl"; same sense, no marker.
  • 1229A — finis praecepti charitas ad justitiam omni credenti (I Tim. I): a conflation of 1 Tim 1:5 (Finis autem praecepti est caritas) with Rom 10:4 (Finis enim legis Christus, ad justitiam omni credenti). Rendered as printed; conflation, no marker. perfecta quippe charitas foras mittit timorem, quia timor non est in charitate (I Joan. IV) — 1 Jo 4:18 rearranged; no marker.
  • 1229B — verbum consummatum quod fecit Dominus super terram (Isa. X; Rom. IX): roman, not a span; allusion to Is 10:23 / Rom 9:28. Qui sitit veniat ad me et bibat, et de ventre ejus fluent aquae vivae (Joan. VII) — Jo 7:37–38 compressed (Si quis sitit … Qui credit in me … flumina de ventre ejus fluent aquae vivae); rendered as printed, no marker.
  • 1229C — triticum suum allusion to Mt 3:12.
  • 1230A — in spi-|ritualis exercitii robur: plate p. 621 prints spiritualis across the line break; our Latin spritualis dropped the i. English "spiritual". TEI patch.
  • 1230A–B — Omnia possum … ✓ Phlp 4:13; fortis est ut mors dilectio ✓ Ct 8:6; de virtute in virtutem ✓ Ps 83:8; nulla sanctos separat tribulatio (Rom. VIII) roman allusion.
  • 1230B — Qui dicit se diligere Deum et non diligit fratrem suum mendax est (I Joan IV) — 1 Jo 4:20 Si quis dixerit quoniam diligo Deum, et fratrem suum oderit, mendax est, paraphrased; negation carried ("loveth not"). No marker. Utrumque enim alterius et probativum: "each is also a proof of the other".
  • 1230D–1231A — eloquia Domini casta et praeceptum Domini lucidum, illuminans oculos (Psal. XVIII): Ps 11:7 eloquia Domini, eloquia casta conflated with Ps 18:9. No marker.
  • 1231A — turris nebulosa (Mich. IV) — Mic 4:8 turris gregis nebulosa; fragment, no marker.
  • 1231C — ex altera dil?tionis cingitur moerore: plate p. 622 and scan B p. 620 (also re-rendered at 600 dpi) print a seven-letter word with no c: diletionis or dilationis; the vowel after dil is ink-filled on A and undecidable on B. Our Latin has dilectionis, which the sense requires (the two girdles: of penance and of love, cf. ex desiderio aeternorum bonorum 1231B). Not rendered from the plate, because the plate reading cannot be fixed: if dilationis it is a real word ("of delay"), if diletionis a non-word. English keeps "love" from our Latin. ⚑ For the orchestrator: a TEI patch here needs a better witness to decide a/e; either way the c is absent from Migne.
  • 1231C — ut et ad priora vitia … non defluat, et ad … virtutum studia … se fortiter stringat: et … et rendered "both … and", the negation carried.
  • 1232B — Poenitentiam agite, appropinquabit enim regnum coelorum (Matth. III) — Mt 3:2 appropinquavit. Plate p. 622 prints appropinquabit. Rendered "will draw near" as printed. Tense only; no marker (following the peers' 1202B facit/fecit decision, for one voice within the work).
  • 1232C — Nimis honorati sunt amici tui, Deus — Ps 138:17 honorificati; same sense, no marker. Dinumerabo … (ibid.) is followed by Hujus with no stop; English likewise.
  • 1232D — sine qua impossibile est placere Deo (Hebr. XI) roman allusion.
  • 1233B — candidationem: "whitening", after the peers' candidatio.
  • 1233C — the verse head: plate p. 623 and scan B p. 621 print VERS. 5.; our Latin has VERS. 3. English "VERSE 5." following the plate. TEI patch. (Migne's other numbers in the range agree with our Latin: 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 12.)
  • 1233C — VERSE 5 Caput tuum sicut Carmelus … juncta canalibus — Ct 7:5 ut Carmelus … vincta canalibus. The gloss (1233D, 1234B) builds on junguntur … canalibus, so juncta carries sense: [var: Vulg. vincta canalibus, "bound in the channels"] at the verse head only; the re-quotations at 1233D and 1234A take no second marker.
  • 1233D — quae sicut purpura regis junct? canalibus dicuntur: plate p. 623 and scan B p. 621 print canalibus with no r; our Latin carnalibus is a digitization error. English "joined to the channels". TEI patch (carnalibus → canalibus). The last letter of juncta here looks like e on both copies (ink-filled on A, italic); not claimed, and no patch proposed for it.
  • 1234A — hanc orationis particulam: "this little portion of the speech".
  • 1234C — Igitur quia caput viri Deus (I Cor. II): 1 Cor 11:3 omnis viri caput Christus est … caput vero Christi, Deus. Migne prints Deus as the head of man; confirmed on plate p. 623. Material (the head changes person), so [var: Vulg. omnis viri caput Christus est, …]. Locator as printed (II for XI).
  • 1234C — decem partibus quasi decem chordis … decachordo: "ten strings … ten-stringed instrument", after the peers.
  • 1234D — laudis ejus praeconia a summo corporis praeconio, hoc est a capite: plate confirms praeconio; the sense wants a word like membro ("from the highest member of the body, that is, from the head"). Real word, rendered literally ("from the highest proclamation of the body"). Crux only (not a negation or correlative).
  • 1234D — ut ostendat se inferius positam, semper se respicere debere ad vitam: plate p. 623 and scan B p. 621 have no comma after respicere; our Latin has one. English "that she, set lower, always ought to look back toward …". TEI patch (punctuation). e regione prints on A.
  • 1235A — ex habitu mentis bone et honeste compositorum: plate p. 624 and scan B p. 622 both print bone. A real form (vocative of bonus) with no grammatical slot where the adverb bene is required beside honeste. [sic: bone] with the sense "well" beside it.
  • 1235A — nam cum istae ista, ille illa, alius alia virtute resplendeat: istae for iste (confirmed on the plate), an ae/e spelling of the period; rendered "this one … that one … another" with no marker (Pattern 9).
  • 1235A — VERS. 6 charissima = carissima (spelling). 1235B alii detur sermo sapientiae … (I Cor. XII): 1 Cor 12:8–10 compressed, with subjunctive detur; rendered "may be given". No marker.
  • 1235D — VERS. 8 apprehendam fructum ejus … sicut odor malorum — Ct 7:8 fructus ejus … sicut malorum. Singular fruit (the gloss builds on the singular: fructus hujus passionis) and an added odor; English "its fruit … like the odour of apples". No marker (English "fruit" does not mark number; the odor adds nothing).
  • 1236A — quia per signi praevaricationem occubuisti: plate confirms signi ("of the sign"), against the answering per ligni oppositionem a few lines on; ligni ("of the tree") is the likely reading. Real word, rendered literally ("through the transgression of the sign"). Crux only.
  • 1236B — Confidite, ego vici mundum ✓ Jo 16:33.
  • 1237B — Sitio ✓ Jo 19:28.
  • 1237D — divinae Scripturae meditatio: "the meditation of Holy Scripture" (Pattern 3; brief).
  • 1237D — VERS. 10 conversio illius — Ct 7:10 ejus. Pronoun; no marker.
  • 1238A — Convertimini ad me, et ego convertar ad vos (Zach. I) — Zach 1:3 Convertimini ad me, ait Dominus exercituum: et convertar ad vos. No marker.
  • 1238B — Quodcirca: medieval form of quocirca; "Wherefore", no marker.
  • 1238C–D — quorum adoptio filiorum est gloria, et testamentum et legislatio, et obsequium, et promissa; quorum patres ex quibus Christus secundum carnem, qui est Deus benedictus in saecula (Rom. IX) — Rom 9:4–5 quorum adoptio est filiorum, et gloria, et testamentum … quorum patres, et ex quibus est Christus secundum carnem, qui est super omnia Deus benedictus in saecula. Migne's word order makes gloria the predicate ("whose adoption of sons is glory"), and he omits super omnia. Rendered as printed. The omission does not reverse any claim (Christ is still called God blessed for ever); crux, no marker.
  • 1238D — credita sunt eis eloquia Dei (Rom. III) — Rom 3:2 illis; no marker.
  • 1239A, 1239C — Migne prints VERS. 12. twice, for Ct 7:12 and Ct 7:13 (plate p. 626 confirms both). Rendered "VERSE 12." both times as printed.
  • 1239A — VERS. 12 videamus si vinea floruit — Ct 7:12 si floruit vinea; word order only.
  • 1239C — ad futura beatudinis bona: plate p. 626 and scan B p. 624 both print beatudinis (a syllable short of beatitudinis). [sic: beatudinis] with "blessedness" beside it.
  • 1239C — praeceptorum tuorum praecepta ( sic ) reserabo: Migne's own (sic) reproduced verbatim; nothing of ours added (Pattern 12).
  • 1239C — VERS. 12 (= Ct 7:13) Mandragorae dederunt odorem in portis nostris. Omnia poma nova et vetera — the Clementine punctuates in portis nostris omnia poma : nova et vetera; Migne (and the gloss at 1241A) join omnia poma to servavi. Punctuation of the comparand is editorial; no marker.
  • 1239D — minus vigilare imo magis quam dormire: construes awkwardly ("to be less wakeful …, nay, more than to sleep"); rendered as printed.
  • 1240A — Laetare, sterilis … ✓ Gal 4:27 (Is 54:1).
  • 1240D — sponsa dilecto alludit: "plays with the beloved".
  • ✓ spans checked at the plate and against the file: Ct 6:12, Ct 7:1 (VERS.), 7:3, 7:4, 7:6 (spelling only), 7:7, 7:9, 7:11, 7:13 (lemma); Ps 85:16, Ps 29:8, Gn 1:26, Ps 22:5, Gal 6:2, Phlp 4:13, Ct 8:6, Ps 83:8, Lc 12:35, Ps 126:5, Act 4:32, Ps 138:18, Os 4:2, Ps 50:16, Jo 16:33, Jac 4:8, Act 13:46, Gal 4:27 all agree.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Following the peers: dilectus / dilecta = beloved; sponsus / sponsa = bridegroom / bride (all lower case in Migne throughout 0065–0069); bride and bridegroom address each other as "thou" (Pattern 17); praeconium / praeconia = proclamation(s); praelati = prelates; discretio = discretion; Libanus … candidatio = whitening; decachordum = ten-stringed instrument; monilia = necklaces; tornatilis = turned; Scriptura divina / sanctae Scripturae = Holy Scripture / the holy Scriptures; VERSE n.--; Cologne numbers (229–246, and the running 229 in every head) verbatim.
  • Introduced here: quadriga = chariot; chori castrorum = choirs of camps; gressus … calceamenta = steps … shoes; filia principis = daughter of the prince; junctura feminum = joining of the thighs (VERS. 1 juncturae femorum = joints of the thighs); umbilicus = navel, meditullium = middle point; crater tornatilis = turned bowl; acervus tritici vallatus liliis = heap of wheat set about with lilies; hinnuli gemelli capreae = young roes that are twins of the she-goat; turris eburnea = tower of ivory; piscinae in Hesebon = fishpools in Hesebon, cingulum moeroris = girdle of mourning; filia multitudinis = daughter of the multitude; provisores = overseers; nasus = nose; Carmelus = Carmelus (Mount Carmel in the Christological reading, where the Latin says monti Carmelo); comae = locks; purpura regis juncta canalibus = purple of the king joined to the channels; statura = stature; botri = clusters of grapes; mala = apples; guttur = throat; conversio = turning; villae = villages; mala punica = pomegranates; mandragora = mandrake.
  • Heads: \229\ CAPUT VII. = 229 CHAPTER VII.; CAPUT VI. (cont.) = CHAPTER VI. (cont.).

Page 627 is shared with the previous stint and was collated from the CAPUT VIII head (1241B) on; page 636 was collated to 1259C, where 0074 ends. Page 628 is the split page (Book III above, LIBER QUARTUS full width below); the anchors 1243A–C, 1244A–C, [1243], 1243D, 1244D follow the plate's reading order. Second witness raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecur195mign.pdf opened at 600 dpi at pp. 625–628 for letter-level readings. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0070.json.

⚠ Tool note. For a long stretch of this stint the image reader returned no picture ("request limit"), so the first rendering of several chunks was written from the Latin before its pages could be seen. Every page 627–636 was then re-rendered and actually read, and the English was corrected to the plate where the two differed (the four TEI patches below, and three withdrawn markers). Some scan-B crops also never displayed; where a reading below rests on A alone it says so.

Migne's foot notes: none on any of pages 627–636; every foot was read full width. No [cn:] inserted; data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0070.tsv is header-only. No sequence to check.

Lemma count: 307 italic spans, 0 guillemet pairs (one « … » pair at 1247B, balanced), counted paragraph-bounded with no paragraph carrying an odd delimiter count (0070 = 71, 0071 = 57, 0072 = 65, 0073 = 58, 0074 = 56). Agrees with the 307 in 11512-lemmata-0070.txt. The English carries the same number per chunk.

Column notes

  • 1241B VERS. 1 … et deosculer, et jam me nemo despiciat — Vulg. Ct 8:1 et deosculer te. Migne's lemma lacks the second te; rendered "and kiss" with no object. No marker (pronoun only); the gloss re-quotations agree with the lemma.
  • 1241C meum stands alone as a span: fratrem, inquam, meum propter …; rendered "my" as the second span.
  • 1241D finis praecepti ad justitiam omni credenti (Rom. X) — Rom 10:4 Finis enim legis, Christus, ad justitiam omni credenti; finis praecepti is the wording of 1 Tim 1:5. [var:] fired: the proof turns on finis.
  • 1242A sive, ut dictum es., virtute fidei — plate p. 627 prints dictum es., with the stop (scan B not seen here). An abbreviation or clipped est rather than broken type; rendered "as has been said" with no marker (the first draft's [sic:] is withdrawn). Our Latin agrees with the plate.
  • 1242A sanae Scripturae intenta — "intent on sound Scripture": sanae is printed (not sanctae), and is rendered as printed, not promoted to "Holy Scripture".
  • 1242A–B Ite, nuntiate fratribus meis (Matth. XXVIII) — Mt 28:10 (Clementine nuntiare, a known Vulgate variant; Migne's nuntiate is the common reading). Quicunque fecerit … ipse meus frater, soror et mater est (Matth. XII) — Mt 12:50 without qui in caelis est and without et before soror. Abridgment; no marker.
  • 1242C Quam dulcia … super mel et favum ori meo! (Psal. CXVIII.) — Ps 118:103 super mel ori meo; et favum is added (cf. Ps 18:11). [var:] fired.
  • 1242D Sapiens implebitur benedictionibus et viventes illum laudabunt (Eccli. XXXVII) — Sir 37:27 Vir sapiens … et videntes illum laudabunt. Plate p. 627 confirms viventes. [var:] fired ("the living" against "they that see him").
  • 1242D audiunt quod unctio tui Spiritus docet me de omnibus (I Joan. II) and cadentes in terram adoraverunt Dominum, pronuntiantes quod vere Deus in nobis sit (I Cor. XIV) — roman, not spans: allusions (1 Jo 2:27; 1 Cor 14:25 in vobis). No marker.
  • 1243A apprehendens ducam te in domum matris meae — gloss re-quotation of Ct 8:2 turned into a participle. No marker.
  • 1243C ut non sit inexusabilis — plate p. 628 prints inexusabilis (no c after x); our Latin agrees. Non-word for inexcusabilis. The missing letter is not a c/e question. [sic: inexusabilis] with "inexcusable" beside it. ⚠ Scan B's crop at this spot did not display to us.
  • 1243C longitudo tamen dierum in dextera ejus est (ibid.) — Pr 3:16 with est added. No marker.
  • 1243C–1244A quia temporalium memoria me tenebit, non tamen ad sui delectationem et [1244A] unum perpetuum, aeternorum vero desiderium totam me circumdabit — the plate prints exactly this (delectationem et at the foot of 1243C, unum perpetuum at the head of 1244A). et unum perpetuum has no construction; something is probably lost at the column break (cf. ad sui delectationem et usum perpetuum?). Rendered literally, "yet not for delight in them and one perpetual". Crux; no marker.
  • 1244A (Phil. III) is followed by a full stop inside our Latin (compellat. [n: (Phil. III)].): plate prints compellat. (Phil. III).; English keeps both stops.
  • 1244A Verum quia haec agit sponsus — lower-case sponsus on the plate; English "bridegroom".
  • 1244B Sive dilectam futuram nullo modo suscitetis — dilectam futuram rendered "the beloved that is to be". Literal.
  • 1244C Nemo autem venit ad me, nisi Pater qui misit me, traxerit eum (Joan. VI) — Jo 6:44 nemo potest venire. The next sentence argues from venit (Si enim trahitur venit, si non venit non trahitur). [var:] fired.
  • 1244C Qui sponte se offerunt Domino, benedicuntur a Domino (Lev. XXII) — not a Vulgate verse (Lv 22 has nothing like it; cf. Idc 5:9 qui propria voluntate obtulistis vos … benedicite Domino). Attribution error or paraphrase; no marker.
  • 1244D Si enim ad modum cantationis meae, jam aliquantulum frondetis — plate p. 628 prints a damaged 'am anquantulum (broken j, and li worn to something like n). Worn sorts in one copy; our Latin jam aliquantulum is kept. Crux only.
  • 1243D Orabo spiritu, orabo et virtute; psallam spiritu, psallam et virtute (I Cor. XIV) — 1 Cor 14:15 orabo et mente … psallam et mente. Plate confirms virtute twice. [var:] fired.
  • 1243D Gaudium et corona mea vos estis in Domino (Philip. V) — Phlp 4:1 gaudium meum, et corona mea: sic state in Domino; conflated with 1 Thes 2:19–20. Locator as printed (V; Philippians has four chapters). Paraphrase; no marker.
  • 1245A dicat vobis concordia et harmonia carnis et spiritus quos invicem sibi non adversari, est pulchre canere — quos (masculine plural) has no antecedent in agreement; the plate prints it. Rendered "for which not to be opposed to one another is to sing beautifully". Crux only.
  • 1245A canticum novum … laus est in Ecclesia sanctorum (Psal. CXLIX) — Ps 149:1 laus ejus in ecclesia sanctorum. Gloss; no marker.
  • 1245B Exuite … veterem hominem, et induite novum qui secundum Deum creatus est in justitia et sanctitate (Ephes. IV) — Eph 4:22–24 compressed (deponere vos … veterem hominem … induite novum hominem … in justitia, et sanctitate veritatis). No marker.
  • 1245C in canticis labiorum vestrorum, et cytharis (Eccli. XXXIX) — Sir 39:20 in voce labiorum vestrorum, et in canticis labiorum, et citharis: the two phrases fused. No marker; the later re-quotations in canticis labiorum et cytharis follow Migne.
  • 1245D in quibus cum benedicitis quando haec omnia … ascribitis — plate p. 629 and scan B p. 627 at 600 dpi both print cum; our Latin has eum. A c/e case, confirmed on B. The English follows the plate: "in which, when you bless, when you ascribe all these things …" (two temporal clauses, no object for benedicitis). TEI patch below.
  • 1245D Populus hic me labiis honorat, cor autem illorum longe est a me (Isa. XXIX) — the wording of Mt 15:8 (labiis me honorat … eorum), cited to Isaiah. No marker.
  • 1246A ut in auribus Domini Sabaoth dulci modulatione carnis et Spiritus concordiam deprimat — deprimat "presses down", printed; the sense wants something like exprimat ("express"). Rendered literally. Crux only.
  • 1246B VERS. 5 … ibi corruvta est mater tua — plate p. 629 and scan B p. 627 at 600 dpi both print corruvta (a turned or wrong sort for p); our Latin has corrupta. The English renders "was corrupted" and carries no marker for now, because a [sic:] must wrap type that is in our Latin. When the patch lands, the English may take [sic: corruvta] at this spot. The gloss re-quotations at 1248C and 1249A print corrupta.
  • 1246D Et derelinquetur … filia Sion sicut umbraculum in vinea … (Baruch VI) — Is 1:8, cited to Baruch. Wording near the Vulgate (sicut for ut). No marker; the locator stands as printed.
  • 1246D Relinquetur … vobis domus vestra deserta (Matth. XXIII) — Mt 23:38 without Ecce. No marker.
  • 1247A ubi dilectus filias Jerusalem adjurat, ne suscitent neque evigilare faciant dilectam donec ipsa velit — Ct 8:4 turned into indirect speech. No marker.
  • 1247A Quid, inquam, omnipotens Deus … in hac ejus dormitione attendit. — the question closes with a full stop on the plate; the English keeps the stop.
  • 1247B si circa mundi strepitum versatur animus omnipotens Pater … — no stop between animus and omnipotens; the English keeps it run-on.
  • 1247B Quae ista quae ascendit de deserto? — the re-quotation lacks est; rendered "Who she that goeth up". Guillemets « … » around the Ps 83:6–7 quotation are Migne's.
  • 1247C–D Hic est Filius meus dilectus, in quo mihi complacui (Matth. [1247D] III) — locator in roman parentheses inside the running text, not an [n:]; kept.
  • 1247D delicias vero istas quibus dilectus affluens ascendit — masculine dilectus where the bride (dilecta) is meant; English "the beloved" has no gender exponent. Crux only (Pattern 9 class).
  • 1248D Si anidmavertis quid in primo parente fueris — plate p. 630 and scan B p. 628 both print anidmavertis (letters transposed); our Latin has animadvertis. English renders the sense, "If thou considerest"; no marker until the patch lands, when [sic: anidmavertis] may be fired. TEI patch below.
  • 1248D ut disceret se aptare ad di ciplinas et obedientiae studium — plate p. 630 and scan B p. 628 both print ad di ciplinas (the s dropped or broken, with a gap left); our Latin has ad disciplinae. The plate's word is a plural accusative: "to fit himself to disciplines and to the pursuit of obedience". The English follows the plate. TEI patch below (the dropped s is a spacing defect, not a different word).
  • 1249A De omni ligno paradisi comede, de ligno autem scientiae boni ac mali ne comedas (Gen. II) — Gn 2:16–17 Ex omni … boni et mali. No marker.
  • 1249A vidit enim et concapivit — plate p. 631 (scan A) appears to print concapivit; our Latin has concupivit. a/u is an ink-fill risk like c/e, and our B crop here did not display, so this is a candidate only: no patch filed, no marker; English "desired".
  • 1249A Hoc ergo peccatum postquam consummatum fuit, mortem generavit (Jac. I) — Jac 1:15 paraphrased in the narrative; roman. No marker.
  • 1249B sed tui in ipsis parentibus tuis magis ad vitia declinasti — plate p. 631 prints tui, which has no grammatical slot (genitive "of thee" as subject of declinasti); the sense wants tu. [sic: tui] with "thou" beside it (7a⁗: the clause's subject).
  • 1249C VERS. 6 — agrees with Ct 8:6.
  • 1249C Omni custodia custodi cor tuum, quoniam ex ipso vita procedit (Prov. IV) — Pr 4:23 serva … quia. Synonyms; the gloss turns on custodia, which both have. No marker.
  • 1249C In omnibus operibus memorare novissima tua … (Eccle. VII) — Sir 7:40 without tuis; cited to Ecclesiastes. No marker.
  • 1249D malus homo, de malo thesauro cordis sui profert malum (Luc. VI) — Lc 6:45 without cordis sui in the second member; Migne adds it. No marker.
  • 1250A Custodi me, Domine, ut pupillam oculi — Ps 16:8 with Domine added. in vanum laborant (Ps 126:1 laboraverunt). No marker.
  • 1250B ut sicut portavit imaginem terreni portet et imaginem coelestis (I Cor. XV) — 1 Cor 15:49 turned into the third person. No marker.
  • 1250C Exemplum dedi vobis, ut quemadmodum ego feci, ita et vos faciatis (Joan. III) — Jo 13:15 (without enim, vobis); locator as printed (III for XIII). No marker.
  • 1250D Phlp 4:8–9 — agrees.
  • 1251A Fides per dilectionem operatur (Gal. V) — Gal 5:6 per caritatem. Synonym; the argument is about charity either way. No marker.
  • 1251B ut omnis qui credit in ipsum non pereat — Jo 3:16 in eum. No marker.
  • 1251D ex desiderio supernae [felicitatis] importat — the square brackets are Migne's (plate p. 632); kept untranslated inside the brackets.
  • 1252A Quis nos separabit a charitate Christi … (Rom. VII) — Rom 8:35–39 printed with persecutio moved forward, vita before mors, potestates and dominationes added (from Col 1:16 / Eph 1:21), instantia, futura, fortitudo, altitudo, profundum omitted, and a charitate quae est in Christo for a caritate Dei, quae est. Plate p. 632 confirms all of this, and the locator VII (for VIII). [var:] fired. On A the a before charitate is blacked out (the known one-letter defect); our Latin has it.
  • 1252B VERS. 8 Soror nostra parvula est … in die quo — Ct 8:8 parva … quando. parvula "a little one" (the gloss's own word); quo "in which". Same sense; no marker. The gloss at 1252D–1253A re-quotes with quando.
  • 1252D evacuare debet quae sunt parvuli (I Cor. XIII) — 1 Cor 13:11 adapted. No marker.
  • 1253A Surge, propera, amica mea, ostende mihi faciem tuam, sonet vox tua in auribus meis (Cant. II) — Ct 2:10 and 2:14 joined. No marker.
  • 1253A VERS. 9 Si murus est, aedificemur super eum propugnacula argentea — Vulg. Ct 8:9 aedificemus. Plate p. 633 confirms aedificemur (passive: "let us be built upon it bulwarks"); the gloss at 1253C twice reads aedificemus. Rendered literally. [var:] fired.
  • 1253A–B se quasi murum pro domo Israel … opponunt (Ezech. XIII) — Ez 13:5 adapted. No marker.
  • 1253C eloquia Domini argentum igne examinatum (Psal. XI) — Ps 11:7 without the repetition. No marker.
  • 1253D Conpingamus — printed with n; an attested spelling. No marker.
  • 1254A Unde et ipsa pictura tabulis cedrinis dieitur cumponenda — our Latin dieitur; scan A cannot separate c from e, and our scan-B crop did not display. So there is no evidence for the plate's letter. The first draft's [sic: dieitur] is withdrawn; rendered "is said", no marker. cumponenda (for componenda) is also in our Latin, and the plate prints com-|ponenda across the line break. TEI patch candidate below (line-break class).
  • 1254A quia Christi bonus odor sunt in omni loco (II Cor. II) — 2 Cor 2:14–15 adapted; plate sets Christi bonus odor and in omni loco in italic with roman sunt between them, and the English follows that span structure.
  • 1254B Graecis et barbaris … debitor sum, ita, quod in me, promptum est et vobis qui Romae estis evangelizare (Rom. I) — Rom 1:14–15 (ac barbaris). No marker.
  • 1254C \262\ VERS. 10 — the Cologne number sits before the verse label on the plate; kept.
  • 1254C necesse … ei intimam adipisci securitatem — ei ("for him") is printed; the sense wants et ("and to obtain"). Rendered "to obtain inmost security for him". Crux only.
  • 1255A ut enim quidam dicit. Sapientia est stultitia caruisse — full stop after dicit printed (plate p. 634); English keeps it. The saying is Horace, Ep. I.1.41–42 (sapientia prima / stultitia caruisse), unnamed.
  • 1255A Habete sal in vobis, et pacem habete inter vos (Marc. IX) — Mc 9:49 with the phrases in Migne's order. No marker.
  • 1255C ut evangelizem Jerusalem (Isa. XL.) — in roman, not a span; paraphrase of Is 40:9. No stop follows the note on the plate; the English has none.
  • 1255C VERS. 11 — agrees with Ct 8:11.

II) — Eph 2:14–17 with in decretis, conderet, faciens pacem moved, reconciliaret utrosque, and at the close his qui longe for vobis, qui longe fuistis. [var:] fired on the close (the change of person).
  • 1256A tactus dolore cordis intrinsecus … Poenitet me fecisse hominem (Gen. VI) — Gn 6:6–7 (fecisse eos). No marker.
  • 1256B Gloria in excelsis Deo, et in terra pax hominibus bonae voluntatis (Luc. II) — Lc 2:14 Clementine in altissimis; in excelsis is the liturgical form. Same sense; no marker.
  • 1256C Salomon quippe interpretatur pacificus — etymology; pacificus rendered "peaceable" throughout, the English word the gloss depends on.
  • 1257B Quod factum est in ipso vita erat (Joan. I) — Jo 1:3–4 read across the verse break (the old punctuation). No marker.
  • 1257C Erat eis cor unum et anima una (Act. IV) — Act 4:32 Multitudinis autem credentium erat cor unum. No marker.
  • 1257D in qua in idipsum dormirent et requiescerent (Psal. IV) — Ps 4:9 in the third person; roman. No marker.
  • 1257D non est ibi transmutatio, nec vicissitudinis obrumbratio (Jac. I) — Jac 1:17 obumbratio. Plate p. 635 prints obrumbratio; our Latin agrees. Non-word; [sic: obrumbratio] inside the italic lemma (no nested italics), with "shadow" beside it.
  • 1257D universalem Ecclesiam ex Judaeis et pentibus congregatam — our Latin pentibus; plate p. 635 prints gentibus with a worn g. The error is in our Latin, not Migne's. English "Gentiles", no marker (the first draft's [sic:] is withdrawn). TEI patch below.
  • 1258B Vos ponite nomen meum super filios Israel, ego Dominus benedicam eos (Num. VI) — Nm 6:27 Invocabuntque nomen meum … et ego benedicam eis. The gloss that follows (Vos per ministerium … meum erit) builds on the second person Migne prints. [var:] fired. The plate has a raised point, not a colon, after the locator; our Latin has a colon, and the English keeps the colon.
  • 1258C–D Attendite vobis … (Act. XX) agrees; Obedite … (Hebr. XIII) — Hbr 13:17 with reddituri moved. No marker.
  • 1258D Vos ergo, hoc est praelatos, … et istos … — Vos where the parallel wants illos; printed. Rendered "you, that is, the prelates". Crux only.
  • 1258D–1259B Speculatorem dedi te domui Israel; quae vides, annuntiabis eis ex me (Ezech. III) — Ez 3:17 abridged, with quae vides in place of et audies de ore meo verbum, and the gloss expounds quae vides. A substitution rather than a pure abridgment, but the gloss is built on it and the sense of the charge is unchanged. No marker; logged. Ez 3:18–19 Si dicente me … animam tuam liberasti (ibid) — paraphrased (et non annuntiaveris, ipse quidem for ipse impius, ut ab iniquitate sua convertatur et ille te non audierit for et ille non fuerit conversus). No marker. anuntiaveris (single n) printed; an attested spelling.
  • 1259A oculo mentuali — plate p. 636 prints mentuali; our Latin agrees. A rare formation for mentali; rendered "the mental eye". Crux only.
  • 1259C Ideo … te non audiunt quia domus exasperans est (Ezech. III) — Ez 3:7/3:9 wording; agrees. domus exasperatrix (Ez 2:8); dura cervice et incircumciso corde … Spiritui sancto resistant (Act. VII) — Act 7:51 adapted. Verba autem Dei spiritus et vita sunt (Joan. VI) — Jo 6:64 adapted. Ps 118:66 alluded to in roman. No markers.

Standing renderings (for merge-time reconciliation)

  • Following the peers: dilectus / dilecta = the beloved; filiae Jerusalem = daughters of Jerusalem ("O" only where Migne prints o); sorores dilectissimae / dilectae = most beloved / beloved sisters, addressed as "you" (plural); the bride and the soul are "thou" by number (Pattern 17); Scriptura sacra / sancta = Holy Scripture (Pattern 3), but sana Scriptura = "sound Scripture"; sponsus lower-case = bridegroom (1244A, 1249D, 1250D, 1255B sponsa = bride); VERSE n.--; Cologne numbers 247–267 verbatim.
  • Introduced here: vinum conditum = spiced wine; mustum malorum granatorum = new wine of pomegranates; laeva / dextera = left hand / right hand; canticum novum = a new canticle; Canticum canticorum = the Canticle of Canticles; cythara = harp; tetrachordum = tetrachord; deliciis affluens = flowing with delights; innixa super dilectum = leaning upon the beloved; signaculum = seal; aemulatio = emulation; propugnacula argentea = bulwarks of silver; tabulae cedrinae = boards of cedar; pacificus = the peaceable one; custodes = keepers (Ct 8:11) and speculatores = watchmen (Ezekiel).
  • Heads: \247\ CAPUT VIII. = 247 CHAPTER VIII.; LIBER QUARTUS. = BOOK FOUR. (after the peers' BOOK ONE / TWO / THREE).

Columns 1259C–1278A: the end of Book IV (Ct 8:11–14) and the author's closing Paraenesis to the sisters. Plate: raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecursu0195mign.pdf, pages 636–645, each opened at 300 dpi with the printed corner columns read first (636 = 1259/1260 … 645 = 1277/1278). Text collated and the foot read full width in the same visit. Page 636 is shared with the previous stint and was collated from Praecepit autem sermo divinus (1259C). Page 645 was collated to the end of the text at 1278A (per omnia saecula saeculorum. Amen.), which sits above a rule. The rest of p. 645 is Migne's INDEX RERUM, VERBORUM ET SENTENTIARUM MEMORABILIUM AD WOLBERONIS COMMENTARIUM IN CANTICA, which is out of scope. Page 638 was also read at 600 dpi. Second witness raw/scans/pl195/patrologiaecur195mign.pdf was opened at 600 dpi at pp. 635, 636, 639 and 641 (corners read). Every c/e or letter-level claim below was settled there. Record: data/briefs/11512-PLATE-READS-0075.json.

Migne's foot notes

  • One note in the whole range: p. 638, (4) Locus obscurus., set under col. 1263. It is keyed in the text at 1263B: …et conferat perfecte aeternam impassibilitatem, immutabilitatem, immortalitatem (4). The key stands before the full stop, at the end of the paragraph (the last sentence of chunk 0075). It was placed in the Latin as [cn: 4 | Locus obscurus.], with nothing in the English. TSV: data/briefs/11512-NOTES-0075.tsv.
  • The number was read, not inferred. On both copies the digit is ink-filled, and in this face 1 and 4 are close (the OCR layer's unreliability here is known). I read it at 600 dpi on scan A (p. 638: the key in the text and the note at the foot) and on scan B (p. 636: the note at the foot). I then compared it pixel-enlarged against 141 in the index on p. 645, the same printing and the same small face. There the 4 has a crossbar projecting to the left at lower mid-height and a closed counter; the 1s have only a top flag and a foot. The foot digit on A shows the left-projecting bar and the counter hole. The text key shows the diagonal into the stem and the counter. Printed: (4), on both key and note. Scan B's glyph is the same ink-filled shape and does not settle it independently.
  • Numbering and gaps: p. 638 carries only (4). There is no (1), (2) or (3) anywhere on the page, or anywhere else in pp. 636–645. Every other page in the range (636, 637, 639–645) has no foot note, and each foot was read full width. So the sequence has a gap of (1)–(3) on this page, like p. 613's lone (3). This has to be documented page by page, as the brief asks; I did not guess at it.
  • Crux (Migne's own Locus obscurus). The note is keyed to the end of the sentence at 1263B: Haec ergo aliquo modo esse potest mensura sive dimensio, quam potest habere fructus iste participans, non secundum se ut dictum est, sed secundum participans, ut quandoque a participante sicuti est adeptus, omnem deinceps adimat dimensionis in crescendo passibilitatem sive mutabilitatem, et conferat perfecte aeternam impassibilitatem, immutabilitatem, immortalitatem. The obscurity is plausibly the subject. fructus iste participans makes the fruit (God) the participans, whereas the whole argument from 1260D calls God the participabile and the soul the participans. The ut-clause then has the fruit, "attained by the participant", take away passibility, but from whom it does not say. Rendered literally ("which this fruit, participating, can have"). No marker, since Migne's note is the witness and stands on the Latin side (Pattern 19).

Lemma count

223 italic spans, 0 guillemet spans, counted paragraph-bounded: 0075 = 34, 0076 = 45, 0077 = 47, 0078 = 29, 0079 = 57, 0080 = 11. No paragraph has an odd delimiter count. The count includes the Cologne numbers 268–286 and the 282 in the heads. It agrees with the 223 in 11512-lemmata-0075.txt. The English carries the same number of spans in every paragraph (with our markers stripped). To hold that, 0077's "a thousand who are peaceable", "keepers of the fruit" and "these friends hearken" were set to match Migne's italic boundaries.

Column notes

  • 1259C–D — Deut 21:19 ad seniores civitatis et ad portam judicii (Vulg. civitatis illius): abridgment, no marker.
  • 1260A — 1 Jn 2:19 exierunt (Vulg. prodierunt): same sense, no marker.
  • 1260B — traditionis rendered "delivering", after the peers' "he let it out … delivering" at Ct 8:11 (0073–0074).
  • 1260C — Job 4:18: Migne italicizes et in angelis suis … reperit and sets iniquitatem roman (Vulg. pravitatem). The plate marks the word as the author's, so no marker. Rendered "in his angels also he found iniquity".
  • 1260D — nam suscipiens et participans est, quando nec suscipit, nec participat is hard. Rendered literally ("the receiving and participating thing is such when it neither receives nor participates").
  • 1260D–1261A — Migne prints a full stop, attendimus. procul dubio, then continues in lower case. Confirmed on A p. 636 and = TEI. Carried as printed: "…beauty. [1261A] beyond doubt…" (Pattern 8).
  • 1261B — efficiunt: on A p. 637 the first letter looks like an open o (officiunt). On B p. 635 it is an e with its eye. No patch, TEI stands.
  • 1261C — rationalis creatura, altera spiritualis … altera corporalis ut elementa calls the elements a rational creature. This is printed, and rendered as printed.
  • 1261C — Jn 4:24 Deus enim spiritus est (Vulg. Spiritus est Deus): order only.
  • 1261D–1262A — Eph 3:17–18, [var:]. Migne prints longitudo, latitudo, altitudo et profundum where the Vulgate has latitudo, et longitudo, et sublimitas, et profundum. The exposition that follows (1262A–D) runs on altitudo and puts length first, so the divergence carries the argument. The reference (Ephes. II) is Migne's for Eph 3. It is verbatim in [n:], and this crux records the mismatch only.
  • 1261D — Rom 12:3 unicuique … mensuram fidei: fragments, as quoted.
  • 1262C — Lk 23:34 Pater, ignosce illis, quia nesciunt (Vulg. dimitte illis: non enim sciunt): same sense, no marker.
  • 1262C — Longa debet esse. quia: the mark after esse is a baseline point on A p. 637 and B p. 635. It could be a full stop or a comma with a broken tail, and cannot be decided. TEI's comma is kept. Candidate only (see below).
  • 1262D — Mt 20:16 multi vocantur, pauci cliguntur: [sic: cliguntur] "are chosen".
  • 1262D — Col 2:9 in Christo habitat (Vulg. in ipso inhabitat): the referent is made explicit, same sense.
  • 1263B — quasi circulum et circumferendo tangis et concludis: the second-person singular is generic, so "thou" (Pattern 17). Rendered "thou, as with a circle, both touchest by carrying round and enclosest".
  • 1263B — Migne's (4) Locus obscurus (above).
  • 1263C — Vir quippe a virtute dicitur: the etymology does not survive into English ("man is so called from virtue"). No bracketed Latin was added, to keep italic parity.
  • 1263D — Ps 92:1 indutus … fortitudine et praecinctus virtute: adapted to the sentence, no marker.
  • 1263D — Gen 4:7, [var:]. Si recte offeras, recte autem non dividas, peccasti is the Old Latin/LXX form (Ambrose, Augustine), and the Vulgate verse is entirely different. The whole exposition (Recte quidem obtulit … sed non recte divisit) is built on the printed form. Confirmed on A p. 638.
  • 1264A — quod erat melius Deo recte negavit: "rightly denied" as printed (sense wants non recte or similar). Real word, no marker, crux only.
  • 1264A — Ps 111:1, [var:]: cupit nimis (Vulg. volet nimis).
  • 1264B — Ps 111:2–3 conflated, with Migne's (ibid) inside the italic run. Carried verbatim.
  • 1264C — de corde puro et conscientia bona, et fide non ficta is 1 Tim 1:5, but Migne cites (II Tim. II). It is verbatim in [n:], and the mismatch is recorded here only.
  • 1265A — Ecl 7:30 ipse vero implicuit se multis quaestionibus (Vulg. et ipse se infinitis miscuerit quaestionibus): same sense, no marker.
  • 1265B — Mt 22:17–21: abridged, Cujus est imago et superscriptio without haec. No marker.
  • 1265D — Sive millena unitas … unum Deum et unum hominem habeat et colat: "one God and one man" as printed.
  • 1266A — VERSE 12, [var:]. The lemma prints custodiunt fructum ejus, singular (Vulg. fructus). The second lemma at 1266B and the lemma at 1267C both print fructus. Confirmed on A p. 639.
  • 1266A — Lev 26:9 aspiciam (Vulg. Respiciam): "look upon" vs "regard", not material, so no marker.
  • 1266B — Is 5:4 uvam, fecit autem labruscas (Vulg. uvas, et fecit): number and connective only.
  • 1266B — coro: rendered "core" (Douay's measure).
  • 1266C — bis duo quatuor, et tertia novem: "and the third, nine" as printed (plate p. 639 = TEI). The sense wants ter tria ("three times three"). A [cj:] was drafted and withdrawn, because the page does not assert anything the author argues against (Pattern 18a bar). Crux only.
  • 1266C / 1267D — binarius centenus = "the hundredfold two" and unitas millena = "the thousandfold unity", used throughout. abacistae = "abacists".
  • 1267A — Igitur mille tui pacifici argentei: the author construes pacifici with argentei, so rendered "a thousand are thy peaceable pieces of silver".
  • 1267B — Jas 2:20/26: otiosa is set roman inside the quotation (the author's insertion), as printed.
  • 1267D — VERS. 13. is printed with no dash (= TEI). English "VERSE 13." to match.
  • 1268A — tanquam erudit jam, et … ad plenum edoctae proclamat: erudit is a real word ("he instructs"). The sense wants eruditae (dative, "to her now instructed"), parallel to edoctae. Rendered as printed ("as though he now instructs, and proclaims to her fully taught"). A [cj:] was drafted and withdrawn as below the bar. Plate p. 640 = TEI.
  • 1268A — Ps 1:2 meditatur (Vulg. meditabitur): tense only.
  • 1268C — Ps 77:6–7, [var:]: audiant et narrent ea filiis suis where the Vulgate has et narrabunt filiis suis. audiant is not in the Vulgate verse, and it is the word the gloss turns on (quare volo audire).
  • 1268D — vinear cum judicor (Ps 50:6 vincas cum judicaris) is set roman and put into the Bridegroom's first person. It is an adaptation, not a quotation, so no marker.
  • 1269A — Lk 15:10 Gaudium est angelis Dei (Vulg. gaudium erit coram angelis Dei): not material.
  • 1269A — Heb 1:14, [var:]: administratorii spiritus sunt in omnem terram where the Vulgate has in ministerium missi. The mission "into all the earth" is the printed sense.
  • 1269A — Dan 12:1 abridged (consurget Michael qui stat), and Dan 10:21 as printed.
  • 1269B — διατέσσαρον is kept in Greek as printed.
  • 1269B — Ps 51:10–11, [var:]: sicut oliva fructificavi in domo Domini, speravi in misericordia Dei mei, et exspectabo nomen tuum ante conspectum sanctorum tuorum conflates vv. 10–11 and turns fructifera into a verb.
  • 1269C — 1 Cor 9:27 servituti redigo, subjicio (roman insertion), dum aliis praedicavero: small variants, no marker.
  • 1269D — ad eamdem gloriam quam isti … adepti sunt, et illi … se adepturos, non dubitant: the construction is loose and is rendered with its two subjects as printed.
  • 1269D — The VERSE 14 label is printed with broken sorts (Vene. 14.) on A p. 641 = TEI VERS. 14.--. It is a structural label, normalized (Pattern 9).
  • 1270A–1270B — fuge enim subtilium rerum in discussione sacramentorum, dilecti hujus fuga est: fuge (imperative) is confirmed on B p. 639, where the noun fugae might be expected. Rendered with our quotation marks as the cried word ("for "flee", in the discussion of the subtle matters of the sacraments, is the flight of this beloved"). Real word, no marker.
  • 1270C — tactu nervi claudicamur: first person plural as printed.
  • 1271A — Ps 30:20 is recast in the third person (dulcedinis ejus, quam abscondit timentibus se): adaptation, no marker.
  • 1271B — Mt 8:8: Migne sets digna roman (feminine, the soul speaking) inside the italic quotation. It is the author's change and is marked by the plate itself.
  • 1271C–1272A — 2 Cor 2:15, [var:]: Deo in omni loco (from 2:14) where 2:15 has Deo in iis qui salvi fiunt.
  • 1272A — 2 Cor 12:4, audierat, (II Cor. XII),: the comma before the note is printed.
  • 1272A–B — Rom 11:33–36: Migne sets honor et roman in ipsi honor et gloria, the author's addition. Otherwise = Vulgate.
  • 1272C — Book IV ends Amen.
  • [1271] / [1271D] / [1272D] / [1273A] — This is the split page 642, and the order is correct (brief).
  • Head — \282\ EPILOGI LOCO AUCTORIS PARAENESIS Ad sacras virgines quibus haec commentaria scripsit et inscripsit. is rendered "282 IN PLACE OF AN EPILOGUE, THE AUTHOR'S PARAENESIS To the sacred virgins for whom he wrote and to whom he inscribed these commentaries.", mirroring the Latin's one-line form. Paraenesis is kept as the term the plate prints.
  • 1271D — dilectae sorores = "beloved sisters", addressed as "you" (plural vos, Pattern 17). The author's own convincor is first person singular.
  • 1273C — Mt 4:17 appropinquabit (Vulg. appropinquavit) is confirmed on A p. 643. Rendered "will draw near" as printed, with no marker, following the peers' decision at 1023B/1232B (tense only).
  • 1273C — Phil 2:8: Deo is set roman inside the quotation (the author's addition).
  • 1273C–D — Ps 140:4 is cited by Migne as (Psal. CXLVI). It is verbatim in [n:], and the mismatch is recorded here only. cor nostrum for cor meum is adapted to the exhortation, so no marker.
  • 1273D–1274A — Musical vocabulary: modus = mode, finalis/finales = final(s), depositio / deponere vocem = cadence / make its cadence. in quatuor ipsorum modorum finalis (for finales) is printed = TEI, and there is no English exponent.
  • 1274A — E contra elegiaca haec est tristis et misera depositio est: the double est is printed and rendered literally ("the elegiac, this is a sad and wretched cadence, is that which…").
  • 1274A — fit rationabile vocum sive tonorum, conjunctione (for rationabili): gender/case only, no exponent.
  • 1274A — in suavem et immitem: split type for insuavem, on A p. 643 and B p. 641. [sic: in suavem] with "unpleasant" beside it.
  • 1274C — ex divina et humana Scriptura = "divine and human Scripture". It is not the canon, so Pattern 3 does not apply.
  • 1274C — sono testitudinis: [sic:] "the lyre" (testudo). Orpheus ca..tu suo is a broken n on both copies (= TEI cantu).
  • 1274B — syrenae at 1274B and sirenarum at 1274D are printed both ways, and both are rendered "sirens" (attested spelling).
  • 1275A — divinus ille … percunctaturus est is Balaam, rendered "that diviner … was about to inquire".
  • 1275A — Num 24:5–6: Migne italicizes Quam and sets dilecta, inquit, roman for the Vulgate's pulchra. The plate marks the substitution as the author's (it ties back to dilecta Domini tabernacula), and the verse is re-quoted with Quam pulchra at 1275B. So no marker. quasi cedri propter aquas multas (Vulg. prope aquas, with multas from v. 7) is glossed by the author himself at 1276A (id est juxta aquas). No marker.
  • 1275B — Is 41:19–20 in solitudine (Vulg. in solitudinem): case only.
  • 1275C — contemplationi virtute ad alta semper surgentes is rendered "rising ever on high by virtue for contemplation", as printed.
  • 1275C — sunt buxus … viventes: a singular noun with plural participles. Rendered "the box, who … live".
  • 1275D — Is 58:11 = Vulgate. Ct 4:12 + 4:15 are conflated (Hortus conclusus, fons signatus, fons hortorum…): the verses are joined, not altered.
  • 1276A — Ps 103:16, [var:]: Saturabuntur … campi et cedri Libani where the Vulgate has ligna campi.
  • 1276A — Ps 103:17 is paraphrased (passeres nidificant).
  • 1276A — Num 24:7 semen ejus (Vulg. semen illius): not material.
  • 1276D — diversae fiunt beatitudines quasi octo modorum species; videlicet humilitas, mansuetudo, luctus, sive tria secundum Dominum, pax…: the list with its sive tria secundum Dominum is obscure and rendered as printed ("or three according to the Lord").
  • 1277A / 1278A — Ps 118:54 is adapted to the sisters (vobis … vestrae) and set roman. The comma before the note, vestrae, (Psal. CXVIII),, is printed. The page carries only bands A of 1277 and 1278, and the text ends at 1278A.
  • 1278A — aeterna liter: split type on A p. 645, [sic:] "eternally".
  • 1278A — 1 Cor 2:9 / Is 64:4 as printed (nec oculus vidit, Vulg. quod oculus non vidit): the same wording as at 1261A and 1269D, no marker.

Scripture: every ⚠ span not covered above

  • These are fragments or re-quoted lemmata, with no divergence from the verse they quote: In charitate (1261D); ad imaginem et similitudinem Dei/nostram (1264C–D, 1265C); Si licet censum… / Cujus est imago… / Caesaris: Reddite (1265B); mille affert argenteos (1265D); in mille argenteis / in quindecim argenteis… / nuptias hujus… / Fodi eam mihi… (1266B–D, Os 3:2 abridged); fructus custodes (1267C); habitas, fac me audire… / vocem tuam audire (1268B–D); Canticum canticorum (1269C, 1277A); Fuge, fuge / hinnulo cervorum / dilecte mi, fuge (1271B–1272B); corde, ore (1271D); novum canticum (1273B–C); factus est obediens / Patri usque ad mortem (1273C); Quam pulchra tabernacula… / pulchra tabernacula ut… / tabernacula pulchra… (1275B–1276A); aqua ejus / veri Jacob / mentis Dominum videntis / Pater excelsus (1276A–B, author's glosses and etymologies); Et semen ejus… (1276B).
  • These carry divergences that were checked and found not material, so no marker: Ps 18:9 (1264C); 1 Tim 1:5 (1264C); Gen 1:26 (1264C); Ps 48:13 similis fiat (1265B); Eph 2:14 (1267A); Heb 11:6 (1267B); 1 Cor 13:9–10 (1270A); Phil 3:20 conversatio ejus (1270D); Ps 83:8 (1272B–C); Ps 149:1 (1273A); Mt 11:29 (1273C); Jn 7:38 Qui in me credit (1276C); Jn 4:13–14 (1276C); Gen 17:5 allusion (1276B).

TEI patches (plate vs our Latin)

  • 1276A · nidificant [n: (Psal. CIII)], quorum abundantia → the plate prints nidificant (Psal. CIII, quorum abundantia, with no closing parenthesis. This is markup-level only: the patch would sit inside the [n:]. Low priority; recorded so the Latin can converge if the orchestrator wishes · p. 644
  • ⚑ Candidate, NOT ready: 1262C · Longa debet esse, quia → the plate shows a baseline point after esse on both A p. 637 and B p. 635, and it cannot be decided between a full stop and a broken comma. It needs a third witness before patching.

No other difference between the plate and our Latin was found in cols 1259C–1278A. All four [sic:] forms are Migne's type and are = TEI.

Du bon, à bon marché — carried to its limit: the good, free.
